# Proposal 0028: Make signature verification verify

- Issue: (to be filed)
- Status: Applied to spec (2026-09-23). Verified 2026-09-23 after 12 adversarial review rounds (48 findings fixed). OQ-11 to OQ-14 resolved by the owner with the stated defaults.
- Date: 2026-09-15

This document stages the proposed spec, code, test, and documentation changes. It does not modify any spec, code, or doc file. Apply the changes in the staged sections after sign-off.

## Summary

**What changes.**

- `pkg/sign` splits the §4.7.9 policy into two axes. A signature the response carries is verified under any policy other than `never`, with no sensitivity consulted. Above `never`, `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` governs whether a *missing* signature aborts the load; `never` checks nothing, including a signature the response carries. The policy takes `never` or `always`, and `medium-and-above` is deleted, because after the split its only behavior is to waive the missing-signature requirement on a label the registry supplies (CODE-13). `sign.EnforceVerification` today returns `nil` before touching the provider whenever `needsVerification` is false, so a response declaring `sensitivity: low` skips the cryptographic path entirely.
- `pkg/sign` makes `Noop.Verify` refuse every signature. `Sign` is unchanged. The value `Sign` produces is `"noop:" + contentHash`, and `content_hash` is served in the clear on every `load_artifact` response, so accepting it lets any party mint a passing envelope for any artifact.
- `cmd/podium-mcp` reorders `deliverLoadArtifact` so that the §6.6 step-2 verification runs before the §8.2 read event and the §4.4.1 sandbox and runtime gates, and maps a missing signature onto `materialize.signature_missing`, a §6.10 cell no served error currently carries (CODE-3). On this proposal's landing that verification is the §4.7.9 policy over the served `signature` and `content_hash`, then the content-hash recomputation. The §5.0 `resources/read` mirror runs the same verification before it returns an artifact's text, through one method `deliverLoadArtifact` also calls (CODE-12). Proposal 0027, which lands after this proposal, replaces what that method verifies with its delivery record. The constraint this proposal states holds for either digest: the signature the policy governs is verified before any gate reads manifest content, and every body the consumer returns, caches, or writes is covered by the digest that signature attests, with the exceptions the edge-case table records for the interval before 0027 lands.
- The registry signs at ingest by default. `PODIUM_SIGN` resolves to `registry-key` when unset and accepts `none` to turn signing off. The keypair is already generated on first run under the process's home, so a standalone deployment whose home persists needs no operator action and an unenforcing consumer ignores the envelope. A registry with signing on and no `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` refuses to start unless its store is the SQLite store in the directory the default key resolves to, so a Postgres deployment, or a SQLite file moved off that directory, sets `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` on the store's persistent storage or sets `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, and a key never sits on storage with a different fate from the store it signs (CODE-4). The Helm chart cannot generate a key, because its root filesystem is read-only and every replica has to sign under one key, so it mounts an operator-supplied Secret at `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` and refuses to render without one unless signing is set to `none`. The documented start that generates that key runs under `env -i` with a scratch `HOME`, because the first start of this release runs the one-shot §13.4 rewrite on whatever store it opens and would otherwise sign the operator's own standalone store under the cluster key (CODE-8). The `docker-compose.yml` evaluation stack pins `PODIUM_SIGN: "none"`, because it has no key management and would otherwise mint a fresh key on every recreate (CODE-11).
- The registry admits each stored row before it serves the row's content, which preserves the protection a consumer under `always` holding the registry's key has today and which proposal 0027's serve-time countersignature would otherwise remove. What admission guarantees, and its limits, are stated once, in SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph, and OQ-13 and OQ-14 carry the limits. CODE-14's column table states what admission does with each stored field. Its resource-source rule serves a resource the row holds inline from the admitted row, so on a deployment with an object store an `artifacts:batchLoad` item carries its small resources in `inline` rather than `presigned_url` (Decisions).
- The consumer defaults become `registry-managed` for `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER` and `always` for `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES`, and `podium-mcp` resolves its verification material once in `loadConfig` and refuses to start when a policy above `never` resolves none. `defaults.verify_signatures` resolves across the three §7.5.2 `sync.yaml` scopes, including `sync.local.yaml` and the walk-up workspace discovery, as every other key in that block does, and `podium config show` reports it. A `never` that a `sync.yaml` file supplied is announced with one startup line naming the file. The standalone bootstrap stops writing `defaults.verify_signatures: never` and writes no key material in its place. `pkg/sign` gains one resolver for the registry-managed key file, `KeyFilePath`, which `internal/serverboot`'s existing `registrySigningKeyPath` becomes a delegate of, plus the readers for that file's `public:` and `private:` lines. `podium verify` resolves the public half and `podium sign` the private half through them, each on the half its command needs. A set `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is authoritative, and a malformed one refuses the start rather than falling through to the key file. A consumer with no `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` resolves the same file the registry writes, so on a standalone machine the producer and the consumer cannot resolve different keys, and no configuration file carries key material.
- §4.7.9 is rewritten. It states what a signature attests, states that the identity and log properties keyless verification does not check are not checked, drops the claim that the registry key is rotated quarterly, and states that a rotation invalidates every prior envelope. §6.2 gains the consumer-side signing rows, §6.9 gains the failure rows, §6.11's host-configuration recipes gain the consumer-side signing variables, §7.5.2 gains the policy key the §13.10 deletion removes from the spec, §7's ingest-error table gains `ingest.sign_failed`, §9.1's `SignatureProvider` built-in becomes the registry-managed key, §13.3's backup list gains the registry signing key, §13.10's signing row flips and its verification relaxation is deleted, §13.12 gains a `### Signing` subsection, and the §14 scenarios stop offering Sigstore-keyless as a registry signing mode and stop enumerating an MCP consumer's variables without the verification key. SPEC-10 adds the §13.4 stored-row admission paragraph, names admission beside the first-start rewrite in §13.12's `PODIUM_MIGRATION_OBJECT_READ_TIMEOUT` row and in §4.7.6's list of the parties that recompute the digest, states in §4.1, §7.2, and §7.6.2 that a resource the registry holds inline on the manifest record is returned inline at any size, and states in §4.7.4 that a declared `replaced_by` is returned on every load.

**Fixed decisions.**

- The whole of this proposal ships in the release that carries proposal 0026. 0026 rewrites every stored content hash in a pass the registry runs on the first start of the new version, before it ingests or serves; where a signer is configured that pass re-signs every row it rewrites and mints a first envelope for a stored row already at the framed digest that carries none. Flipping the producer default in the same release means that pass signs every row it writes. CODE-14's class table states what admission answers for a row the pass leaves untouched, and SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph and OQ-14 state when the pass runs again. The constraint is hard: a default flipped in a later release signs no stored row while the completion record is present. Within the release the producer default flips before the consumer defaults (S12 before S14), because the consumer defaults are safe only once the rewrite has attached envelopes.
- 0026 lands and is implemented first. Its pass takes the boot path's own signer, and the start is refused in two cases while the rewrite has not completed: a row the pass would rewrite is signed and no signer is configured, and signing is on, the key file is absent, and a stored row carries a signature. The signing-mode test behind both lives in one function, `registrySigningEnabled`, so CODE-4's change there reaches the loader and both refusals together, and no logic in the pass changes here. CODE-4 and TEST-4 own the fixtures in 0026's tests that read an unset `PODIUM_SIGN` as no signer, and the no-signer refusal's error wording. The Relationship section states the handoff.
- The policy has two axes. Verification of a present signature is unconditional under any policy other than `never`; the policy governs only a missing signature. `needsVerification` is deleted rather than kept beside the new predicate.
- The signature the §4.7.9 policy governs is verified before any gate reads manifest content: the §8.2 read event, the §4.4.1 sandbox and runtime gates, and the §6.7 adapter. Every body the consumer returns, caches, or writes is covered by the digest that signature attests, and the §5.0 `resources/read` mirror runs the same verification before it returns text (CODE-12). On this proposal's landing the digest is the served `content_hash`. Proposal 0027 replaces it with its delivery record and owns the order of the checks inside §6.6 step 2 from then on.
- This proposal lands after the implemented 0026 and before proposal 0027, and all three ship in one release. It is implementable and correct against the tree 0026 left, with no 0027 field present: the consumer verifies the served `signature` over the served `content_hash` and recomputes that hash from the served bytes. It stages no §6.6 step-2 text, no new §7.6.2 envelope field, and no consumer-side binding of the served record. Those are 0027's, and 0027 retargets this proposal's consumer policy onto its `delivery_signature`; the Relationship section names each surface it retargets. This proposal's §7.6.2 edit states the existing `inline` resource reference, which CODE-14 makes the form of every batch resource the row holds inline.
- The registry admits a stored row before it serves the row's content, in the form the owner chose on 2026-09-23 and SPEC-10 states: object-held bodies are read, the stored signature is verified where a signer is configured, every row of an `extends:` chain is admitted before its parent pin is followed, and the fields CODE-14's column table marks derived are served from the admitted bytes. The registry keeps no admission verdict.
- Both serve paths serve a resource the row holds inline from the admitted row, at any size, and presign only a resource whose bytes admission read from object storage (CODE-14's resource-source bullet). The resulting §7.6.2 batch wire change, `inline` in place of `presigned_url` for small resources on a deployment with an object store, ships in this MINOR release with no compatibility flag, and so does a declared `replaced_by` returned on every load.
- The policy reads no `sensitivity`, and `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` takes `never` or `always`. `medium-and-above` is deleted rather than kept as a rollout posture (CODE-13): after the split its only behavior waives the missing-signature requirement on a label the registry supplies, and for a child declaring `extends:` the merge takes the most-restrictive value while the hash covers the child's pre-merge bytes, so no attested source for that label exists.
- `Noop.Sign` stays. The audit anchor and the ingest tests depend on it. `Noop.Verify` refuses unconditionally, and "this deployment does not verify" is stated with `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never`.
- Verification material is resolved once, in `loadConfig`, by one resolver, into one `config` field. There is no second resolution and no capability predicate stated beside the resolver: a failed resolution under a policy above `never` is the startup refusal, and under `never` no material is resolved at all.
- Consumer key distribution stays out of band for every deployment other than standalone, where the key is on the same filesystem. This proposal adds no key-publication route and no trust-on-first-use pin.
- The consumer resolves the registry's key file by the same path resolution the registry uses, `sign.KeyFilePath`, rather than through a `sync.yaml` key. No configuration file carries key material, and the resolution order is stated once, in §4.7.9.
- The statements this proposal falsifies are enumerated once, as AUDIT-1's claim inventory. Each claim carries its finder: a token sweep over the identifiers the claim names, a prose finder for a file that asserts the claim without naming an identifier, or both. No deliverable carries an edit-site list that is not a record of what a named finder returns, and no deliverable carries its own exception to the sweep. The lists that are not such a record name sites a deliverable breaks through behavior rather than through a claim in prose, so no finder returns them, and the deliverable that names each owns it and S9.5 assigns them to no step: CODE-8's and TEST-8's enumeration of the `render` and `renderErr` call sites in `test/chart/render_test.go`, TEST-4's standard-stack e2e boots that the generated-key refusal reaches after a migration, and MAN-1's S64 disposition, whose **Expect** blocks hold only for an unsigned row. The assignment of files to steps is made at S9.5 and both axes are re-run at S17. A list in this document records what the finders return on one tree; the finders are what the implementor runs. A `CHANGELOG.md` release-note entry for a behavior this release adds is written from the deliverable it records, as `.claude/rules/release-process.md` requires, and is not an edit site. A pre-existing `CHANGELOG.md` paragraph that a finder returns is an edit site like any other.
- Sigstore verification correctness is a separate proposal. `SigstoreKeyless.Sign` is implemented and `podium sign --provider sigstore-keyless` mints a keyless envelope (`pkg/sign/sigstore.go:99-146`, `cmd/podium/sign.go:168-175`), but no registry signing mode does, so nothing puts a keyless envelope into the store at ingest and every verification defect there is latent for served artifacts. What lands here is the §4.7.9 text that stops claiming otherwise.
- No path attaches an envelope to a stored row, or replaces one, once the §13.4 first-start rewrite has completed, while the record of that completion stays in the metadata store. `podium admin migrate-to-standard` clears it on a target by design, and a party with store write access can delete it, which SPEC-10 states as a limit and OQ-14 carries. A row stored unsigned keeps that state and gains an envelope only when a new version of the artifact is ingested, and every earlier version stays as it is; on a signing registry, admission refuses each such row until then. A row signed under a key the registry no longer holds keeps its envelope, admission refuses it with `materialize.signature_invalid`, and the sentences below name its repair. The proposal states no delete-and-re-ingest rebuild as a repair, because no command hard-deletes a stored row, deleting manifest rows leaves every §4.7.3 dependency edge in place to be duplicated by the re-ingest, and the re-ingest restores only the version each directory currently declares. For a row whose stored hash is already the framed digest, which covers a key lost or rotated after the §13.4 rewrite and a migrated row the source had already framed, the repair is the key restore and restart SPEC-5's §13.12 text states, and it requires that key to still exist. A row the rewrite classified `signature_unverified` while it held the pre-framing hash stays at that hash and is not repaired by restoring the key alone. Two existing routes make the rewrite run again over such a row with the right key, and the docs keep both: on an upgraded store, restoring the backup the upgrade order takes, which carries no rewrite marker, and starting with the key that signed the rows (`CHANGELOG.md:87-91`), at the cost of every write made since that backup; and on a migration target, recreating the target store empty and re-running `podium admin migrate-to-standard` with the source's key at the target's `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, because the command clears the target's rewrite marker (`docs/reference/cli.md:627-629`, `CHANGELOG.md:96-108`). A new version of the artifact is the repair only where neither route is available, or where the key that signed the row no longer exists. OQ-5 carries a re-triggerable signing pass.
- Key rotation is not repaired. A rotation invalidates every envelope made under the retired key, the first-start rewrite does not re-sign a row whose envelope fails under the configured key, and each affected artifact loads again only at a new version. §4.7.9 states the behavior, and OQ-2 carries a key set.
- Podium is pre-1.0. There is no grace policy value, no "artifacts ingested before the upgrade" exemption, and no compatibility path for an unsigned row.

**Watch out for.**

- **Enabling signing on an existing registry signs no stored row.** `ingest.Ingest` short-circuits on `existing.ContentHash == mr.ContentHash` with `res.Idempotent++; continue` before the signing block, and no ingest path writes a signature onto an existing row. 0026's first-start rewrite is the one path that signs stored rows, and a default flipped in a later release signs no stored row while its completion record is present (SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph, OQ-14). A registry that enables signing after that start therefore keeps every row stored before it unsigned, and an artifact gains an envelope only when a new version of it is ingested. CODE-14's class table and SPEC-10 state what admission answers for such a row, and the Decision on the policy split states what `never` then covers. No command hard-deletes a stored row so that the same version can be re-ingested (`podium layer unregister` soft-deletes), and a delete-and-re-ingest done against the store directly duplicates every dependency edge and restores only the currently declared versions, so the proposal does not offer it as a repair. That is why the release window is a constraint rather than a preference.
- **The standalone relaxation fails open and no code change removes it from an existing machine.** `writeFileIfAbsent` never rewrites `~/.podium/sync.yaml`, so a machine that ran `podium serve` once keeps `defaults.verify_signatures: never` indefinitely, including when it is later pointed at a production registry through `PODIUM_REGISTRY`. The remedy is an operator editing one line, and the proposal says so rather than implying self-healing.
- **The relaxation also fires on the explicit-server-config branch.** `standaloneStartup` bootstraps on both branches, so a SQLite-backed server with an OIDC identity provider on a non-loopback bind writes the relaxation too. Deleting the line removes that leak; do not attempt to rescope the bootstrap itself, which also writes the registry pointer, `registry.yaml`, and the artifacts directory.
- **Do not reintroduce a sensitivity threshold.** `sensitivity` has no attested source for a child declaring `extends:`, so reading it from the hash-covered bytes lowers the gate on exactly the artifacts §4.6 protects, and reading it off the envelope lets the registry opt an artifact out of the requirement. Do not implement the relocation an earlier review asked for.
- **The cache replays pre-upgrade buckets through the new gate.** `deliverLoadArtifact` is the shared path, so a bucket warmed before the upgrade reaches the enforcing gate keyed under the pre-framing digest and carrying whatever envelope it was served with, and an `offline-only` consumer refuses it with no in-product recovery. 0026's mandated cache-directory clear repairs it in the same release.
- **The standalone server and the bridge do not share a home in the e2e harness.** `startServer` pins the server to `HOME=t.TempDir()` (`test/e2e/helpers_test.go:436`) while `mcpExec` reaches `runBin`, which pins the bridge to `cmdharness.IsolatedHome(t)` (`test/e2e/helpers_test.go:134`). `cmdharness.Run` also sets `PODIUM_NO_AUTOSTANDALONE=1` (`internal/testharness/cmdharness/cmdharness.go:52`), which suppresses `bootstrapStandaloneFiles` entirely. No existing test inherits the bootstrapped `sync.yaml`, so a test that exercises the standalone journey wires the two homes together deliberately (TEST-6).
- **Two separate breakages hit the existing suites, at S12 and at S14.** The first is a per-artifact refusal that arrives at S12: CODE-1 verifies any present signature under any policy above `never`, `Noop.Verify` refuses everything from CODE-2, and CODE-4 makes a zero-flag `startServer` registry sign every bootstrapped fixture, so from S12 every load through `podium-mcp` of a `startServer`-served artifact is refused whatever the fixture's sensitivity is, while the consumer defaults are still `noop` and `medium-and-above`. The second is the startup refusal CODE-5 adds at S14, under which any `podium-mcp` process with no verification material refuses to start. The audit therefore runs before S12 and covers every process that starts the bridge and every in-process `loadConfig` caller, rather than medium and high fixtures. The blanket `mcpServerEnv` edit reaches only the call sites built on that helper, and the e2e spawns that pass an inline environment or another helper are found with the audit's `mcpExec(` finder.
- **A standalone server signs at ingest from the step that flips the producer default.** `startServer` passes no `--sign` (`test/e2e/helpers_test.go:431-437`) and the boot path hands the resolved signer to the filesystem bootstrap ingest (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:903-910`, `:1002`, `:510`), so every fixture a zero-flag server ingests carries an envelope from S12 onward. A case whose subject is an unsigned artifact starts its server with `PODIUM_SIGN=none`; one that does not stays in the suite asserting a different code. `test/manual-validation.md` inherits the same change, and its isolation block has to keep the generated key and the bootstrapped `~/.podium/sync.yaml` inside the scenario's throwaway directory.
- **The default key lives in the registry process's home.** `registrySigningKeyPath` falls back to `~/.podium/standalone/registry-signing.key` (`internal/serverboot/signing.go:22-31`), and the published image, which `.github/workflows/release.yml:448-454` builds from the repository-root `Dockerfile`, runs as `nonroot` on `gcr.io/distroless/base-debian12:nonroot` (`Dockerfile:62`, `:69`), so its home is `/home/nonroot`, with no volume over it. Where the store outlives that home, a recreated container would generate a fresh key: once the §13.4 rewrite has completed, every row the lost key signed would be refused by the registry's admission and at the consumer with `materialize.signature_invalid`, and while the rewrite is held back, as a `body_unavailable` row holds it (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:356-361`, the marker decision at `:407-411`), no key that still exists would repair the rows the pass already signed (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:365-375`). The Helm chart (CODE-8) and the compose stack (CODE-11) are handled by configuration, and every other deployment by CODE-4's persistence refusal: a start with signing on, no `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, and a store outside the directory the default key lives in is refused before any key is generated. The published image run with its default SQLite store keeps the key and the store in the same directory, so a recreate loses both together and strands no signature.
- **A key file outlives the signing mode that generated it.** `sign.KeyFilePath` resolves the same path whatever `PODIUM_SIGN` says, and nothing in the product deletes the file, so a home whose earlier start generated a key resolves it under `PODIUM_SIGN=none` and under a later remote registry. The consumer then starts and refuses each load, with `materialize.signature_missing` for an unsigned row and `materialize.signature_invalid` for a row another key signed. Both arms are fail-closed, and TEST-5 and TEST-6 pin one each.
- **`materialize.signature_missing` is a matrix cell and a documented code that no served error carries.** `deliverLoadArtifact` prefixes every policy failure with `materialize.signature_invalid`, so the missing-signature case surfaces under the wrong code. Existing tests do not all pass either way: `assertUnsignedHighRefused` (`test/e2e/governance_adoption_test.go:959`) and `TestArtifactResponse_SignatureInvalid` (`test/e2e/artifact_response_test.go:749`) match the literal `signature_invalid` on a load whose signature is absent, and `spi.Error.Error()` returns the Message alone (`pkg/spi/errors.go:33`), so `materialize.signature_missing: signature_missing: …` contains no `signature_invalid` and both go red at S11. TEST-2 carries their disposition.
- **Admission refuses direct-store test fixtures and object-held bodies on unwired registries.** From S13, a record written without ingest whose hash or envelope admission would not reproduce is refused, and so is an object-held body on a registry given no object store through `WithAdmission`. TEST-14's fixture and object-store finders select both, and the dispositions land in S13.
- **Admission reads object storage on a full load.** A full load reads every object-held body of every row in the requested artifact's `extends:` chain, parents included. An object-storage outage therefore refuses such a load with `registry.unavailable`, and the outage edge row states the outcome. A HEAD and a matching conditional GET read nothing (SPEC-10).
- **`podium sync` and both SDKs verify nothing at any setting.** `EnforceVerification` has one production caller. Every claim this proposal makes about what a consumer verifies is a claim about `podium-mcp`. Neither `podium sync` nor either SDK compares a fetched body with its hash (CODE-14's bullet on the paths that serve no admitted content). Do not write a sentence that credits "the consumer" with that check.
- **CODE-14 changes two wire behaviors.** On a deployment with an object store, an `artifacts:batchLoad` item carries its resources at or below the inline cutoff in `inline` rather than `presigned_url`, so a third-party client that reads only `presigned_url` loses them. On a SQLite or Postgres store, a load of an artifact that is not deprecated returns its declared `replaced_by`, as the memory store already does. SPEC-10 states both, and DOC-1's `CHANGELOG.md` bullet carries a `Changed` entry for each.

## Implementation checklist

- [ ] **S1 · spec** — SPEC-10, SPEC-1, SPEC-3, SPEC-4, SPEC-5, and SPEC-6, applied, verified, and committed as one spec sub-step. §13.4 gains the stored-row admission paragraph and its sentence on a row still at the previous release's digest names the registry's refusal, §13.12's object-read deadline row names admission, §4.7.6's recomputing-parties sentence names admission, §4.7.4's lifecycle bullet returns a declared `replaced_by` on every load, §4.1's inline-cutoff bullet and §7.2's data-plane paragraph return a resource held inline on the manifest record at any size, and §7.6.2's Bandwidth bullet names the inline resource reference (SPEC-10). §4.7.9 is rewritten: what a signature attests, the two policy axes, the constraint that the governed signature is verified before any gate reads the manifest, consumer-side key handling, the rotation limitation, and what keyless verification does not check, and §14.9 step 3 and §14.12 step 4, which offer Sigstore-keyless as a selectable registry signing mode, are rewritten with it (SPEC-1). §6.2's `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` default becomes `always`, and the table gains the consumer-side provider, key, and trust-root rows (SPEC-3). §6.9 gains the verification failure rows, and spec/07's ingest-error table and ingest `Errors.` sentence gain `ingest.sign_failed` (SPEC-4). §13.3's backup list gains the registry signing key, §13.10's signing row flips, its signature-verification bullet is deleted, and §13.12 gains a `### Signing` subsection (SPEC-5). §7.5.2's `defaults:` schema block and prose gain `verify_signatures`, the one signing-related key that lives in `sync.yaml` (SPEC-6). The six land in one commit because their staged texts cite one another: SPEC-1's §4.7.9 text cites §13.10's flipped signing row and the key path SPEC-5 adds, §13.12's `### Signing` subsection, the §6.9 refusal row SPEC-4 adds, and the §13.4 admission paragraph SPEC-10 adds; SPEC-3's `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` and `PODIUM_SIGSTORE_TRUST_ROOT_PEM_FILE` rows cite that §6.9 row; SPEC-5's text relies on the rewritten §4.7.9, the §13.4 admission paragraph, and the §7.5.2 key SPEC-6 adds; and SPEC-6's text cites the rewritten §4.7.9.
      Levels: —. Depends on: —

- [ ] **S8 · spec** — SPEC-8. §6.11's host-configuration recipes gain `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER` and `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, §14.7 step 1 defers to the §6.11 recipe instead of enumerating variables, and §14.5's operator setup gains the key-distribution statement as an unnumbered sentence, so no numbered item moves.

Run both axes of the sweep, assign each file either axis returns to the step that falsifies its statement, and record the files that assert nothing that changes with the reason. Produces no code change; the assignment is what S10, S12, S13, S14, S15, S16, and S17 apply.

- [ ] **S10 · code** — CODE-1, CODE-2, TEST-1, TEST-9 (the cases CODE-2 alone reddens, which TEST-9's step-assignment paragraph names), AUDIT-1's S10 share of the S9.5 assignment, which includes `pkg/sign/registry_managed.go`'s doc comment on the key's scope and rotation. `EnforceVerification` splits into the two axes, `needsVerification` is deleted, `Noop.Verify` refuses, and the tests that assert a `Noop` envelope verifies, are moved onto a registry-managed fixture. The CLI round trips that reach `provider.Verify` without going through `EnforceVerification` become refusal assertions here and move onto a keyed registry-managed fixture in S14, because `loadSignatureProvider` hands the CLI a keyless `RegistryManagedKey` until CODE-5 resolves key material. **Ordering constraints.** The spec steps precede the code per `.claude/rules/spec-driven-development.md`. This proposal lands before proposal 0027, so no step here depends on it, and 0027's steps follow S17. The step ids are the ones the checklist carried before the scope cut, so the removed steps leave gaps and every disposition that names a step keeps its meaning; S13 is new, and no earlier disposition names it. S1 carries SPEC-10, SPEC-1, SPEC-3, SPEC-4, SPEC-5, and SPEC-6 as one spec sub-step, and the ids S3, S4, S5, S5a, and S6 are retired into it rather than reused. The implement-proposal pipeline applies, verifies to clean, and commits each spec sub-step on its own before the next begins, and these texts cite one another in both directions, as S1's entry records: SPEC-1's §4.7.9 text cites §13.10's flipped signing row, §13.12's `### Signing` subsection, and the §6.9 refusal row, while SPEC-5's text relies on the rewritten §4.7.9 (`spec/13-deployment.md:153` still reads "Disabled by default", and no spec file names the key path or `config.signature_provider_unavailable` today). No sequence of separate commits leaves every commit verifying clean. S7 and S8 stay separate because their texts cite only sections S1 has already landed. S9.5 follows all three spec steps, because AUDIT-1 treats a file under `spec/` as a confirmation of edits already landed and routes a claim that a spec-only deliverable falsifies in code to a later code step. S13 follows S1, which states the admission rule, and S12, because from S12 a zero-flag registry signs, so admission's signature check runs on every registry the suites boot and TEST-14's fixture disposition is settled once. S10 precedes S11 because the reordered pipeline calls the two-axis function. S12 precedes S14 so that the first-start rewrite 0026 stages signs the rows it writes before any consumer starts requiring a signature; landing S14 first refuses every load in the window between them. S15 and S16 follow S12 because the chart's and the compose stack's signing values name the modes CODE-4 defines, and S17 follows the code so the changelog describes what the tested build does.

## Current state and the gap

### The policy skips the cryptography on a value the registry supplies

`sign.EnforceVerification` (`pkg/sign/sign.go`) returns `nil` before reaching the provider whenever `needsVerification(policy, sensitivity)` is false, which under the documented `medium-and-above` default (`spec/06-mcp-server.md:34`) is every artifact the response does not report at `medium` or `high`. The sensitivity it reads is `resp.Sensitivity`, the JSON envelope field, served from the `manifests` column and carried across a restart by the cache's side files. A registry that sets `"sensitivity":"low"` on a response therefore skips the entire cryptographic path with no forgery, no key, and no collision.

Cross-checking the wire field against the frontmatter closes nothing, because a registry forging an artifact writes the same value into the frontmatter and recomputes the hash over its own bytes. Relocating the read to the hash-covered bytes is worse than neutral. `pkg/manifest/merge.go` merges sensitivity most-restrictive and `foldExtendsParent` folds the merged value into the stored column, so for a child declaring `extends:` the served value can be `high` while the pre-merge `ARTIFACT.md` the hash covers declares `low` or declares nothing. A consumer reading the trigger from the attested bytes would skip verification on an artifact the registry itself classified `high`. There is no attested source for a child's effective sensitivity, which makes the conditional trigger a control with no sound input.

### The order gives the content-hash check nothing to defend against

`deliverLoadArtifact` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go`) emits the §8.2 read event, enforces the signature policy, enforces the §4.4.1 sandbox gate, and enforces the §4.4.1 runtime gate, and only then recomputes the digest in `verifyContentHash`. Each of the three gates reads `resp.Frontmatter` at a point where nothing has bound those bytes to the served digest, and the read event applies the manifest's `audit_redact` directive from the same unverified bytes.

`verifyContentHash` compares a value recomputed from registry-supplied bytes against a registry-supplied digest, which detects a tampering intermediary and nothing else. It becomes a check against the registry only once the digest has been attested. `Provider.Verify(ctx, contentHash, signature)` consumes no manifest bytes, so the signature check can run before any fetch, and running it first is what gives the recomputation a party to defend against. The spec fixes neither order: §6.6 step 2 is one undivided sentence (`spec/06-mcp-server.md:239`). CODE-3 moves the signature check and the recomputation ahead of every gate that reads manifest content. Proposal 0027, which lands after this proposal, then replaces the recomputation with the §4.7.10 delivery record.

### No default verifies anything

`registrySignerFor` (`internal/serverboot/signing.go`) returns `(nil, nil)` unless the mode is `registry-key`, and `registry-key` is the only accepted non-empty value, so `ingest.Request.Signer` stays nil and `mr.Signature` is never assigned. `buildSignatureProvider` maps both `""` and `"noop"` to `sign.Noop{}`, whose `Verify` accepts `"noop:" + contentHash`, a value any party holding the response can compute. The standalone bootstrap writes `defaults.verify_signatures: never` into `~/.podium/sync.yaml`, honored by `loadConfig` whenever `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` is unset.

The one verifying configuration needs a registry started with `--sign registry-key`, a consumer provider of `registry-managed`, a consumer key in `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, and a policy above `never`, coordinated across two processes with no in-band key distribution. `test/e2e/signed_artifact_helpers_test.go` is the only place in the repository where all of them are set.

### Enabling signing later signs no stored row

`ingest.Ingest` short-circuits the idempotent branch before the signing block, `PutManifest` enforces the §4.7 immutability invariant, and `podium layer unregister` soft-deletes without removing the row. A `podium layer reingest` after enabling signing therefore reports every artifact idempotent and leaves every stored `Signature` empty. 0026's first-start rewrite is the one path that attaches an envelope to a stored row: where a signer is configured it re-signs every row it rewrites and mints a first envelope for a stored row already at the framed digest that carries none (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:207-215` classifies that row `classUnmigrated`, and the apply step signs it at `:365-375`). It runs on the first start of the new version, so flipping the producer default in the same release signs every row the pass writes, in the start every deployment already performs. The apply loop `continue`s past the `signature_unverified`, `unreproducible`, `body_missing`, and `body_unavailable` classes before the signing block (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:343-362`), and CODE-14's class table states what the registry's stored-row admission answers for each. SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph and OQ-14 state when the pass runs again. While its completion record is present, a registry that enables signing after that start gets no second pass, and an artifact whose stored rows are unsigned gains an envelope only when a new version of it is ingested, because ingest signs a row it inserts (`pkg/registry/ingest/ingest.go:780-796`) and short-circuits on every row it already holds at the same content hash (`:759-766`).

### The bootstrap relaxation is sticky and out of scope

`verifySignaturesFromSyncYAML` resolves `defaults.verify_signatures` from the workspace `.podium/sync.yaml` and then the home-global file with no association to the registry in use, and `writeFileIfAbsent` never rewrites an existing file. A developer who runs `podium serve` once and is later pointed at a production registry through `PODIUM_REGISTRY` keeps the relaxation. `standaloneStartup` also bootstraps on the `hasExplicitServerConfig` branch, so a multi-user SQLite deployment with an OIDC provider writes it too.

### Codes this surface returns are unregistered

`materialize.content_hash_mismatch` gained its §6.9 row, its §6.10 axis cell, and its annotated test from proposal 0026, so it needs nothing here. `config.signature_provider_unavailable` and `ingest.sign_failed` are returned by `pkg/sign` and `pkg/registry/ingest` and appear in neither. `materialize.signature_missing` is on the matrix axis and in the docs but nowhere in `spec/`, and no served error carries it as its leading code, because `deliverLoadArtifact` prefixes every policy failure with `materialize.signature_invalid`.

### §4.7.9 describes a model the code does not implement

The section calls Sigstore-keyless preferred and names a transparency-log entry. `SigstoreKeyless.Verify` never reads the certificate's subject or issuer, validates the chain at wall-clock time against a certificate that lives minutes, and treats an HTTP 200 from Rekor as sufficient while discarding the body and the attested time. The log check is also skipped when the envelope itself declares `log_index: -1`. None of this has a product effect: `registrySignerFor` accepts only `registry-key`, and no code path anywhere outside tests mints a Sigstore envelope into the store. The section also states that the registry-managed key is rotated quarterly, while `RegistryManagedKey` holds one keypair and no path re-signs a stored row, so a rotation invalidates every prior envelope permanently.

## Decisions

- **Split the policy into two axes, and delete the sensitivity condition.** The conflation of "check the signature I was given" with "require a signature" is the defect. Unconditional verification of a present signature costs nothing operationally and closes the free bypass for every signed artifact. After the split, `medium-and-above`'s only behavior is to waive the missing-signature requirement for an artifact the registry reports below `medium`, and the registry is the party that requirement defends against, so the value is deleted (CODE-13) and the policy takes `never` or `always`. `never` keeps a catalog loadable only against a registry running with `PODIUM_SIGN=none`: on a signing registry the §13.4 stored-row admission refuses every row stored unsigned to every reader whatever the consumer's policy (CODE-14), so a deployment that means to sign keeps signing on from its first start. `docs/deployment/progressive-adoption.md`, which was built on the value, is restated around the two (DOC-1). The deletion lands with the fallback flip to `always` rather than with the split, so no commit defaults to a value that no longer exists.
- **Verify before any gate reads the manifest, and leave the §6.6 step-2 text to 0027.** This proposal lands first, so CODE-3 orders today's checks: the §4.7.9 policy over the served `signature` and `content_hash`, then the recomputation, both ahead of the §8.2 read event and the §4.4.1 gates. It stages no §6.6 step-2 text, because proposal 0027 replaces that step with its delivery record in the same release, and step-2 text written against the recomputation would be rewritten one proposal later. §4.7.9 states the constraint that holds for any digest: the signature the policy governs is verified before any gate reads manifest content, and every body the consumer returns, caches, or writes is covered by the digest that signature attests.
- **Move the read event below the verification.** It applies the manifest's `audit_redact` directive, so it is a control reading unattested input, which is the same defect class as the sensitivity read. CODE-3 moves the read event and the two §4.4.1 gates below the verification and keeps the read event above the two gates. A load the verification refuses therefore records no local `artifact.loaded` event, while a load the §4.4.1 gates refuse still records one. The read event also moves below whatever runs above the verification, which is the inline-resource decode and the large-resource fetch, so a load that fails either records nothing locally where today it records the event. That widening is a cost the move carries rather than a goal, and the registry's own audit stream records the call in every case.
- **Make `Noop.Verify` refuse.** A provider that accepts a value derived from a public field is an acceptance predicate rather than a placeholder, and it satisfies `always`. `Sign` is a legitimate placeholder and is unchanged.
- **Resolve the verifier once rather than per load.** `buildSignatureProvider` runs on every `load_artifact` today (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2008`), reading the environment at call time, so adding the key-file fallback to it would put a file read on every delivery. Moving the call into `loadConfig` and storing the result makes the refusal a consequence of the resolution rather than a predicate beside it, so nothing states twice what "can verify" means, and it removes the per-load I/O. The cost is that a key file replaced under a running bridge takes effect at restart, which matches every other `config` field.
- **Flip the producer default before the consumer defaults, in that order, in one release.** Signing is producer-side and breaks nothing on its own. The consumer defaults are what break a deployment, and they are safe only once 0026's first-start rewrite has attached envelopes.
- **Resolve the consumer's key from the same path the registry resolves, rather than through a `sync.yaml` key.** `readOrCreateEd25519` writes one file carrying a `private:` line and a `public:` line, at mode `0o600` in a `0o700` directory, and `registrySigningKeyPath` already resolves its location from `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` or the `~/.podium/standalone/registry-signing.key` default. That resolver moves into `pkg/sign` as `KeyFilePath`, and the consumer falls back to it when `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is unset, so the handover is the file mode rather than a configuration value. A config key would have to be written into a file the bootstrap does not rewrite, which means a line-inserting mutator over the operator's own YAML, and it would be scope-resolved workspace-first, which puts trust material on a path a checked-in `.podium/sync.yaml` controls. Neither cost buys anything the shared resolver does not already give.
- **Keep consumer key distribution out of band, except in standalone.** In standalone the registry's key file sits on the same filesystem, under the same user account, as the consumer, so the consumer reads it directly. Elsewhere the operator copies the public half into each consumer's `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, which is a documented step rather than a new protocol: DOC-1 stages the extraction command on `docs/deployment/clustered.md`, and TEST-12's D-clustered case executes it. A published-key route with a first-use pin is a mechanism with its own security semantics and belongs in its own proposal.
- **Carry the §4.7.9 correction now and defer the Sigstore work.** A spec that claims verification properties the code does not deliver is the defect this proposal exists to remove, and correcting text costs nothing. Building certificate-identity policy and transparency-log verification carries a dependency decision and has no product effect until the registry gains a keyless signing mode.
- **Do not repair key rotation.** A key set is a provider redesign that belongs with the Sigstore work. 0026's first-start rewrite leaves untouched a row whose stored envelope does not verify under the configured key, because it cannot tell a rotated key from a forged envelope, so neither its first run nor a rerun (SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph) repairs a rotation. CODE-14's class table and SPEC-10 state what admission answers for a row signed under a retired key, and a rotation leaves every earlier version refused until a new version of it is ingested. §4.7.9 states the accepted behavior instead, and OQ-2 carries the question.
- **Admit a stored row at the registry, reading its object-held bodies and its stored signature.** Once proposal 0027 lands, the registry countersigns whatever it serves, so a row altered in the metadata store would reach every consumer with a valid delivery signature. Today a consumer under `always` holding the registry's key refuses such a row: the stored envelope does not verify over the altered bytes' digest, and a party that can write the store cannot mint that envelope without the key. The registry keeps that property by checking the row before it serves it. CODE-14's column table gives each stored field one disposition and its reason. Recomputing the hash alone admits an editor who writes new bytes and their digest together, so the stored signature is verified where a signer is configured. A signing registry also refuses an absent signature, because treating one as admissible admits an editor who clears the envelope. Such an editor's one route to an envelope is the limit SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph states and OQ-14 carries. Verifying the signature alone admits an editor who alters a column the hash does not cover. So the body, type, sensitivity, deprecation flag, replacement, and `audit_redact` key set are served from the admitted bytes, the version a load returns is the stored row's, and each bundled resource's stored hash, size, and path are bound to the bytes admission read. Admitting the requested row alone admits an altered parent folded into a child, so every row of the chain is admitted. The chain is read from the stored parent pin, which neither the hash nor the signature covers, so each row's pin is checked against the `extends:` reference its admitted bytes declare. A pin redirected to another version that still satisfies a range or unpinned reference passes that check, and the edge-case table records it. The selection columns stay unbound, because the signature covers the content hash alone (`pkg/sign/registry_managed.go:75`, called at `pkg/registry/ingest/ingest.go:786`) and the manifest carries no artifact ID (`pkg/manifest/types.go:100-136`); SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph states the limit, and OQ-13 carries the question. A HEAD and a conditional GET whose validator matches return no content, so they are answered without admission, and no verdict is kept (OQ-11). The check recomputes the hash before it verifies the signature, so a row still at the previous release's digest reports `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`, the code proposal 0026's upgrade note names, whatever its envelope. The refusals reuse the three consumer codes they correspond to and `registry.unavailable` for a failed object read, so no §6.10 code is minted. The serve paths choose each resource's source by the rule the next Decision gives.
- **Serve each resource the row holds inline from the admitted row, on both paths, and accept the §7.6.2 wire change.** Admission hashes a ref's `Inline` bytes when they are present and reads no object for that ref, so a presigned link to that ref's object names bytes nothing checked. Today `loadOneForBatch` presigns every ref whenever an object store is configured (`pkg/registry/server/batch_load.go:135-148`), and ingest keeps an inline copy beside the uploaded object for every resource at or below the cutoff (`pkg/registry/ingest/ingest.go:1276-1291`). So on a deployment with an object store, every small resource reaches batch readers through an object admission never read. Admission could instead read the object of every ref a batch item presigns. That costs one object read per small resource per batch item, and during an object-storage outage it would refuse every batch item that bundles any resource. Serving the inline bytes admission already hashed reads nothing more, and this proposal takes that repair. On a deployment with an object store, each `artifacts:batchLoad` item then carries every resource at or below the §4.1 inline cutoff in the reference's `inline` field, with `inline_base64` for bytes that are not valid UTF-8, and no `presigned_url`, where today it carries a `presigned_url`. A resource above the cutoff keeps its `presigned_url`. The field already exists in the standalone-without-storage mode (`pkg/registry/server/batch_load.go:57-69`), and both SDKs' batch materializers already read it (`sdks/podium-py/podium/client.py:344-355`, `sdks/podium-ts/src/index.ts:606-618`). `podium-mcp`'s prefetch decodes no batch resource (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2401-2417`). A third-party client that reads only `presigned_url` loses the small resources. Podium is pre-1.0, so the change ships in this MINOR release with SPEC-10's §7.6.2 edit and a `CHANGELOG.md` `Changed` entry, and with no compatibility flag. A batch response grows by each item's small inline resources, which the 50-item cap and the per-resource cutoff bound, the same per-artifact bound `load_artifact` already carries under §7.2. CODE-14's resource-source bullet states the rule.

## Spec amendment: §4.7.9 signing

**SPEC-1.** Replace the section body under the `### 4.7.9 Signing` heading (`spec/04-artifact-model.md:895`), keeping the heading. The replaced span runs from `Each artifact version is signed by the author's key at commit time, or by a registry-managed key at ingest. Two key models:` through `` `podium verify <artifact>` for ad-hoc verification. `podium sign <artifact>` for explicit signing outside the ingest flow. ``, which is `spec/04-artifact-model.md:897-904` as 0026 left the file. Match on those two lines rather than on the numbers, which drift.

```markdown
Each artifact version is signed by the author's key at commit time, or by a registry-managed key at ingest. Two key models:

- **Registry-managed key.** One Ed25519 keypair per registry deployment, loaded from `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` and generated on first run when absent. Every process serving one store signs under that one key, across every tenant that deployment serves. The registry signs at ingest by default (§13.10).
- **Sigstore-keyless.** An OIDC-attested signature with a transparency-log entry and no key management. No registry signing mode produces one; the consumer-side verifier does not check the certificate's signer identity and does not check the log entry's contents, so a keyless envelope attests that some certificate the configured trust root issued signed the digest, and nothing about who holds it.

The signature the registry mints at ingest attests the artifact's `content_hash` (§4.7.6) and nothing else, and it is stored with the artifact. A registry configured with a signer verifies it at its §13.4 stored-row admission before it serves the artifact, and a registry with signing off serves it as stored. It is served with the artifact as `signature`, and that served signature is the one the policy below governs.

Under any policy other than `never`, the MCP server verifies every signature the registry serves it before the §8.2 read event, the §4.4.1 gates, or the §6.7 adapter reads manifest content (§6.6 step 2), and every body it returns, caches, or writes is covered by the digest that signature attests or is served by the registry from stored rows it admitted (§13.4). The §6.4 workspace overlay is outside this: its bytes come from the consumer's own filesystem rather than from the registry, it carries no signature, and no policy value requires one for it. The §5.0 resources mirror of `load_artifact` runs the same §6.6 step-2 verification before it returns an artifact's text, returns no text for a load that verification refuses, and returns only text the verified record covers. Above `never`, `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` (§6.2) governs whether a *missing* signature aborts the load; `never` checks nothing, including a signature the response carries. The policy takes `never` or `always`, and `always` requires one on every registry-served artifact. No policy value reads `sensitivity`: the registry reports that value and signs the record that carries it, so a threshold on it would be set by the party verification defends against. A signature that does not validate aborts materialization with `materialize.signature_invalid`; a required signature that is absent aborts with `materialize.signature_missing`.

The consumer verifies the signature it is served under the registry's public key, which it holds. It resolves that key in one order, which is normative here and cross-referenced everywhere else: the base64 value in `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, and otherwise the `public:` line of the registry-managed key file at `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, which defaults to `~/.podium/standalone/registry-signing.key` (§13.10). A set `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is authoritative: the key file is consulted only when the variable is unset, and a set value that does not decode to an Ed25519 public key refuses the start with `config.signature_provider_unavailable`, naming the variable, rather than falling through to the file. The second source resolves only where the registry runs on the same machine under the same user account, because the registry writes that file readable by its owner alone; every other deployment supplies the variable. `podium sign` needs the private half and resolves it from the `private:` line of the same file, on the same path resolution, with no variable carrying a private key value. A consumer resolves this material once, at startup, and refuses to start with `config.signature_provider_unavailable` when its policy is above `never` and no usable material resolves (§6.9). Under `never` it resolves none and verifies nothing. No configuration file carries key material.

A registry deployment that serves one store from more than one process resolves `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` to the same key file in every process. A consumer holds one verification key, so a process signing under a different key mints envelopes that consumer refuses (§13.12).

Rotating the registry-managed key invalidates every signature made under the retired key, and the registry's stored-row admission (§13.4) refuses each row that key signed. A stored signature is written at ingest, and the §13.4 first-start rewrite re-signs only a row whose stored envelope verifies under the configured key and does not run again while the record of its completion stays in the store (§13.4), so neither repairs a rotation, and no other path re-signs a stored row. An artifact regains a verifiable signature only when a new version of it is ingested; every version stored before the rotation stays refused.

`podium verify <artifact>` performs ad-hoc verification, and `podium sign <artifact>` signs explicitly outside the ingest flow. Under `registry-managed`, `podium verify` resolves the public key by the order stated above and reads no private key, and `podium sign` resolves the private key from the key file alone and does not read `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`. An invocation that cannot resolve the key it needs fails with `config.signature_provider_unavailable`.
```

SPEC-1 also carries the two §14 sentences that offer Sigstore-keyless as a selectable registry signing mode, which this section stops describing as one. Both are rewritten in the same step, because after the replacement above the spec would otherwise instruct an operator to select a mode §4.7.9 and §9.1 no longer define, and `PODIUM_SIGN` accepts only `registry-key` and `none` (SPEC-5).

- `spec/14-common-scenarios.md:123`, step 3 of §14.9, whose current text is `3. Configure freeze windows, admin grants, signing (Sigstore-keyless or registry-managed).`, becomes `3. Configure freeze windows, admin grants, and registry-managed-key signing (§4.7.9).`
- `spec/14-common-scenarios.md:180`, step 4 of §14.12, whose current text is `4. Sigstore-keyless requires public OIDC infrastructure; air-gapped deployments use the registry-managed signing key path instead.`, becomes a statement that the registry signs with a registry-managed key in every deployment, that an air-gapped deployment points `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` at a key file on internal storage, and that no public OIDC infrastructure is involved (§4.7.9, §13.12).

## Spec amendment: §6.2 the consumer signing settings

**SPEC-3.** Replace the `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` row at `spec/06-mcp-server.md:34` and append the rows below it. The table's preamble (`spec/06-mcp-server.md:21`) states that each row's `--flag` and config-file equivalents are accepted. The bridge's flag and config-file parser has a case for `verify-signatures` and `signature-provider` and none for the key-material settings (`cmd/podium-mcp/cliconfig.go:97-133`), and §4.7.9 states that no configuration file carries key material, so each key-material row states that it is environment only, in the form §13.12 already uses (`spec/13-deployment.md:416`). No CODE item adds a flag or a config-file key for them. The `PODIUM_SIGSTORE_TRUST_ROOT_PEM_FILE` row is added because the §6.9 refusal SPEC-4 stages names that variable for `sigstore-keyless`, and no spec section defines it today (`grep -rn PODIUM_SIGSTORE spec/` returns nothing), while the bridge and the CLI already read it (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2183`, `cmd/podium/sign.go:169`).

| Parameter | Description | Default |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` | Signature policy on materialization: `never` or `always` (§4.7.9). Any other value, including `medium-and-above`, refuses startup | `always` |
| `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER` | Selected `SignatureProvider` (§9.1): `noop`, `registry-managed`, or `sigstore-keyless`. An unrecognized value refuses startup with `config.invalid` | `registry-managed` |
| `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` | Base64 Ed25519 public key for `registry-managed` verification, supplied out of band. Environment only; no flag or config-file key | (unset → the `public:` line of the key file at `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, §4.7.9) |
| `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_KEY_ID` | Expected signing-key fingerprint. When both it and the envelope's `key_id` are set, an envelope from another key is refused. Environment only; no flag or config-file key | (unset → no fingerprint check) |
| `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` | Registry-managed key file. A consumer process reads it and never writes it: the `public:` line supplies the verification key when `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is unset, and `podium sign` takes the `private:` line from the same file (§4.7.9). The `public:` line is consulted for verification only under the `registry-managed` provider, and only when `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is unset; `podium sign` reads the `private:` line whatever that variable holds, because it carries a public key alone. A policy above `never` under which `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is set and does not decode to an Ed25519 public key, or is unset and the key file yields no usable key, refuses the start with `config.signature_provider_unavailable` (§4.7.9, §6.9). Under `sigstore-keyless` the file is not consulted for verification. Environment only; no flag or config-file key | `~/.podium/standalone/registry-signing.key` |
| `PODIUM_SIGSTORE_TRUST_ROOT_PEM_FILE` | Path to the PEM trust root `sigstore-keyless` verification checks an envelope's certificate chain against. The MCP server reads it only under the `sigstore-keyless` provider and a policy above `never`; unset, or naming no readable file, under that provider and such a policy refuses the MCP server's start with `config.signature_provider_unavailable`, naming the variable (§4.7.9, §6.9). `podium sign` and `podium verify` read it under `--provider sigstore-keyless` whatever the policy and do not refuse on an unset or unreadable value; `podium verify` then refuses each envelope as `materialize.signature_invalid`, because no trust root is configured. Environment only; no flag or config-file key | (unset) |

## Spec amendment: §6.9 failure modes and the ingest code

**SPEC-4.** Replace the single row at `spec/06-mcp-server.md:411`, whose current text is `| Signature verification failure                | Fail with `materialize.signature_invalid`; do not write to disk.                                                                                            |`, with the rows below. Match on that text rather than on the number, which drifts. The table runs `spec/06-mcp-server.md:398-418`, and every other row in it stays as it is, including the "Content hash mismatch at the §6.6 step-2 check" row at `:412` that proposal 0026 added, which proposal 0027 later amends in place.

| Failure | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Signature verification failure | Fail with `materialize.signature_invalid`; do not write to disk. The check runs before any gate reads the manifest (§6.6 step 2) |
| Required signature absent | Fail with `materialize.signature_missing`; do not write to disk. Required on every registry-served artifact under `always` |
| A policy above `never` and no verification material | Refuse to start with `config.signature_provider_unavailable`, naming each source tried. Applies to `noop` under any such policy, to `registry-managed` when `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is unset and the key file does not resolve, or `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is set and does not decode to an Ed25519 public key, and to `sigstore-keyless` when `PODIUM_SIGSTORE_TRUST_ROOT_PEM_FILE` is unset or names no readable file (§6.2). The consumer supplies material or sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` |

§6.10 enumerates no codes. It carries the envelope schema, worked `auth.*` and `registry.*` examples, and the sentence naming the namespaces (`spec/06-mcp-server.md:502`), so the three consumer-side codes this proposal makes normative land in the §6.9 rows above rather than in a §6.10 list, and §6.10 is not edited. `tools/matrix/matrices.go` is the registration surface for the §6.10 axis, and CODE-7 updates it in the same change; each cell that is new to the axis carries a `// Matrix: §6.10 (<code>)` annotated test.

`ingest.sign_failed` is an ingest-time failure that §6.9's MCP failure table does not cover, and signing at ingest becomes the default here, so it gains a spec home in §7: a row in the **Ingest cases:** list (`spec/07-external-integration.md:78-87`) reading that a configured signer that fails rejects that one artifact with `ingest.sign_failed` while the rest of the batch is accepted, and the code added to the ingest **Errors.** sentence (`spec/07-external-integration.md:129`).

## Spec amendment: §13.3, §13.10, and §13.12 the deployment defaults

**SPEC-5.** Replace the §13.10 comparison table's Signing row, whose current text is `| Signing | Sigstore-keyless or registry-managed key | Disabled by default; opt in via `--sign registry-key` |` (`spec/13-deployment.md:153`).

```markdown
| Signing                 | Registry-managed key                            | Enabled by default. The Ed25519 keypair is generated on first run at `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` (default `~/.podium/standalone/registry-signing.key`). `--sign none` disables it |
```

Delete the bullet beginning `- **Signature verification.** The consumer-side signature-verification default relaxes to `never`` (`spec/13-deployment.md:207`) in full. The standalone deployment no longer relaxes the consumer policy, because the registry signs by default and a consumer on that machine resolves the registry's own key file (§4.7.9).

Add a bullet to §13.3's backup list (`spec/13-deployment.md:70-75`), after the `- Object storage: cross-region replication or snapshots.` bullet. With signing on by default the registry signing key is durable state.

```markdown
- Registry signing key: the file at `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, or the Secret a Helm deployment mounts there, backed up beside the store it signs. Losing it leaves every row it signed refused at the §13.4 stored-row admission, and no path re-signs a stored row (§4.7.9, §13.12).
```

Add a `### Signing` subsection to §13.12, after the `### Identity provider` heading's subsection (`spec/13-deployment.md:495`) and before the heading that follows it.

```markdown
### Signing

| Setting | Description | Default |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PODIUM_SIGN` | Ingest signing mode: `registry-key` or `none`. Unset resolves to `registry-key`. Any other value refuses startup with `config.invalid_sign_mode`. Also settable with `podium serve --sign`, which takes precedence; no config-file key | `registry-key` |
| `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` | Path to the registry-managed Ed25519 keypair. The registry process generates it on first run when absent; a consumer process on the same machine reads it and generates nothing (§6.2, §4.7.9). Required while signing is on, unless the store is the SQLite store in the directory the default path resolves to. Environment only; no config-file key | `~/.podium/standalone/registry-signing.key` |

Every registry process serving one store resolves `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` to the same key file. The default resolves per process, under that process's home, so a registry with signing on and `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` unset refuses to start, naming `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` and `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, unless its store is the SQLite store in the directory the default path resolves to. The refusal runs before the registry generates a key or rewrites a row. A deployment running more than one replica, or whose store is not the default SQLite store, therefore supplies the path explicitly on the store's persistent storage and mounts one key, or sets `PODIUM_SIGN=none`. A registry that loses a generated key signs under a fresh one on its next start once the §13.4 rewrite has completed, and every row the lost key signed is refused at the §13.4 stored-row admission. The registry loads its key once, at start, so the repair is to stop the registry, restore the key file, and start it again. While the rewrite has not completed, that start is refused by the §13.4 generated-key rule, and because the rewrite signs the rows it writes before it decides whether to record completion, no key that still exists makes those rows verify again. Replicas signing under different keys each refuse at admission a row another replica signed, and a consumer holds one verification key, so loads fail with `materialize.signature_invalid` on some fraction of requests and succeed on the rest.

Signing is producer-side: it determines whether an artifact ingested from now on carries a §4.7.9 envelope. Ingest does not attach one to an already-stored artifact, because a stored signature is written once, at ingest. The §13.4 first-start rewrite signs each row it rewrites, including a stored row whose hash already equals the new value and that carries no signature, and leaves every other row untouched with whatever envelope it had. An untouched row is admitted or refused by the §13.4 stored-row admission checks, as any other row is. Turning signing on after the rewrite has completed leaves every row stored before it unsigned: while the record of the rewrite's completion stays in the store, no path attaches an envelope to a stored row, and an artifact gains one only when a new version of it is ingested. A start that finds that record removed runs the rewrite again, which is the limit §13.4 states for a party that can write the metadata store.

The consumer-side settings that decide whether a signature is checked are client configuration and live in §6.2 and §7.5.2. `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` is the one setting both process kinds read. The registry process writes the file it names, and a consumer running under the same user account on the same machine reads the `public:` line from it when `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is unset (§4.7.9).
```

## Spec amendment: §7.5.2 the consumer signing keys

**SPEC-6.** §7.5.2 documents no `verify_signatures` key today: the `defaults:` schema block (`spec/07-external-integration.md:331-336`) carries `registry`, `harness`, `target`, and `profile`, and the string does not occur in the file. The §13.10 bullet SPEC-5 deletes is the spec's only statement of `defaults.verify_signatures`, and the code still reads that key (`pkg/sync/config.go:43`, `cmd/podium-mcp/cliconfig.go:170`), so SPEC-6 lands that key here. Placing the key in this schema puts it under §7.5.2's per-key precedence over all three file scopes (`spec/07-external-integration.md:303-324`). The bridge reads only `<cwd>/.podium/sync.yaml` and then `~/.podium/sync.yaml` today, with no walk-up and no `sync.local.yaml` (`cmd/podium-mcp/cliconfig.go:174-196`), so CODE-5 moves its resolution of this key onto the three §7.5.2 scopes rather than SPEC-6 stating an exception, and `podium config show` gains the key so §7.7's provenance output covers it.

Add the line below to the `defaults:` schema block, after `profile:`:

```yaml
  verify_signatures: always # never | always — see §4.7.9
```

and add the paragraph below to the prose following the block:

```markdown
`defaults.verify_signatures` sets the §4.7.9 signature policy the MCP server applies when `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` is unset. The MCP server resolves it across the three file scopes by the precedence above, discovering the workspace by the same walk up from CWD, so a project-local `sync.local.yaml` value overrides a project-shared one. It is the only signing-related key in this block: verification key material is resolved from the environment and from the registry's key file, in the order §4.7.9 states, and is never written to or read from `sync.yaml`.
```

Configure the harness's MCP server entry (§6.11) with `PODIUM_REGISTRY` and `PODIUM_HARNESS`.`, stops enumerating variables and defers to the §6.11 recipe, keeping its existing `PODIUM_OVERLAY_PATH` sentence. ## Spec amendment: §13.4 stored-row admission, §13.12, §4.1, §4.7.4, §7.2, §7.6.2, and §4.7.6

**SPEC-10.** The edits below are applied in S1, with SPEC-1, SPEC-3, SPEC-4, SPEC-5, and SPEC-6.

In `spec/13-deployment.md` §13.4, replace the sentence "A consumer's load of a row still at the previous release's digest fails closed: the §6.6 delivery check refuses it with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` when no earlier step refuses it." with:

> A load of a row still at the previous release's digest fails closed at the registry's stored-row admission check.

In the same section, after the paragraph that ends "or the source registry started on the new version.", insert:

> Before the registry serves an artifact's manifest, `SKILL.md`, or bundled resources, on a `load_artifact` that returns a body and on each item of `artifacts:batchLoad`, it admits every stored row the served record is built from: the requested row and, for an artifact that declares `extends:`, each row of its chain. Admission runs these checks in order. (1) It recomputes the §4.7.6 content hash from the row's stored manifest, `SKILL.md`, and bundled-resource bytes, reading any body held in object storage within the `PODIUM_MIGRATION_OBJECT_READ_TIMEOUT` deadline (§13.12). It requires the row's bundled resources to have distinct paths, and each resource's stored per-resource content hash and size to be those of the bytes it read, whether they came from the row or from object storage. Two resources under one path, a hash that differs from the stored one, a resource whose stored content hash or size differs from its bytes, and a body object storage reports as absent are refused with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`. Any other failed or timed-out object read is refused with `registry.unavailable`. (2) It checks the row's stored parent pin against the `extends:` reference the admitted manifest declares. The pin is empty exactly when the manifest declares none, and a manifest that is empty or does not parse declares none. Otherwise the pin names the referenced parent ID and, unless the reference is a content-hash reference, a version that satisfies the reference (§4.7.6). A failed pin is refused with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`. (3) Where a §4.7.9 signer is configured, it verifies the stored signature over the stored content hash under that signer. A row with no stored signature is refused with `materialize.signature_missing`, and a row whose signature does not verify, including one signed under a key the registry no longer holds, is refused with `materialize.signature_invalid`. (4) Admission follows a row's pin only after that row has passed checks (1) through (3): it reads the pinned parent row, admits that row by these same checks, and, for a content-hash reference, requires the parent row's admitted content hash to be the one the reference names, refusing a mismatch with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`. Admission walks a chain from the requested row toward its root, and a row is refused with the code of the first check it fails in this order, so the first refusal in the walk decides the load's code. A pin that passes check (2) but names no stored row, or that returns to a row already walked, fails the load as an unresolvable `extends:` chain, as it does today. A parent row deleted after its child was ingested reaches the first case. A load whose chain holds a refused row is refused with that row's code, and the error names only the requested artifact. The registry serves the body, type, sensitivity, deprecation flag, replacement, and `audit_redact` key set a load returns from the admitted bytes, and for an `extends:` child from the §4.6 merge over the admitted rows. The version a load returns is the stored row's. A row whose manifest is empty or does not parse is served with its stored values; ingest never stores such a row. The registry serves a bundled resource the row holds inline from those admitted bytes, whatever its size, and a presigned object-storage link only for a resource whose bytes admission read from object storage (§7.2, §7.6.2). The registry keeps no admission verdict. A `load_artifact` HEAD, and a conditional `load_artifact` whose validator matches the one the registry publishes for the resolved row, return no content and are answered from the resolved row without admission. Search results, the §4.7.3 reverse index, the §4.5 domain listings, and the §13.12 object route serve no admitted content and are served without admission. Admission checks a row's content and does not check which row answers a request. The stored signature attests the content hash alone (§4.7.9), so admission does not bind a row to its tenant, artifact ID, version, or layer, to its ingest time, or to the soft-delete and deprecation state that version resolution reads. A party that can write the metadata store without the signing key can therefore withhold a row, serve an admissible row under another row's identity, or have an older admissible row served in place of a newer one. Such a party that can also cause a restart can have content it chose signed: the record that the §13.4 first-start rewrite has completed is held in the metadata store, and a start whose listener binds and that finds no record runs the rewrite, which signs with the registry's key every stored row that carries no signature and whose bytes reproduce the new digest or the previous release's digest. The start logs the rewrite's summary line, which is where that run is observable. A pin moved to another stored version of the declared parent that still satisfies an unpinned, `latest`, minor, or major reference is a choice of the same kind, because ingest resolves such a reference against the versions stored at ingest time and the bytes do not record which version it chose. An author who needs the parent fixed pins it by exact version or by content hash.

In §13.12's backend table, replace the `PODIUM_MIGRATION_OBJECT_READ_TIMEOUT` row's description, "Deadline on each object-storage read the first-start stored-value rewrite makes (§13.4), as a duration. Environment only; no config-file key.", with "Deadline on each object-storage read the first-start stored-value rewrite and the stored-row admission check make (§13.4), as a duration. Environment only; no config-file key.".

In `spec/04-artifact-model.md` §4.1, replace the bullet "**Inline cutoff (256 KB)**: below this, resource bytes are returned in the `load_artifact` response body; above, presigned URL." with:

> - **Inline cutoff (256 KB)**: below this, resource bytes are returned in the `load_artifact` response body; above, presigned URL. A resource the registry holds inline on the manifest record is returned inline at any size (§7.2).

In `spec/04-artifact-model.md` §4.7.4, replace the sentence "Deprecated artifacts return a warning when loaded and are excluded from default search results; if `replaced_by:` is set, the registry surfaces the upgrade target alongside the warning." with:

> Deprecated artifacts return a warning when loaded and are excluded from default search results. When the manifest sets `replaced_by:`, every load of the artifact returns the upgrade target, and a load of a deprecated artifact returns it alongside the warning.

In `spec/07-external-integration.md` §7.2, replace the sentences "The control plane never streams bytes for resources above the inline cutoff (256 KB). Instead, `load_artifact` returns presigned URLs that the Podium MCP server fetches directly from object storage." with:
