> The control plane never streams bytes for a resource above the inline cutoff (256 KB) that the registry holds in object storage. Instead, `load_artifact` returns presigned URLs that the Podium MCP server fetches directly from object storage. A resource the registry holds inline on the manifest record, which includes every resource of a row ingested while no object store was configured, is returned inline whatever its size, because no object exists to presign.

In `spec/07-external-integration.md` §7.6.2, replace the bullet "**Bandwidth:** large bundled resources travel via presigned URLs (§4.4) so the response body stays small; the SDK fetches resources concurrently after the response." with:

> - **Bandwidth:** a bundled resource the registry does not hold inline on the manifest record travels via a presigned URL (§4.4) so the response body stays small, and the SDK fetches those resources concurrently after the response. A resource the registry holds inline on the manifest record travels in the reference's `inline` field in place of `presigned_url`, including when a copy of it also exists in object storage, base64-encoded with `inline_base64: true` when its bytes are not valid UTF-8. The registry holds inline every resource at or below the §4.1 inline cutoff, and every resource of a row ingested while no object store was configured (§7.2), so every link it serves names an object the §13.4 stored-row admission read.

In `spec/04-artifact-model.md` §4.7.6, replace the sentence "The registry computes the digest at ingest, the consumer recomputes it at the §6.6 delivery check, and a workspace overlay (§6.4) computes it from the overlay package's bytes." with:

> The registry computes the digest at ingest and recomputes it from a stored row's bytes at the §13.4 first-start rewrite and at the §13.4 stored-row admission check, the consumer recomputes it at the §6.6 delivery check, and a workspace overlay (§6.4) computes it from the overlay package's bytes.

TEST-14 cites §13.4, §4.7.4, §7.2, and §7.6.2. Proposal 0027's SPEC-6 anchors on the §4.7.6 sentence as this edit leaves it.

## Proposed solution

### CODE-1: split `EnforceVerification` into two axes

The item edits `pkg/sign/sign.go`. The exported signature and name are unchanged, so every caller compiles untouched. Compiling is not passing: `test/conformance/reference_visibility_test.go` and `pkg/registry/core/extends_served_record_test.go` sign with `sign.Noop{}` and then assert that `EnforceVerification` under `PolicyAlways` returns nil, which CODE-2 turns into a failure. TEST-1 carries their disposition. `needsVerification` is deleted; leaving both predicates is how they drift.

`pkg/sign/sign.go` is in AUDIT-1's set and its statements of the single-axis model are rewritten under this item, on the assignment S9.5 records. The rewritten comments state the two axes and cite §4.7.9 rather than §6.2, whose default SPEC-3 changes.

The code below is what CODE-1 lands. It keeps `medium-and-above` and the `sensitivity` parameter, because the consumer fallback still names that value until CODE-5 flips it, and CODE-13 deletes both beside that flip.

```go
// EnforceVerification applies policy to one served artifact.
//
// Two axes, deliberately separated. A signature that is present is always
// verified under any policy other than never, because the sensitivity the
// policy would otherwise gate on is reported by the same party that supplies
// the signature, and for a child declaring extends: (§4.6) no attested source
// for it exists: the merge takes the most-restrictive value
// (pkg/manifest/merge.go) while the content hash covers only the child's
// pre-merge bytes. Whether a missing signature aborts the load is the axis the
// policy governs, and only `always` resists a registry that serves an unsigned
// forgery.
//
// spec: §4.7.9, §6.6 step 2.
func EnforceVerification(ctx context.Context, policy VerificationPolicy, provider Provider, sensitivity manifest.Sensitivity, contentHash, signature string) error {
	if policy == PolicyNever {
		return nil
	}
	if signature == "" {
		if !requiresSignature(policy, sensitivity) {
			return nil
		}
		return fmt.Errorf("%w: sensitivity %q requires a signature", ErrSignatureMissing, sensitivity)
	}
	return provider.Verify(ctx, contentHash, signature)
}

// requiresSignature reports whether a missing signature is a refusal. An
// unrecognized policy fails closed; loadConfig refuses such a value at startup
// (§6.2), so this is defense in depth for a direct constructor.
func requiresSignature(policy VerificationPolicy, s manifest.Sensitivity) bool {
	switch policy {
	case PolicyAlways:
		return true
	case PolicyMediumAndAbove:
		return s == manifest.SensitivityMedium || s == manifest.SensitivityHigh
	case PolicyNever:
		return false
	default:
		return true
	}
}
```

### CODE-2: `Noop.Verify` refuses

The item edits `pkg/sign/sign.go`. `Noop.Sign` and `Noop.ID` are unchanged, and the type stays selectable so `podium sign` keeps its scaffolding value and the SPI keeps a trivially constructible implementation. The type's doc comment loses the sentence calling it a safe default in standalone deployments, which stops being true once any policy above `never` is in force.

```go
// Verify always refuses. The value Sign produces is "noop:" + contentHash, and
// contentHash is served in the clear on every load_artifact response, so
// accepting it would let any party mint a passing signature for any artifact.
// A deployment that does not verify says so with PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never
// (§6.2); it does not say so by configuring a provider that accepts a public
// value.
func (Noop) Verify(_ context.Context, _, _ string) error {
	return fmt.Errorf("%w: the noop provider does not verify; set PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER to registry-managed or sigstore-keyless, or set PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never", ErrSignatureInvalid)
}
```

### CODE-3: the read event below the verification, and the missing-signature code

The item edits `deliverLoadArtifact` in `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go`. Today the body runs `fetchManifestBody`, `auditLoadArtifact`, `enforceSignaturePolicy`, `enforceSandboxPolicy`, `enforceRuntimePolicy`, `decodeInlineResources`, `fetchLargeResources`, `verifyContentHash`, then the cache writes (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:1567`, `:1574`, `:1578`, `:1581`, `:1587`, `:1594`, `:1600`, `:1606`). The read event and both §4.4.1 gates read `resp.Frontmatter` before anything has bound it to the served digest, and the read event applies the manifest's `audit_redact` directive from those bytes.

After this item the body runs `verifyServedArtifact` (CODE-12), `auditLoadArtifact`, `enforceSandboxPolicy`, `enforceRuntimePolicy`, then the cache writes and materialization. `verifyServedArtifact` runs `fetchManifestBody`, `decodeInlineResources`, `fetchLargeResources`, `enforceSignaturePolicy` with the two-code mapping below, and then `verifyContentHash`, in that order, so a record whose signature fails reports the signature code whatever its bytes, and the recomputation runs only over a record the policy passed. The large-resource fetch stays where §6.6 step 1 puts it, above the verification: `fetchLargeResources` already refuses a body that does not hash to its link's content hash (`cmd/podium-mcp/large_resources.go:95-101`).

A load the verification refuses records no local `artifact.loaded` event, while a load one of the two §4.4.1 gates refuses still records one. The read event also moves below the inline-resource decode and the large-resource fetch, so a load that fails either records nothing locally where today it records the event, and during an object-storage outage the consumer's local sink carries nothing for a failed large-resource load, while the registry's own stream still records the call. TEST-2 pins each of these classes. Proposal 0027, landing after this proposal, replaces `verifyContentHash` inside `verifyServedArtifact` with its delivery-record check and retargets the policy onto its `delivery_signature`; the order this item sets is the order 0027 keeps.

The doc comment on `deliverLoadArtifact` states the order and why it is normative, citing §6.6 step 2 and §4.7.9. The moved gates keep their error codes. `loadArtifactFromOverlay` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:1842`) is a separate delivery path that never reaches this function; it carries no registry signature, SPEC-1 states that the policy does not apply to it, and it is unchanged.

`enforceSignaturePolicy`'s call site stops prefixing every failure with one code:

```go
if err := s.enforceSignaturePolicy(resp); err != nil {
	// §6.10: a missing required signature and a signature that does not
	// validate are separate codes with separate operator remedies. Both were
	// served as materialize.signature_invalid, so the documented
	// materialize.signature_missing code never reached a client.
	if errors.Is(err, sign.ErrSignatureMissing) {
		return errorResult("materialize.signature_missing: " + err.Error())
	}
	return errorResult("materialize.signature_invalid: " + err.Error())
}
```

### CODE-4: the registry signs by default

The item edits `internal/serverboot/signing.go`, `internal/serverboot/serverboot.go`, `internal/serverboot/rehash.go`, and `cmd/podium/serve.go`. `registrySignerFor` treats an empty mode as `registry-key` and returns `(nil, nil)` only for `none`. The startup validation that returns `config.invalid_sign_mode` accepts the empty string, `registry-key`, and `none`, and refuses any other value; the empty string resolves to `registry-key` rather than being rejected, so a zero-configuration start signs. Its error text (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:2401`) names both accepted values. `cmd/podium/serve.go`'s `--sign` help text names both values.

0026 is implemented, and the mode reading now lives in `registrySigningEnabled` (`internal/serverboot/signing.go`), which `registrySignerFor` and the generated-key refusal `refuseGeneratedSigningKey` (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go`) both call. The change lands in that one function: an empty mode and `registry-key` enable signing, and `none` disables it, so ingest, the first-start rewrite, and both refusals read the mode identically. `registrySignerFor` already returns the whole `sign.Provider`. The rewrite's refusal to start when a row it would rewrite is signed and no signer is configured is `refuseStrandedSignature` (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:294-307`), keyed on a nil provider, which `none` still produces, so no logic in the pass changes. Its error string is the one thing in the pass this item edits: it tells the operator to "set PODIUM_SIGN=registry-key and point PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH at the key that signed them" (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:307`), while under this proposal the refusal is reachable only under `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, so the trailing clause becomes "remove PODIUM_SIGN=none and point PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH at the key that signed them", which is what TEST-4's boot refusal case asserts. The generated-key refusal needs no edit, and the default now reaches it: a store holding signed rows whose key file is absent at a start before the rewrite completes is refused with no signing configuration set, which the edge-case table records. `refuseGeneratedSigningKey` keys on the key file's absence (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:516`), so a key file that is present but is not the key that signed the stored rows passes the guard; the edge-case table records what happens then, and TEST-4 pins it. Extending the guard to verify a stored signature under the present key is not staged here, because the pass already classifies such a row `classSignatureUnverified` and the question of whether that class should hold the start belongs with the rotation work OQ-2 carries.

**Persistence refusal.** The default key path resolves under the registry process's home, and a store need not live there, so a key the loader generates can be lost while the rows it signed survive, and no path re-signs them. `refuseUnpersistedSigningKey(cfg *Config) error`, new in `internal/serverboot/signing.go`, refuses that start. It returns nil when `registrySigningEnabled(cfg.signMode)` is false, when `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` is set, when `cfg.storeType` is `memory`, which persists nothing and so strands no signature, and when `cfg.storeType` is `sqlite` and `filepath.Dir(filepath.Clean(cfg.sqlitePath))` equals `filepath.Dir` of `registrySigningKeyPath("")`, which is `<home>/.podium/standalone`. `LoadConfig` resolves an unset `PODIUM_SQLITE_PATH` to `<home>/.podium/standalone/podium.db` (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:2345-2349`), so a zero-configuration standalone start passes and signs with no operator action. Every other case returns an error naming the store backend, the SQLite path when the backend is `sqlite`, `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, and `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, and never a DSN: `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH is unset, so the registry signing key would be generated under the process's home, outside the directory that holds the store; set PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH to a file on the store's persistent storage, or set PODIUM_SIGN=none`. An unresolvable home returns the error `registrySigningKeyPath` returns. `run` calls it immediately before `refuseGeneratedSigningKey` (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:895`), whatever the bind outcome, so a refused start writes no key and rewrites no row.

- **State it reads.** It reads `cfg.signMode`, `cfg.storeType`, `cfg.sqlitePath`, the `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` environment variable, and the home `registrySigningKeyPath` resolves. It writes nothing and caches nothing.
- **Every site that sets and clears that state.** `LoadConfig` sets the three fields from `PODIUM_SIGN` or `--sign`, `PODIUM_REGISTRY_STORE`, and `PODIUM_SQLITE_PATH` or their `registry.yaml` keys. The operator, the Helm chart (CODE-8), and the test harnesses set `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`; the compose stack sets `PODIUM_SIGN=none` (CODE-11).
- **Callers and interfaces.** `run` alone calls it. No interface changes.
- **When it does not fire.** It does not fire for a standalone SQLite store in its default directory, a memory store, a set key path, or signing off. The memory arm is a code-level exemption that SPEC-5's staged §13.12 text does not state, because §13.12 selects the store from `postgres` and `sqlite` alone (`spec/13-deployment.md:396`) and `openStore` calls `memory` an undocumented test affordance (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:2496-2500`), so the spec names only the SQLite exemption and names no store it does not define. A SQLite store elsewhere that happens to share a volume with the home is refused anyway, because the predicate reads paths rather than volumes; the operator sets the key path.
- **Test.** TEST-4's persistence cases cover it.

0026's accepted-failure text for a `signature_unverified` row states that such a row "loads where the policy does not cover the artifact's sensitivity" (`CHANGELOG.md:76-77`). CODE-1 and CODE-14, rather than the reorder, falsify it: CODE-1 verifies a present signature under every policy other than `never` with no sensitivity consulted, and on a signing registry CODE-14's admission refuses such a row before any consumer check runs, under every consumer policy, `never` included, with the code CODE-14's class table gives the `signature_unverified` class. That sentence closes the class-to-code paragraph at `CHANGELOG.md:68-77`, whose earlier sentences CODE-14's admission falsifies for every untouched row that fails a §13.4 check, because the registry refuses each such row with the code CODE-14's class table gives whatever the consumer's policy, and DOC-1 stages the whole paragraph's replacement.

No new key material is written: `loadOrGenerateRegistrySigner` already generates the keypair on first run at `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, in the one file `readOrCreateEd25519` writes, carrying a `private:` line and a `public:` line (`internal/serverboot/audit_anchor.go:182-189`). That file is what CODE-10's resolver and readers give the consumer, on the path resolution the registry itself uses. No `.pub` sibling is written, and `readOrCreateEd25519` stays shared with the §8.3 audit anchor unchanged.

### CODE-5: the consumer defaults, and one verification-material resolution

The item edits `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go`, `cmd/podium-mcp/cliconfig.go`, `cmd/podium/sign.go`, and `cmd/podium/clientconfig.go`.

- `envDefault("PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER", "noop")` becomes `"registry-managed"` at all three sites (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:286`, `cmd/podium/sign.go:27`, and `cmd/podium/sign.go:83`), and the `case "", "noop"` arm splits in both switches so that an empty name is no longer an alias for `noop`. `buildSignatureProvider("")` falls to the default arm and returns the existing `unknown PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER: ` error (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2209`), and `loadSignatureProvider("", use)` returns the CLI's `unknown signature provider: ` error for either `keyUse` (`cmd/podium/sign.go:183`). Refusing is the reading consistent with `loadConfig` refusing an unrecognized provider: after the default flip an empty name can no longer arrive from configuration, so it names no provider rather than selecting one. `TestBuildSignatureProvider` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main_helpers_test.go:475-481`) asserts today that an empty name constructs, and TEST-5 carries its disposition. `cmd/podium/sign.go` and `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go` are in AUDIT-1's set, and every statement in them that names `noop` the default is rewritten under this item on the S9.5 assignment. The rewritten text names `registry-managed` and states where each half of the CLI's key material resolves.
- The `verifyPolicy` fallback in `loadConfig` becomes `sign.PolicyAlways`. The `sync.yaml` resolution above it moves onto the three §7.5.2 scopes, as the next bullet but one states, and an operator-set `never` in any of them still wins over the fallback.
- `loadConfig` refuses an unrecognized `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER` with `config.invalid`, beside the existing refusals for an unknown policy, identity provider, cache mode, and harness. The check is row 1 of the table below, it runs under every policy including `never`, and it runs on the name as `applyFlagsAndConfig` left it (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:317`, `cmd/podium-mcp/cliconfig.go:123-124`), so a flag-set or config-set name is checked too.
- `defaults.verify_signatures` resolves across the §7.5.2 file scopes, and the bridge announces a stale `never` that came from a file. `verifySignaturesFromSyncYAML` (`cmd/podium-mcp/cliconfig.go:174-186`) returns the resolved value alone today and consults `<cwd>/.podium/sync.yaml` and then `~/.podium/sync.yaml` through `verifySignaturesFromWorkspace` (`:190-196`), with no walk-up, no `sync.local.yaml`, and no report of which file answered. SPEC-6 places the key under §7.5.2's per-key precedence, so the function becomes `verifySignaturesFromSyncYAML() (value, path string)` over the three scopes: it discovers the workspace with `synccfg.DiscoverWorkspace(cwd)` (`pkg/sync/resolve.go:56`), the walk-up §7.5.2 states, and reads `<workspace>/.podium/sync.local.yaml`, then `<workspace>/.podium/sync.yaml`, then `<home>/.podium/sync.yaml` through `synccfg.ReadConfigFile(path)` (`pkg/sync/resolve.go:73`), returning the first non-empty `Defaults.VerifySignatures` with the path of the file that carried it, and two empty strings when no scope sets it. With no workspace found only the home file is read, which is what `synccfg.LoadMergedConfig` does (`pkg/sync/resolve.go:94-121`). A file that is absent or does not parse contributes nothing, as today. `verifySignaturesFromWorkspace` is deleted, because nothing else calls it. `registryFromSyncYAML` (`cmd/podium-mcp/cliconfig.go:145`) keeps its present two-scope order: its resolution is not a signing surface and is outside this proposal. `LoadMergedConfig` is not reused, because it merges no `VerifySignatures` (`mergeDefaults`, `pkg/sync/resolve.go:153-169`) and reports no per-key scope, which the warning and CODE-13's error need. At the call site in `loadConfig` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:344-350`), where `c.verifyPolicy` is still empty because no environment value, flag, or config value set it, a resolved value of `never` with a non-empty path writes one line to stderr with `fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, ...)`, naming the path and `defaults.verify_signatures` as the key to remove. That is the form the cache-disabled warning a few lines below already takes (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:363`), and it is what the unit companion's `captureStderr` helper can read: the helper swaps the `os.Stderr` variable, while the `log` package's default logger holds the `*os.File` it was constructed with at package init, so a `log.Printf` line would reach the original descriptor and never the pipe. The line is informational: the resolved policy stays `never`, no verification material is resolved under `never`, and startup proceeds.
- `podium config show` reports the key with the rest of the `defaults:` block. `defaultsKeys` (`cmd/podium/clientconfig.go:25`) gains `verify_signatures` after `profile`, and `defaultsField` (`:64-79`) gains the `case "verify_signatures": return cfg.Defaults.VerifySignatures` arm, so the merged view and `--explain verify_signatures` name the scope that supplies the value the bridge resolves. `loadClientScopes` (`:31-60`) already reads the same three files with the same walk-up, so the command and the bridge agree on the winning file. `resolveDefaults`, the human view, and `clientPayload` iterate `defaultsKeys` and need no other edit. The existing `cmd/podium/clientconfig_test.go` cases set no `verify_signatures` and stay green. TEST-5 carries the new case.
- `config` gains a `verifier sign.Provider` field beside `verifyPolicy` and `signatureProvider` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:209-210`), and `loadConfig` sets it by calling one resolver once, after the flags and the config file have been applied, after the policy has resolved, and after the existing validations. `enforceSignaturePolicy` reads `s.cfg.verifier` and constructs nothing (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2007-2019`). There is no capability predicate beside the resolver: the resolution either produces a verifier or returns the error, and that error is the startup refusal.

  The resolver is `resolveVerifier(policy sign.VerificationPolicy, name string) (sign.Provider, error)`, new in `cmd/podium-mcp`. It carries the policy axis, which is what the existing `buildSignatureProvider(name string) (sign.Provider, error)` cannot: that function takes the provider name alone and returns `sign.Noop{}` with a nil error for `noop` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2178-2181`), so no call to it can refuse a `noop` bridge under an enforcing policy. `resolveVerifier` is the whole resolution and `buildSignatureProvider` is its provider-construction half, called by it and by nothing else in the bridge. It is `resolveVerifier` that returns each refusal the table states, including the `config.signature_provider_unavailable` of the `noop` row.

  The resolution is one table over the resolved policy, the provider name, and the material the named provider needs. It is normative here. The edge-case rows, TEST-5, and the other proposal deliverables cite a cell rather than restate the rule; the spec sections state the rule in their own terms, because a spec sentence cannot cite a proposal deliverable, and §6.9 (SPEC-4) is the spec section that carries the refusal in full, which §4.7.9 and §6.2 cite.

  | Policy | Provider name | Material | `resolveVerifier` |
  |:--|:--|:--|:--|
  | any | not `noop`, `registry-managed`, or `sigstore-keyless`, including empty | not read | refuses with `config.invalid` |
  | `never` | recognized | not read | returns; `verifier` is nil and nothing is verified |
  | above `never` | `noop` | none exists | refuses with `config.signature_provider_unavailable`, before `buildSignatureProvider` is reached |
  | above `never` | `registry-managed` | `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is set and decodes to an Ed25519 public key; the key file is not read | returns; `verifier` is that `RegistryManagedKey` |
  | above `never` | `registry-managed` | `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is set and does not decode to an Ed25519 public key; the key file is not read | refuses with `config.signature_provider_unavailable`, naming the variable |
  | above `never` | `registry-managed` | `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is unset, and the `public:` line at `sign.KeyFilePath(os.Getenv("PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH"))` resolves | returns; `verifier` is that `RegistryManagedKey` |
  | above `never` | `registry-managed` | `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is unset, and the key file does not resolve a usable key (the unresolved row) | refuses with `config.signature_provider_unavailable`, naming both sources |
  | above `never` | `sigstore-keyless` | `PODIUM_SIGSTORE_TRUST_ROOT_PEM_FILE` names a readable file | returns; `verifier` is that `SigstoreKeyless` |
  | above `never` | `sigstore-keyless` | that variable is unset or names no readable file | refuses with `config.signature_provider_unavailable`, naming the variable |

  A set variable is authoritative, which is the rule today's code already applies (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2198-2203` returns `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY: <error>` for a malformed value and reads no file). A set value that is undecodable or not `ed25519.PublicKeySize` bytes refuses the start and is never passed over for the key file, so a mistyped remote key on a machine that once ran a standalone registry is a startup refusal naming the variable rather than a bridge that starts on the stale local key and refuses every load. "The key file does not resolve" is one outcome with one code whatever the cause: the file absent, unreadable, carrying no `public:` line, carrying an undecodable value, carrying a value that is not `ed25519.PublicKeySize` bytes, or a home `os.UserHomeDir` cannot resolve. A malformed value, in the variable or in the file, gets no second code, because the operator's repair is the same, and `config.invalid` stays the code for an unrecognized provider *name*. The message names each source tried and what was wrong with it.

  The two material rows are `buildSignatureProvider`'s own: it stops discarding the trust-root read error (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2183`, `root, _ := os.ReadFile(...)`) and returns it, which is what makes the last row a refusal rather than a per-load `materialize.signature_invalid: no trust root configured` out of `pkg/sign/sigstore.go:168-169`, and it resolves the `registry-managed` key on the order below and returns an error when the variable is set and malformed, or is unset while the key file does not resolve. Its own contract therefore changes: for `sigstore-keyless` and `registry-managed` it returns an error where it returns a nil error today, which TEST-5 dispositions.

Key resolution for `registry-managed` is the §4.7.9 order and nothing else: `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` through `sign.PublicKeyFromBase64` when the variable is set, and only when it is unset `sign.KeyFilePath(os.Getenv("PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH"))` through `sign.PublicKeyFromKeyFile` (CODE-10). `buildSignatureProvider` performs that resolution and returns an error when the variable is set and malformed, or is unset while the key file does not resolve, and `resolveVerifier` calls it once, from `loadConfig`. No `sync.yaml` scope is consulted for key material.

- **State it reads and writes.** It reads the resolved policy, the provider name, and the environment and file the table names. It writes `config.verifier` once. Nothing reads the signing environment after `loadConfig` returns, so a key file replaced while the bridge runs does not change what that process verifies with, and the operator restarts the bridge.
- **Every site that sets and clears that state.** `config.verifier` is set by `loadConfig` alone (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:251`), which `main` alone calls (`:127`). Nothing clears it. `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` and `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` are set by the operator, the §6.11 recipes (SPEC-8), and the Helm chart (CODE-8). The file those name is written by `readOrCreateEd25519` alone (`internal/serverboot/audit_anchor.go:167-190`, the write itself at `:182-189`) and nothing in the product deletes it.
- **Callers and interfaces.** `enforceSignaturePolicy` is the only reader of `config.verifier` and the only `sign.EnforceVerification` caller in `cmd/podium-mcp` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2007-2019`). `sign.Provider` is untouched, so `Noop`, `RegistryManagedKey`, `SigstoreKeyless`, and the `pkg/audit` test fake (`pkg/audit/scheduler_test.go:119`) satisfy it unchanged.
- **When it does not fire.** A bridge that started holds a verifier, so no load fails for absent material. The one per-load key-material failure that remains is `materialize.signature_invalid` from a key that resolved and did not sign the row. A nil `verifier` is reachable only under `never`, where `sign.EnforceVerification` returns before touching the provider (`pkg/sign/sign.go:100-103`); CODE-1's rewrite keeps that ordering, which is an obligation on CODE-1 rather than a guard here.
- **In-process `config` constructions.** A test that builds `&config{}` directly bypasses `loadConfig` and sets no verifier. Every such construction that reaches `enforceSignaturePolicy` or `deliverLoadArtifact` under a policy above `never` sets `verifier` explicitly. On the current tree that is `cmd/podium-mcp/cache_mode_test.go:213-217`, which supplies its key through `t.Setenv("PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY", ...)` at `:218` and instead sets `verifier: sign.RegistryManagedKey{PublicKey: pub}`. `cmd/podium-mcp/cache_mode_test.go:257-261` needs no verifier, because its subject is a missing signature, which `sign.EnforceVerification` answers before the provider is used (`pkg/sign/sign.go:104-106`). The third such construction above `never` is `TestEnforceSignaturePolicy_BadProviderConfig` (`cmd/podium-mcp/policy_test.go:11-20`), which builds `&config{signatureProvider: "bogus", verifyPolicy: sign.PolicyAlways}` and asserts that `enforceSignaturePolicy` bubbles up the provider-construction error. That subject moves to `loadConfig`: the unrecognized-name assertion becomes row 1 of `TestLoadConfig_VerifierResolution`, and the case is deleted rather than kept, because after this change `enforceSignaturePolicy` constructs nothing and the case would pass on the missing-signature answer while asserting nothing about the provider, which is the vacuous-negative-control failure TEST-9's tamper controls are listed to avoid. The fourth is the shared helper `overlayTestServer` (`cmd/podium-mcp/overlay_signature_test.go:15-27`), which builds `&config{harness: "none", verifyPolicy: policy, signatureProvider: "noop"}` and is called above `never` at `:44`, `:78`, `:106`, and `:138`. It needs no `verifier`: three of those callers reach `loadArtifactFromOverlay`, which never calls `enforceSignaturePolicy`, and the fourth, `TestDeliverLoadArtifact_PolicyAlwaysRejectsUnsigned` at `:78`, passes an empty `Signature` (`:86`) that `sign.EnforceVerification` answers before the provider is used. That enumeration is of the tree before S10, and S11 adds constructions of its own, so the S14 applier re-derives the set over the tree as it stands at S14 rather than applying the constructions named above alone. The rule is: a construction needs `verifier` exactly when its policy is above `never` and a response it delivers carries a non-empty `signature`, because `sign.EnforceVerification` answers an empty signature before the provider is used. The finder is `grep -n '&config{' cmd/podium-mcp/*_test.go`, read against that rule. Before S14 such a construction supplies its key the only way the tree then offers, `signatureProvider: "registry-managed"` with `t.Setenv("PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY", ...)`, which `enforceSignaturePolicy` reads per load (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2007-2019`); in the S14 commit it sets `verifier: sign.RegistryManagedKey{PublicKey: pub}` instead and drops the `t.Setenv`. Beyond the constructions named above, the ones this proposal itself adds that the rule selects are TEST-2's cases whose response carries a signature under a policy above `never` (S11). With no `verifier` at S14, each would call `Verify` on a nil interface and panic the `cmd/podium-mcp` test binary, which is what observes an omission. Every construction the finder returns that the rule does not select sets `sign.PolicyNever`, delivers no signature, or reaches neither function, and CODE-12's `resources_test.go` disposition is what puts the one mirror construction that reaches `enforceSignaturePolicy` in the `sign.PolicyNever` camp.
- **Test.** `TestLoadConfig_VerifierResolution` in `cmd/podium-mcp/config_env_test.go` is table-driven with one case per row above, asserting the returned error's code or the resolved `verifier`'s `ID()`. The startup refusal itself runs in a spawned binary, so `TestSignedArtifact_NoVerifyKeyRefusesUnderTheDefaults` in `test/e2e/` pins the observable exit under `GOCOVERDIR`, per `.claude/rules/test-coverage.md`.

`cmd/podium/sign.go` resolves key material on the same order. `loadSignatureProvider("registry-managed")` returns a bare `sign.RegistryManagedKey{}` today (`cmd/podium/sign.go:176-181`), which fails every call with `ErrRegistryManagedUnavailable`, so with the default flipped no `podium verify` invocation could succeed. `signCmd` (`cmd/podium/sign.go:55`) and `verifyCmd` (`:121`) both build their provider through that one function, and the two commands need different halves under different rules, so the function gains a second parameter naming the half its caller needs: `loadSignatureProvider(name string, use keyUse) (sign.Provider, error)`, where `keyUse` is a new unexported type in `cmd/podium/sign.go` with the two constants `keyForSign` and `keyForVerify`. `signCmd` passes `keyForSign` and `verifyCmd` passes `keyForVerify`. The `noop` and `sigstore-keyless` arms ignore the parameter. The `registry-managed` arm resolves exactly one half:

- **`keyForVerify`.** The public half, on the authoritative-variable rule the bridge applies: `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` through `sign.PublicKeyFromBase64` when the variable is set, and only when it is unset `sign.KeyFilePath(os.Getenv("PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH"))` through `sign.PublicKeyFromKeyFile`. A set variable that does not decode is an error naming it, and the key file is not read. The private half is not read, so a consumer machine that sets `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` and has no key file verifies. The arm returns `sign.RegistryManagedKey{PublicKey: pub}`.
- **`keyForSign`.** The private half alone, from `sign.KeyFilePath(os.Getenv("PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH"))` through `sign.PrivateKeyFromKeyFile`, because the registry's key file carries a `private:` line beside its `public:` one (`internal/serverboot/audit_anchor.go:182-189`). `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is not read, whatever it holds, because it carries a public key alone, which is the rule SPEC-3's §6.2 `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` row states. The arm returns `sign.RegistryManagedKey{PrivateKey: priv}`.

A failed resolution in either arm returns an error whose text leads with `config.signature_provider_unavailable: ` and names each source tried and the key path, and both commands print it through their existing `error: %v` branch and exit 1 (`cmd/podium/sign.go:55-58`, `:121-124`) before any `Sign` or `Verify` call. The error is returned by the loader rather than left to `RegistryManagedKey.Sign`, because that method's `ErrRegistryManagedUnavailable` prints its `Message` alone (`pkg/sign/registry_managed.go:62`, `pkg/spi/errors.go:33`), so `signCmd`'s `sign failed: %v` line (`cmd/podium/sign.go:62`) would never name the code SPEC-1 requires. On a zero-configuration standalone machine both commands resolve with no flags and no variables, because `sign.KeyFilePath` falls back to the same `~/.podium/standalone/registry-signing.key` the registry generated, and the file's `0o600` mode is what confines that resolution to the user account the registry runs as.

- **State it reads.** `keyForVerify` reads `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, and `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` with the file it names only when that variable is unset. `keyForSign` reads `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` and the file it names. Nothing is cached across invocations: the CLI is one process per invocation.
- **Every site that sets and clears that state.** `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is set by the operator and by the §6.11 recipes (SPEC-8). `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` is set by the operator and by the Helm chart (CODE-8). The file it names is written by `readOrCreateEd25519` alone, on the registry's first start with signing on, and nothing in the product deletes it. No configuration file participates. The `keyUse` value is set by the caller's command alone.
- **Callers and interfaces.** `signCmd` and `verifyCmd` are the production callers, and `TestLoadSignatureProvider` (`cmd/podium/cli_helpers_test.go:118`) is the only other; TEST-9 gives it the new argument. `sign.Provider` is untouched, so no type satisfying it changes.
- **When it does not fire.** For `podium verify`, the variable is set and malformed, or is unset and the key file resolves no usable public key. For `podium sign`, the key file resolves no usable private key, whatever `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` holds. In each case the CLI exits 1 with the `config.signature_provider_unavailable` error rather than falling back to a provider that does not verify. A `podium sign` invocation on a machine whose registry runs under `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, and whose home carries no key file from an earlier start with signing on, has no keypair file to read and takes that exit.
- **Test.** TEST-9 adds the new cases: `podium sign` with no flags on a `HOME` whose standalone registry generated the key file produces an envelope that `podium verify` with no flags accepts; `podium verify` with a malformed `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` on a `HOME` whose key file resolves exits non-zero naming `config.signature_provider_unavailable` and the variable; `podium sign` with a malformed `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` on a `HOME` whose key file resolves succeeds; `podium sign` with a valid `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` and no key file exits non-zero naming `config.signature_provider_unavailable` and the key path; and `podium verify` with a valid `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` and no key file succeeds.

The stale-`never` warning is specified on the same terms:

- **State it reads.** It reads `c.verifyPolicy` as it stands after the environment, the flag, and the config file have been applied, and the value and path `verifySignaturesFromSyncYAML` returns. It writes no state and caches nothing.
- **Every site that sets and clears that state.** `defaults.verify_signatures` is written into any of the three §7.5.2 files by an operator, and, on a machine that started a registry before this release, into `~/.podium/sync.yaml` by the bootstrap CODE-6 stops writing it from. Nothing in the product clears it, which is why the line is a warning rather than a repair. `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` is set by the operator and by the §6.11 recipes.
- **Callers and interfaces.** `loadConfig` alone calls `verifySignaturesFromSyncYAML`, an unexported function in `cmd/podium-mcp` that satisfies no interface, so the two-value return reaches no other caller. `verifySignaturesFromWorkspace` has no caller after the change and is deleted.
- **When it does not fire.** An environment-set `never` logs nothing, because the file did not supply it and there is no file to name. A policy above `never` logs nothing. A run in which no §7.5.2 scope sets the key logs nothing, because the path is empty. In each of these cases the only observable difference from a run that logs is the absent line.
- **Test.** TEST-6's `TestStandaloneBootstrap_PreservesAnExistingNever` reads the line off the bridge's stderr, and TEST-5 adds the unit companion in `cmd/podium-mcp/config_env_test.go` beside the existing `sync.yaml`-precedence cases: a home-global `verify_signatures: never` with `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` unset logs the line naming that file, and the same file with `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` set logs nothing. Two further cases pin the §7.5.2 scopes. In the first, the working directory is a subdirectory of a workspace whose `.podium/sync.yaml` carries `verify_signatures: never` and whose `.podium/sync.local.yaml` carries `always`, with verification material set; `loadConfig` resolves `always`, which pins that `sync.local.yaml` outranks `sync.yaml`, because a resolution that reads the workspace's `sync.yaml` and not its `sync.local.yaml` resolves `never`. That case cannot pin the walk-up, because a resolution that skips it reads no workspace file and resolves the S14 `always` fallback. In the second, the working directory is the same subdirectory and the same workspace carries `never` in `sync.local.yaml` alone, with `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` unset; `loadConfig` resolves `never` and the warning names the `sync.local.yaml` path. A resolution that skips the walk-up reads no workspace file from the subdirectory, resolves the `always` fallback, and logs nothing, so the second case is the one that pins `synccfg.DiscoverWorkspace`. `cmd/podium/clientconfig_test.go` gains `TestConfigClientShow_ExplainVerifySignatures`, in the form of `TestConfigClientShow_Explain` (`cmd/podium/clientconfig_test.go:113`): a home file carrying `never` and a workspace `sync.local.yaml` carrying `always`, asserting that `--explain verify_signatures` resolves `always` from the project-local scope. Each carries `// Spec: §7.5.2`. The warning's positive case and both scope cases clear the variable with `t.Setenv("PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES", "")` beside `hermetic(t)`, as `TestLoadConfig_VerifySignaturesDefaultsToMediumAndAbove` already does (`cmd/podium-mcp/config_env_test.go:305`), because TEST-5 gives `hermetic` a `never` of its own and the `sync.yaml` read is reached only while `c.verifyPolicy` is empty. The file already carries a stderr capture helper for this purpose, `captureStderr` (`cmd/podium-mcp/config_env_test.go:55`), whose doc comment records that a case using it must not be parallel.

### CODE-6: the standalone bootstrap stops relaxing the policy

The item edits `internal/serverboot/standalone_bootstrap.go` and `pkg/sync/config.go`.

The bootstrapped `sync.yaml` body loses its policy line and gains nothing:

```go
syncBody := []byte("defaults:\n  registry: " + cfg.publicURL + "\n")
```

That is the whole change to the write. No key material and no key path is written, under any signing mode, so `bootstrapStandaloneFiles` keeps its existing contract: it writes a file only when one is absent, and it reads no existing file. `writeFileIfAbsent` is unchanged, and no helper is added beside it.

First run under the default is green because the registry signs and the consumer resolves the registry's own key file through `sign.KeyFilePath` (CODE-10), rather than because verification was switched off. A machine whose `~/.podium/sync.yaml` `podium init --global` wrote is green for the same reason and needs no rewrite of that file. Deleting the `verify_signatures` line also removes the relaxation from the `hasExplicitServerConfig` branch, so no rescoping of `bootstrapStandaloneFiles` is needed.

Under `PODIUM_SIGN=none` the registry mints no envelope and generates no key file. The consumer on that machine takes CODE-5's table unchanged, on whether a key file from an earlier default-mode start is present. Where none is present nothing resolves and the bridge is refused. Where one is present the stale key resolves, the bridge starts, and each unsigned row is refused with `materialize.signature_missing`. Both arms are fail-closed, TEST-6's `PODIUM_SIGN=none` case pins both, and the operator who turns signing off sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` on that machine's bridge.

`pkg/sync.Defaults.VerifySignatures` keeps its field and gains no sibling. `pkg/sync/config.go` is in AUDIT-1's set, and the statements in it that describe the standalone `never` write are rewritten under this item on the S9.5 assignment: the key is described as operator-set, citing §7.5.2 and §6.2 rather than §13.10, whose relaxation bullet SPEC-5 deletes.

- **State it reads.** It reads `cfg.publicURL` and the presence of `~/.podium/sync.yaml`, unchanged from today.
- **Every site that sets and clears that state.** The sites are unchanged: `bootstrapStandaloneFiles` writes the file when absent and never rewrites one.
- **When it does not fire.** An existing file is left byte-for-byte as it was, including an existing `verify_signatures: never`. The consumer on such a machine keeps verifying nothing, which CODE-5's startup warning is the only mechanism that reaches.
- **Callers and interfaces.** `standaloneStartup` alone calls it, and no interface changes.
- **Test.** TEST-6 adds the e2e cases: a fresh `HOME` whose bootstrapped `sync.yaml` carries `defaults.registry` and no `verify_signatures`, and a bridge on that home that loads with no signature environment. `TestBootstrapStandaloneFiles_PreservesExisting` (`internal/serverboot/standalone_bootstrap_test.go:87-111`) keeps asserting byte-for-byte preservation of an existing file and needs no edit, because nothing now writes into one. The doc comment at `:86-87` and the boot-path comment at `internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:830`, which both state that existing files are preserved, stay true and are not edited.

An existing `verify_signatures: never` is never rewritten. `podium-mcp` announces such a file at startup instead, which is CODE-5's item because the resolution it reads lives in `cmd/podium-mcp/cliconfig.go` rather than in either file this item edits.

### CODE-7: register the §6.10 cells

The item edits `tools/matrix/matrices.go`. The §6.10 axis gains `config.signature_provider_unavailable`, `ingest.sign_failed`, and `config.invalid_sign_mode`; `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` is already on it from proposal 0026; `materialize.signature_missing` is already on the axis and gains its spec entry through SPEC-4. `config.invalid_sign_mode` joins because SPEC-5's §13.12 table makes it spec-normative for the first time, which is how 0026 handled `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`. Each cell new to the axis needs a `// Matrix: §6.10 (<code>)` annotated test, staged in TEST-3. A cell is registered in the checklist step that makes its behavior reachable, because `matrix-audit` runs at every step boundary and an annotated test for behavior no code has yet fails there: `ingest.sign_failed` in S11, `config.invalid_sign_mode` in S12 beside CODE-4, and `config.signature_provider_unavailable` in S14 beside CODE-5, which introduces the startup refusal it names.

### CODE-8: the Helm chart mounts the signing key

The item edits `deploy/helm/podium/values.yaml` and `deploy/helm/podium/templates/deployment.yaml`, in the form the chart's `runtimeKeys` block already uses. `values.yaml` gains:

```yaml
# Registry signing key (§4.7.9, §13.10). Every replica signs under this one key.
signing:
  mode: registry-key        # or none
  secretName: ""
  mountPath: /signing
  key: registry-signing.key
```

When `signing.mode` is `registry-key` the deployment template renders `PODIUM_SIGN=registry-key` and `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` as `<mountPath>/<key>`, mounts the Secret read-only at `signing.mountPath`, and resolves the Secret's name through `required "signing.secretName is required unless signing.mode=none"`, so a render without a Secret fails with that message. When it is `none` the template renders `PODIUM_SIGN=none` and neither the variable for the path, the mount, nor the volume. Any other value fails the render. The `volumeMounts` and `volumes` guards, which today test `objects.enabled` and `runtimeKeys.enabled`, also test the signing mode.

The Secret holds the key file in the format `readOrCreateEd25519` writes, carrying both the `private:` and the `public:` line. The loader reads an existing file and writes nothing, so the read-only mount is sufficient, and no companion file is needed, because a consumer of this deployment is handed the base64 public value out of band rather than a path, extracted from the same file by the command DOC-1 states. The operator produces the file by starting a standalone registry once, and creates the Secret from it; DOC-1 states the commands. That start is isolated from every store the operator uses: it runs under `env -i` carrying `PATH`, `HOME` set to a fresh scratch directory, and `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` naming a file in that directory, and nothing else. The first start of this release runs the one-shot §13.4 rewrite against whatever store it opens, with the configured key as its signer (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:895`, `:903`, `:934-944`), and a standalone start with no store configuration opens `~/.podium/standalone/podium.db` and `~/.podium/standalone/objects` under the invoking home (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:2346-2354`). Run against the operator's own home, the key-generation start would sign every unsigned personal row under the key destined for the cluster and record the rewrite's completion marker (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:407-408`), so the operator's next ordinary `podium serve` generates a different key at the default path, the local bridge resolves that key, and every personal row is refused with `materialize.signature_invalid` with no second pass to repair it; on a home whose store already carries signed rows the start is refused by the generated-key rule instead. It would also bootstrap `~/.podium/registry.yaml`, `~/.podium/sync.yaml`, and the artifacts directory into that home. Clearing the environment as well as `HOME` keeps an inherited `PODIUM_SQLITE_PATH`, `PODIUM_FILESYSTEM_ROOT`, `PODIUM_CONFIG_FILE`, or `PODIUM_REGISTRY_STORE` (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:789`, `:2152-2156`) from pointing the start back at a store the operator uses. A deployment upgrading a store that already holds signed rows creates the Secret from the key that signed them, because the §13.4 rewrite leaves a row whose envelope does not verify untouched.

The chart's default now requires a value it did not before, so every site that renders or installs the chart without setting a signing value follows, whether or not it passes other overrides: every `render` and `renderErr` call site in `test/chart/render_test.go` that sets no signing value, which TEST-8 enumerates, the `helm install` command in `docs/deployment/clustered.md`, and the chart-install manual scenario in `test/manual-validation.md`.

**IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** the wording of the value comments and of the `fail` message for an unknown mode, subject to the message naming `signing.mode` and both accepted values.

### CODE-10: the resolver and the readers for the registry key file

The item adds `pkg/sign/keyfile.go`. The registry writes its keypair as the two-line format `readOrCreateEd25519` produces, and the public half is read out of it by the bridge's `buildSignatureProvider`, called once from `loadConfig` through `resolveVerifier`, and by `cmd/podium/sign.go`'s provider construction. `pkg/sign` also owns the path resolution, so the writer and the readers cannot resolve different files: `KeyFilePath(env string) (string, error)` returns `env` when non-empty and otherwise `<home>/.podium/standalone/registry-signing.key`. It is the body of `internal/serverboot`'s `registrySigningKeyPath` (`internal/serverboot/signing.go:22-31`), moved rather than copied; `registrySigningKeyPath` keeps its name and becomes a one-line delegate, so `loadOrGenerateRegistrySigner` (`internal/serverboot/signing.go:58`), `refuseGeneratedSigningKey` (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:510`), and the unit case at `internal/serverboot/rehash_test.go:1402-1411` are unchanged and cannot drift from the consumers. `podium sign` also reads the private half out of the same file. `pkg/sign` owns the format so the writer and the readers cannot disagree.

```go
// PublicKeyFromKeyFile reads the registry-managed Ed25519 verification key
// from a key file written by the registry (internal/serverboot's
// readOrCreateEd25519): a text file whose "public:" line carries the base64
// key. The private half, when the file carries one, is ignored.
//
// spec: §4.7.9.
func PublicKeyFromKeyFile(path string) (ed25519.PublicKey, error)
```

- **State it reads.** It reads the file at `path` and nothing else. It holds no state of its own, and there is no cache to invalidate.
- **Format.** The first line whose trimmed text begins `public:` supplies the value, which then goes through `PublicKeyFromBase64`. A file with no such line, an undecodable value, or a value that is not `ed25519.PublicKeySize` bytes returns an error naming the path. `PrivateKeyFromKeyFile(path string) (ed25519.PrivateKey, error)` is the matching reader for the `private:` line, on the same terms: the first line whose trimmed text begins `private:` supplies the value, and a file with no such line, an undecodable value, or a value that is not `ed25519.PrivateKeySize` bytes returns an error naming the path. It is called only by `cmd/podium/sign.go`'s `registry-managed` arm, which needs a private half for `podium sign`.
- **Callers.** `cmd/podium-mcp`'s `buildSignatureProvider`, called once from `loadConfig` through `resolveVerifier`, reads the public half, `cmd/podium/sign.go`'s `loadSignatureProvider` reads the public half for `podium verify` and the private half alone for `podium sign`, and `internal/serverboot`'s `registrySigningKeyPath` delegates the path resolution. No SPI interface changes: `sign.Provider` is untouched, so every type satisfying it is unaffected.
- **When it does not fire.** An unreadable or malformed file is an error, and each caller turns it into a refusal: the bridge refuses to start with `config.signature_provider_unavailable`, and the CLI exits non-zero with the same code. Nothing falls back to an unverifying provider.
- **Test.** TEST-4 adds the `internal/serverboot` unit case, which writes a keypair through `loadOrGenerateRegistrySigner`, resolves the path through `sign.KeyFilePath`, and reads both halves back. It lands in S14 with this deliverable rather than with the rest of TEST-4 in S12, because it calls functions this deliverable creates. It is the one case in which the writer, the path resolver, and both readers are visible together, and it is stated there rather than restated here. `pkg/sign` carries the malformed-input, missing-file, and unresolvable-home cases.

### CODE-11: the evaluation stack pins its signing configuration

The item edits `docker-compose.yml`. The `registry` service sets neither `PODIUM_SIGN` nor `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` and mounts no volume over the container's home, while `postgres_data` persists the store, so under the flipped default each recreate would generate a fresh key while the stored rows hold envelopes from the previous one, which is the unrepairable rotation. The service's environment block gains `PODIUM_SIGN: "none"` with a comment naming why: the evaluation stack has no key management, and a consumer pointed at it sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never`. DOC-1 states the same in the evaluation-stack paragraph of `docs/deployment/clustered.md`.

### CODE-12: the resources mirror runs the same verification

The item edits `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go` and `cmd/podium-mcp/resources.go`. `handleResourcesRead` answers `resources/read` on `podium://artifact/<id>` by fetching `/v1/load_artifact` (`cmd/podium-mcp/resources.go:83`) and returning `resp.Frontmatter + resp.ManifestBody` (`:93-101`), and it runs no verification: the policy check and the digest check are called only inside `deliverLoadArtifact` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:1578`, `:1606`). §5.0 names the mirror a read-only mirror of `load_artifact` (`spec/05-meta-tools.md:22`), so under `always` a host reading the mirror would receive an unsigned or forged manifest that `load_artifact` refuses. The verification is called only inside `deliverLoadArtifact`, so reordering it there does not reach the mirror either.

The §6.6 step-1 reconstitution and the §6.6 step-2 verification CODE-3 orders, `fetchManifestBody`, `decodeInlineResources`, `fetchLargeResources`, the §4.7.9 policy with CODE-3's two-code mapping, and then the content-hash recomputation, are one method that both paths call. The large-resource fetch is inside the method because the recomputation hashes every bundled resource: `verifyContentHash` builds its resource map from `resp.Resources` alone and passes it to `version.CanonicalContentHash` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:1779-1783`, `pkg/version/version.go:278-290`), and a resource above the cutoff reaches `resp.Resources` only through `fetchLargeResources` (`cmd/podium-mcp/large_resources.go:87-104`). A mirror that skipped the fetch would recompute a hash without that resource and refuse every artifact carrying one, under every policy. `handleResourcesRead` fetches no resource today (`cmd/podium-mcp/resources.go:81-101`).

```go
// verifyServedArtifact runs the §6.6 step-1 reconstitution and the §6.6
// step-2 verification on a registry-served response and returns an error
// whose message leads with the §6.10 code the caller returns.
// deliverLoadArtifact and the §5.0 resources mirror both call it, so neither
// path can serve bytes the other would refuse. spec: §6.6 step 2, §4.7.9, §5.0.
func (s *mcpServer) verifyServedArtifact(resp *loadArtifactResponse, o deliverOpts) error
```

`deliverLoadArtifact` calls it first and returns `errorResult(err.Error())` on an error, then continues with CODE-3's read event, the §4.4.1 gates, and the cache writes. The method prefixes a `fetchManifestBody` or `fetchLargeResources` failure with `materialize.fetch_failed: `, as `deliverLoadArtifact` does today, and returns a `decodeInlineResources` error as that function words it. `handleResourcesRead` calls it after its existing `resources.not_found` check, with the `deliverOpts` refreshers the live `load_artifact` path passes (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:1454-1455`) built from `map[string]any{"id": id}`, returns `errorResult(err.Error())` on an error, and otherwise returns the frontmatter and body the verified record carries. The mirror still emits no read event, caches nothing, runs neither §4.4.1 gate, and materializes nothing, and it returns no resource bytes. It now reconstitutes a presigned manifest body, which it returned empty before, and fetches the artifact's large resources, because the record it verifies covers both.

- **State it reads.** It reads the served response and the `config` fields `enforceSignaturePolicy` already reads, and it writes nothing but the response it is handed.
- **Every site that sets and clears that state.** The sites are unchanged. The response is built per call by the caller, and the policy, provider, and verifier are set by `loadConfig` alone.
- **Callers and interfaces.** `deliverLoadArtifact` and `handleResourcesRead` call it, and nothing else does. No interface changes.
- **When it does not fire.** A mirror read the method refuses returns the refusal's code and no text.
- **Test.** TEST-2's mirror cases and TEST-7's `TestSignedArtifact_ResourcesReadVerifies`. Of the existing `cmd/podium-mcp/resources_test.go` constructions, `TestResources_ReadReturnsManifestBody` (`:87`) is the one that reaches the method, so it gains `verifyPolicy: sign.PolicyNever` in the step that adds the method, and its stub serves a record that passes CODE-3's verification; the constructions at `:54`, `:113`, and `:132` answer `resources/list`, a bad URI, or a not-found response before the method runs and need no edit. `test/e2e/mcp_server_test.go`'s `TestMCPResources_MirrorListAndRead` builds its environment through `mcpServerEnv` and takes the blanket disposition TEST-7's audit states, and `test/integration/mcp_test.go`'s `TestPodiumMCP_ResourcesMirror` takes the integration disposition the same audit states.

### CODE-13: delete `medium-and-above`

The item edits `pkg/sign/sign.go`, `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go`, `cmd/podium-mcp/resolution_cache.go`, and `pkg/sync/config.go`. It lands beside CODE-5, whose fallback flip removes the last default that names the value.

- `PolicyMediumAndAbove` is deleted. `ValidPolicy` accepts `PolicyNever` and `PolicyAlways`, and its doc comment, the constant block's comments, and the package comment (`pkg/sign/sign.go:1-4`, `:29-58`) state the two values.
- `requiresSignature` loses its `manifest.Sensitivity` parameter and its `PolicyMediumAndAbove` arm: `PolicyNever` returns false, and `PolicyAlways` and the fail-closed default arm return true. `EnforceVerification` loses its `sensitivity` parameter and becomes `EnforceVerification(ctx context.Context, policy VerificationPolicy, provider Provider, contentHash, signature string) error`; its missing-signature error stops naming a sensitivity. `pkg/sign` then imports no `pkg/manifest`.
- Every caller drops the argument: `enforceSignaturePolicy` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2012-2018`), `test/conformance/reference_visibility_test.go:191` and `:213`, `pkg/registry/core/extends_served_record_test.go:115`, and the `pkg/sign` tests. The `:213` conformance case, whose subject is a tampered signature on a `medium` artifact, moves to `PolicyAlways` and keeps that subject.
- `loadConfig`'s invalid-policy error (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:376`) reads `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES must be never | always, got %q`. When the value came from a `sync.yaml`, the message also names that file, which `verifySignaturesFromSyncYAML` returns under CODE-5's two-value signature. A consumer configured with `medium-and-above`, in the environment, a flag, the config file, or `sync.yaml`, therefore refuses to start, and the bridge exits 2 as it does for any unrecognized policy today (`test/e2e/governance_adoption_test.go:590`). The policy comments at `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:281-284` and `:344-346` state the two values and the `always` fallback.
- `pkg/sync/config.go:44`'s enumeration of the values, and the `loadArtifactFromCache` comments that say the cached `sensitivity` drives `enforceSignaturePolicy` (`cmd/podium-mcp/resolution_cache.go:257-261`, `:279-283`), are restated. The side file and its recovery stay, because the value is still served; no check reads it.
- Every test that names the value moves to `always` or, where its subject is the value itself, is deleted, on AUDIT-1's assignment of the files axis A returns to the step carrying this item. TEST-13 names the cases whose disposition is a deletion.

- **State it reads.** It reads the resolved policy and, for the error text, the path `verifySignaturesFromSyncYAML` returns. It holds no state.
- **Every site that sets and clears that state.** The sites are unchanged from CODE-5: the environment, the flag, the config file, and `sync.yaml` set the policy, and `loadConfig` resolves it once.
- **Callers and interfaces.** The callers are `EnforceVerification`'s callers above, and no others. `sign.Provider` is untouched.
- **When it does not fire.** A consumer whose configuration names `medium-and-above` refuses to start with the invalid-policy error naming `never` and `always`, and the file when a `sync.yaml` supplied the value. No path maps the old value onto either survivor.
- **Test.** TEST-13 covers it.

### CODE-14: the registry admits a stored row before it serves it

The item adds `pkg/registry/core/admit.go` and edits `pkg/registry/core/core.go`, `pkg/registry/server/server.go`, `pkg/registry/server/batch_load.go`, `internal/serverboot/serverboot.go`, and `internal/serverboot/rehash.go`, plus the package that holds the shared deadline-bounded object read (the object-read bullet), and restates the comments AUDIT-1's B6 finder assigns to S13 in `pkg/sign/registry_managed.go` and `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go` and the batch-resource comments in `sdks/podium-py/podium/client.py` and `sdks/podium-ts/src/index.ts`. It also restates the comments the resource-source and deletions bullets name, in `pkg/registry/core/core.go`, `pkg/registry/server/server.go`, `pkg/registry/server/batch_load.go`, `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go`, `test/e2e/http_api_test.go`, and `test/e2e/frontmatter_schema_test.go`. It implements SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph and its §4.1, §4.7.4, §7.2, and §7.6.2 edits, and the Decisions "Admit a stored row at the registry" and "Serve each resource the row holds inline from the admitted row" give the reasons.

- **The check.** `(r *Registry) admit(ctx context.Context, rec store.ManifestRecord) (store.ManifestRecord, error)` returns the row with its served fields taken from the admitted bytes, or an error. It assembles each bundled-resource body as the first-start pass does (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go`, `rehashPlanner.assemble`): `ref.Inline` when it is non-nil, and otherwise the object stored under `strings.TrimPrefix(ref.ContentHash, "sha256:")`, the key `persistResources` wrote. It computes `"sha256:" + version.CanonicalContentHash(rec.Frontmatter, rec.SkillRaw, bodies)`, the call ingest makes, and compares it with `rec.ContentHash`. For every ref, including one whose `Inline` is set, it also requires `"sha256:" + hex(sha256(body)) == ref.ContentHash` and `int64(len(body)) == ref.Size` over the body it assembled. `version.CanonicalContentHash` covers neither column (`pkg/version/version.go:278-290`), and the serve paths state both to a consumer: every `artifacts:batchLoad` resource reference carries `ref.ContentHash` (`pkg/registry/server/batch_load.go:63-69`), and a large-resource link carries `ref.ContentHash` and `ref.Size` (`pkg/registry/server/server.go:1245-1257`). For a ref whose `Inline` is nil they also choose what is served: `resourceKey(ref)` names the object presigned or read (`:1282-1284`), and `ref.Size` chooses between inline delivery and a presigned link on the single load. For a ref whose `Inline` is set, neither chooses a key or a source, because the resource-source bullet never presigns such a ref. The column table's `Resources[].ContentHash`, `Resources[].Size` row records this reason. Before it assembles any body, it requires the refs' `Path` values to be distinct. `version.CanonicalContentHash` and the assembly both key bodies by path (`pkg/version/version.go:278-290`, `internal/serverboot/rehash.go:239-256`), so a second ref under one path drops out of the recomputed hash while `attachResources` still serves it (`pkg/registry/server/server.go:1146-1167`), and `podium-mcp` lays `large_resources` over `resources` by path (`cmd/podium-mcp/large_resources.go:87-103`). Ingest writes one ref per path (`pkg/registry/ingest/ingest.go:1245-1266`). A duplicate `Path`, a mismatch of the row hash, an empty stored hash, a ref whose stored content hash or size differs from its body, and a body the object store reports absent return `ErrContentHashMismatch`. It then parses the admitted manifest with `manifest.ParseArtifact(rec.Frontmatter)`, the parse the served fields use, and checks `rec.ExtendsPin` against the parsed `Extends`: an empty `Extends` requires an empty pin; otherwise the pin, split with `splitParentRef` (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1840`), names the ID the reference names, and for an unpinned, `latest`, exact, minor, or major reference `version.Resolve(ref, []string{pinnedVersion})` succeeds, which is the match ingest's `resolveExtendsPin` applies (`pkg/version/version.go:110`, `:224-238`; `pkg/registry/ingest/ingest.go:1450`). A content-hash reference names no version, so `admit` checks only its ID, and `admitChain` (the callers bullet) compares the pinned parent row's admitted `ContentHash` with the hash the reference names once it has admitted that parent, which is SPEC-10's check (4). A row whose `rec.Frontmatter` is empty or does not parse, which `manifest.ParseArtifact` reports through `SplitFrontmatter`'s `ErrNoFrontmatter` or `ErrInvalidYAML` (`pkg/manifest/parse.go:41-45`, `:91-99`), is treated as declaring no `extends:`, so it passes the pin check when its pin is empty. Every pin failure returns `ErrContentHashMismatch`. Where the registry holds a signer, it then returns `ErrStoredSignatureMissing` for an empty `rec.Signature` and `ErrStoredSignatureInvalid` when `signer.Verify(ctx, rec.ContentHash, rec.Signature)` fails. An object read that fails for any other reason, that exceeds the deadline, or that has no object store to read from returns an error wrapping the existing `ErrUnavailable`. The hash comparison, and with it the per-ref comparison and the absent-body refusal, runs before the pin check and the signature check. Each refusal is logged with `log.Printf`, naming the tenant, the artifact ID, the version, and the reason.
- **The sentinels.** `ErrContentHashMismatch = errors.New("materialize.content_hash_mismatch")`, `ErrStoredSignatureMissing = errors.New("materialize.signature_missing")`, and `ErrStoredSignatureInvalid = errors.New("materialize.signature_invalid")`, in `pkg/registry/core`, beside `ErrUnavailable`.
- **The object read.** **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** where the first-start pass's `getWithDeadline` (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go`) and the §13.12 default read timeout (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:288`) move so that admission can call them — every object read admission makes is bounded by the `PODIUM_MIGRATION_OBJECT_READ_TIMEOUT` deadline, operator-tunable per §13.12, with its default stated once and shared by the first-start pass, admission, and `server.NewFromFilesystem`; a read the provider does not abandon on its context is still abandoned at the deadline; a failed or timed-out read maps to `registry.unavailable` and a body the store reports absent maps to `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`; and the placement adds no import cycle, because `internal/serverboot` imports `pkg/registry/core`.
- **The served fields.** `admit` returns the row with every field the column table below marks derived replaced by the value the admitted bytes state. `Body` is taken from `rec.Frontmatter` and `rec.SkillRaw` as `manifestRecordFor` sets it (`pkg/registry/ingest/ingest.go:1194-1236`). `Type`, `Sensitivity`, `Deprecated`, `ReplacedBy`, and `AuditRedact` are taken from the parsed `manifest.Artifact` through one helper, which `mergeChain` also calls with the merged artifact in place of its five assignments (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1787-1791`), so a chained record and an unchained one take those fields by one rule. A row whose manifest is empty or does not parse keeps every derived field as stored, the fallback `parsedArtifact` already applies to the indexed type and sensitivity (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1822-1833`), because its admitted bytes state none. Ingest refuses a manifest that does not parse (`pkg/registry/ingest/ingest.go:1118-1121`), so only a row written to the store directly reaches this fallback. On a signing registry, a party that can write the store cannot turn an ingested row into such a row without the stored envelope failing, and on a registry without a signer that party can already recompute the hash over any bytes. For an `extends:` child, `mergeChain` runs over the admitted rows, so the most-restrictive sensitivity §4.6 defines is computed over admitted values. Each served ref's `ContentHash` and `Size` are the values the check above bound to the ref's bytes, and the serve paths choose each ref's source by the resource-source bullet below. A row's `ExtendsPin` is followed only after `admit` has passed that row, ancestors included, so every pin `admitChain` follows (the callers bullet) and every pin the hidden-parent strip `mergeChain` applies (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1798-1810`) is one the admitted bytes agree with.
- **The column table.** Every field of `store.ManifestRecord` (`pkg/store/store.go:101-175`) and `store.ResourceRef` (`:183-199`) has one disposition. A field added to either type takes a row here, and `TestAdmit_EveryRecordFieldIsDisposed` fails until it does.

| Field | Disposition | Consumer and reason |
|:--|:--|:--|
| `Frontmatter`, `SkillRaw` | Covered | Inputs of `version.CanonicalContentHash` (`pkg/version/version.go:278-290`) |
| `Resources` | Covered, through its elements | The slice itself. Each element is disposed of by the `Resources[].*` rows, and the check above requires the elements' paths to be distinct |
| `Resources[].Path`, `Resources[].Inline` | Covered, with paths checked distinct | The framed keys and bodies of the hash. The hash takes a map keyed by path, so the check above refuses a second ref under one path. A nil `Inline` makes admission read the ref's object, and the resource-source bullet serves it from there |
| `ContentHash` | Checked | Recomputed, and the signature is verified over it. A content-hash pin selects the row by it before admission runs (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1578-1591`) |
| `Resources[].ContentHash`, `Resources[].Size` | Checked | Bound to the body admission read. The hash `CanonicalContentHash` computes covers neither, and the serve paths state both to a consumer: every batch resource reference carries the hash, and a large-resource link carries both. For a ref whose `Inline` is nil they also name the object key and the single load's choice between inline delivery and a presigned link. For a ref whose `Inline` is set they choose neither |
| `ExtendsPin` | Checked | Against the admitted `extends:` reference, before `admitChain` follows it. It selects the chain `admitChain` walks, which today `resolveExtendsChain` walks unadmitted (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1701-1720`) |
| `Signature` | Checked where a signer is configured; otherwise served as stored | Without a signer the consumer verifies it, and a party without the key mints none that verifies |
| `Body`, `Type`, `Sensitivity`, `Deprecated`, `ReplacedBy`, `AuditRedact` | Derived | `resultFromRecord` serves the first five (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1860-1875`), `Type` also picks the manifest-body document (`CanonicalManifestDoc`), and `AuditRedact` is the §8.2 read event's key set (`:1554`). `latest` resolution reads the stored `Deprecated` to choose a row before admission runs (`:1615`), so a store edit to it steers selection as the Selection row describes, and the served flag is the derived one. Ingest writes each from the bytes, and no path updates them afterwards: the SQL stores update only `content_hash`, `signature`, and `deleted_at` (`pkg/store/sqlite.go:528`, `:916`, `:945`; `pkg/store/postgres.go:923`, `:1392`, `:1426`), and neither has a `replaced_by` or `audit_redact` column (`pkg/store/sqlite.go:80-102`) |
| `TenantID`, `ArtifactID`, `Version`, `Layer`, `IngestedAt`, `DeletedAt`, `DeprecatedAt` | Selection | They choose which stored row answers a request, or whether it still exists (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1482-1496`, `:1605-1626`, `:1991`; the §8.4 purge reads `DeprecatedAt`, `pkg/store/store.go:410-415`), and are served as stored, so the version a load returns is the stored row's. The stored `Deprecated` flag steers `latest` resolution the same way. Neither the bytes nor the signature binds them. SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph states the limit, the edge-case table records it, and OQ-13 carries it |
| `Name`, `Description`, `WhenToUse`, `Tags`, `SearchVisibility` | Search-only | No load path reads them. Search reads the stored columns: `SearchVisibility` filters results (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1209`), the lexical projection `tokensFor` reads `Name`, `Description`, `WhenToUse`, and `Tags` (`:2269-2281`), and the §4.7 embedding projection `projection.Artifact` reads the same four (`pkg/registry/projection/projection.go:30-39`). Search also filters on the stored `Type`. `parsedArtifact` reads `Description` and `Tags` only for an unparseable manifest, whose served fields are stored anyway |
| `Resources[].ContentType` | Served as stored | The large-resource link's `content_type` (`pkg/registry/server/server.go:1258`). `podium-mcp` decodes it and reads it nowhere (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2000`), the TypeScript SDK copies it (`sdks/podium-ts/src/index.ts:356`), the Python SDK does not read it, the batch envelope does not carry it, and the `/objects/{key}` route serves the object store's recorded type (`server.go:1353`), so no consumer acts on the value |

- **The resource source.** Both serve paths choose each bundled resource's source from the admitted ref's `Inline`, which records where admission read the body. This bullet is the one statement of the rule. SPEC-10's §13.4 and §7.6.2 text, the Decision "Serve each resource the row holds inline from the admitted row", the Summary, and DOC-1 refer to it. Binding a ref's key to its `Inline` bytes says nothing about the object stored under that key, which admission does not read, so a ref whose `Inline` is set is never presigned. A writer who sets or clears `Inline` changes only which source admission reads, and admission binds the served bytes either way. For each admitted ref:
  - `Inline` non-nil, at any `Size`: served from `ref.Inline`, the bytes admission hashed. `load_artifact` places it in `resources`. An `artifacts:batchLoad` item places it in the reference's `inline` field, base64-encoded with `inline_base64` when it is not valid UTF-8, and carries no `presigned_url`.
  - `Inline` nil and `Size` above `objectstore.InlineCutoff`: `load_artifact` places a `presignResource` link in `large_resources`, and a batch item carries that link's URL as `presigned_url`.
  - `Inline` nil and `Size` at or below the cutoff: `load_artifact` serves it in `resources` through `inlineBytes`, which reads the object a second time after admission, and a batch item carries `presigned_url`. Ingest never writes this ref; a direct store write can. An object replaced between admission's read and the serve read is served, which is the case of an object altered after admission, and `podium-mcp`'s content-hash recomputation refuses it.

  **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** how `attachResources` (`pkg/registry/server/server.go:1148`, today `ref.Size > objectstore.InlineCutoff && s.objectStore != nil`) and `loadOneForBatch` (`pkg/registry/server/batch_load.go:135-136`, today `s.objectStore != nil` and a direct `Presign`) are rewritten to apply the three cases above — every serve path takes a resource's bytes only from the admitted row: the source is chosen from the admitted ref's `Inline` and not from the server's object-store configuration, a ref whose `Inline` is set is never presigned, a ref with a nil `Inline` is presigned or read only after admission read its object, and a server built without the object store its core admits against answers an error rather than serving bytes. A ref with a nil `Inline` reaches a serve path only after admission read its object, because a registry whose admission holds no object store refuses it as unavailable. `resourceRefsFor` sets `Inline` on every ref (`pkg/registry/ingest/ingest.go:1263`), and `persistResources` clears it only for a ref above the cutoff when ingest has an object store (`:1286-1288`). No other non-test code sets or clears it. `podium admin migrate-to-standard` copies rows as stored, and the SQL stores' JSON column round-trips a nil `Inline` as `null` and an empty one as `""`, so a zero-byte inline resource stays inline. Against today's code, on a server with an object store, a row ingested with an object store keeps its `load_artifact` delivery, and a batch item's resources at or below the cutoff move from `presigned_url` to `inline`. A row ingested while no object store was configured keeps every resource inline (`persistResources` returns before its loop when it has no put function, `pkg/registry/ingest/ingest.go:1276-1279`, and `openObjectStoreOrNil` leaves the server without an object store when the store fails to open, `internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:2513-2520`).
