Served by a registry with an object store, such a row is served inline on both paths, above the cutoff included. Today both paths presign a key ingest never wrote, and the consumer's fetch fails. SPEC-10's §4.1 and §7.2 edits state this case: a resource held inline on the manifest record is returned inline at any size, because no object exists to presign. The comments that state the size-only or object-store-only rule, the `LoadArtifactResponse` doc comment (`pkg/registry/server/server.go:572-575`), the `BatchResource` doc comment (`pkg/registry/server/batch_load.go:57-62`), `loadOneForBatch`'s loop comment (`:129-133`), and the `batchLoadEnvelope` doc comment (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2401-2404`), are restated to this rule. AUDIT-1's B7 finder returns each of them.

- **The callers.** `assembleResult` (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1671`) calls `admitChain(ctx context.Context, rec store.ManifestRecord, requestedID string) ([]store.ManifestRecord, error)`, new in `pkg/registry/core/admit.go`, as its first statement, in place of its `resolveExtendsChain` call (`:1675`). A one-row result is served unmerged, as an empty pin is today (`:1672-1674`), and a longer one goes through `mergeChain`. `admitChain` walks from the requested row toward the root and reads no row's `ExtendsPin` before `admit` has passed that row, which is SPEC-10's order. It admits the current row and stops when the admitted row's pin is empty. Otherwise it applies `resolveExtendsChain`'s cycle guard to the pinned parent's `<id>@<version>` key, reads the parent with the `splitParentRef` and `GetManifest` calls `resolveExtendsChain` makes (`:1708-1715`), admits the parent, and, where the current row declares a content-hash reference, compares the parent's admitted `ContentHash` with the named hash before it continues from the parent. It returns the admitted rows parent first, the order `mergeChain` takes. A pin that a row's admitted bytes do not declare, on the requested row or on any ancestor, is therefore refused by that row's `admit` with `ErrContentHashMismatch` before anything reads it: a cleared pin, an added pin, a pin naming another artifact, and an ancestor's pin added toward a row that does not exist or pointed back at the child. A pin that `admit` accepted but that names no stored row returns `ErrNotFound`, and one that returns to a row already walked returns `ErrInvalidArgument`, the sentinels `resolveExtendsChain` returns today (`:1704`, `:1714`). `GetManifest` reads no soft-deleted row (`pkg/store/sqlite.go:513`), so a parent deleted after its child was ingested reaches the first. Every error `admitChain` returns, admission refusals and those two included, is built from its sentinel alone as `fmt.Errorf("%w: %s", sentinel, requestedID)`, and the failing chain member, its pin, and the underlying error are logged with `log.Printf`, so no chain member's ID reaches the message. `resolveExtendsChain` keeps its body and remains the unadmitted walk `revalidationRedactKeys` uses. Every `LoadArtifact` resolution path reaches `assembleResult` through its `assemble` closure (`:1530`), and `artifacts:batchLoad` reaches it through `LoadArtifact` (`pkg/registry/server/batch_load.go:105`) and never sets the option below. `LoadArtifactOptions` gains a revalidation option, `Revalidate`, which `handleLoadArtifact` (`pkg/registry/server/server.go:1000`) sets and through which `assemble` returns an unadmitted result built from the resolved row without calling `assembleResult`. **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** `Revalidate`'s signature, and how `assemble` builds the unadmitted result and records the read event on a bypass — `assemble` consults the option after the scope check; every field the served validator reads, including the version, content hash, and layer, is set on the unadmitted result before the option is consulted; the read event of a bypassed read records the version, content hash, and layer and carries `revalidationRedactKeys`'s key set as its `RedactKeys`; the handler reports a bypass for a HEAD, and for a GET only when the validator its ETag branch computes from that result is non-empty and `If-None-Match` matches it under `ifNoneMatchHit` (`:1118`), through one expression shared with the ETag branch (today `contentHashETag(res.ContentHash)`, `:1106`), so the bypass is taken for a GET only when the ETag branch answers 304; a GET carrying `If-None-Match: *`, which `ifNoneMatchHit` matches before it reads the validator (`:1122`), and a GET whose validator does not match or is empty receive the admitted result, so a row with an empty stored hash is refused with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` rather than served a 200 body built from the unadmitted result; an `as_admin` GET, which the handler accepts on the same route (`pkg/registry/server/server.go:1021`) and runs through the same ETag branch (`:1050-1052`), takes the bypass by the same rule as any other GET, as SPEC-10's §13.4 text carries no `as_admin` exception; and the handler's existing ETag and HEAD branches (`:1049-1065`) answer from that result unchanged. Proposal 0027's CODE-5 adds `Extends` to the fields set before the option is consulted (Relationship to proposal 0027). `revalidationRedactKeys`, new in `pkg/registry/core/admit.go`, supplies the key set. Without it, a bypassed read would record the three eligible keys unmasked on every HEAD and every 304, because today the event's `RedactKeys` is set only after `assembleResult` (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1530-1567`) and the SQL stores hold no `audit_redact` column; §8.2 states that the registry honors redaction directives (`spec/08-audit-and-observability.md:36`). **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** the body of `revalidationRedactKeys` — it records the same `audit_redact` key set a full load of the row would, through admission's derivation and its stored-column fallback for an empty or unparseable manifest, folded child-wins over the `extends:` chain `resolveExtendsChain` returns, as `manifest.MergeExtends` folds `audit_redact` (`pkg/manifest/merge.go:119-121`); it makes no object read; and an error from resolving the chain keeps the sentinel `resolveExtendsChain` returned, `ErrNotFound` or `ErrInvalidArgument`, and is rebuilt as `fmt.Errorf("%w: %s", sentinel, requestedID)`, the construction `admitChain` uses, with the underlying error logged through `log.Printf`, so no ancestor's ID or pin reaches the message. The sentinel is the unadmitted walk's and can differ from a full load's: on a row whose ancestor carries a pin its bytes do not declare, toward a row the store does not hold or back at the child, a bypassed HEAD or matching conditional GET answers `registry.not_found` or `registry.invalid_argument` where a full load answers `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`, because a revalidation read is answered without admission (SPEC-10, OQ-11). The input is unattested, so a store edit that removes a directive unmasks those keys on revalidation reads, while the next full load refuses the edited row at admission. The bypass surfaces no author-named frontmatter field into the event context, so no other value depends on the set.
- **The paths that serve no admitted content.** `SearchArtifacts`, `load_domain`, `/v1/catalog`, `/v1/dependents`, and `/v1/sync/manifest` serve descriptors, edges, and identifiers; a search descriptor carries the frontmatter header block (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:2075`, `:1302`), and none carries a manifest body, a `SKILL.md`, or a resource. The `/objects/{key}` route serves bytes addressed by their content hash. `podium-mcp` checks each large-resource and manifest-body body it fetches against the hash its link carries (`cmd/podium-mcp/large_resources.go:42-47`, `:95-101`). `podium sync` and both SDKs do not: `pkg/sync/server.go:61-63` decodes no link hash and `:117-126` stores the fetched bytes unchecked, the Python SDK's `_fetch_bytes` returns the response body unchecked (`sdks/podium-py/podium/client.py:183-185`) and applies the manifest-body document unverified (`:239-246`), and the TypeScript SDK writes the fetched bytes with no hash comparison (`sdks/podium-ts/src/index.ts:451-459`). A body altered in object storage after admission, and a manifest-body object replaced at its key, which admission does not read and `attachManifestBody` does not overwrite once it exists (`pkg/registry/server/server.go:1212-1221`), therefore reach those two consumers unchecked. This proposal adds no check to them (Non-goals).
- **The errors on the wire.** `writeCoreError` (`pkg/registry/server/server.go:1393`) gains a case for each of the three sentinels that answers its code with status 500, and `errorEnvelopeFor` (`pkg/registry/server/batch_load.go:192`) gains the same three cases for a batch item. `errorCodeRegistry` carries no entry for the three codes, so each is served `retryable: false`. An `ErrUnavailable` refusal reaches `writeCoreError`'s default arm and `errorEnvelopeFor`'s `ErrUnavailable` arm, which answer `registry.unavailable`, retryable.
- **The wiring.** `(r *Registry) WithAdmission(signer sign.Provider, objects objectstore.Provider, readTimeout time.Duration) *Registry` sets admission's three inputs, and nothing clears them. The boot path (`run` in `internal/serverboot/serverboot.go`) hands admission the signer `registrySignerFor` returned, the object store `openObjectStoreOrNil` returned, and `cfg.migrationObjectReadTimeout`, which are the values the first-start pass already takes; the signer is loaded once at start (`internal/serverboot/signing.go:46-72`), so a restored key file takes effect at the next start. `server.NewFromFilesystem` (`pkg/registry/server/server.go:305-362`) hands admission the object store it was given and no signer, so a filesystem registry admits the object-held bodies its own ingest wrote (`pkg/registry/ingest/ingest.go:1286-1288` drops the inline copy above the cutoff). **IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE:** where in `run` and in `server.NewFromFilesystem` the `WithAdmission` call is made — it runs before the registry serves any request, it passes the §13.12 default read timeout where no operator value applies, and a registry with an object store and no admission fails closed: a registry built with `core.New` and no `WithAdmission`, which the test suites build and hand to `server.New`, admits with no signer and no object store, so it checks the content hash and refuses a row with an object-held body as `registry.unavailable`. `server.WithObjectStore` sets the server's object store alone (`pkg/registry/server/server.go:227-229`) and does not reach the core registry, so a test that builds its registry that way calls `WithAdmission` with the same object store itself (TEST-14's object-store disposition).
- **What it deletes.** `admit` derives `AuditRedact` and `ReplacedBy` for every served record, and `mergeChain` does so for a chained one. So the frontmatter fallback in the read-event block (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1543-1559`) and `replacedByOf` (`:1878-1894`) are deleted, the read event takes `served.AuditRedact` as it stands on a full load and `revalidationRedactKeys`'s set on a bypassed read (the callers bullet), and `resultFromRecord` reads `rec.ReplacedBy`. For a row with an unparseable manifest, both fallbacks parsed and failed, so the served values do not change. On the SQL stores, a row that is not deprecated but whose manifest names `replaced_by` now serves that value, as the memory store and a chained record already do, so the deployment modes stop diverging (§11). SPEC-10's §4.7.4 edit states that behavior: a declared `replaced_by` is returned on every load, and alongside the warning on a deprecated one. The comments that name `core.replacedByOf`, which AUDIT-1's B7 finder returns (`test/e2e/http_api_test.go:1632`, `test/e2e/frontmatter_schema_test.go:950`), name admission's derivation instead, and the `LoadArtifactResult.ReplacedBy` field comment (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1414-1415`), which the finder also returns, drops "deprecated" from "when the deprecated artifact's manifest names one".
- **The comments.** `internal/serverboot/rehash.go:109-111` and its function comment name the registry's §13.4 stored-row admission as the refusal of a served row still at the previous digest, in place of a consumer's §6.6 step-2 check. AUDIT-1's B6 finder gives S13 three more comments: `pkg/sign/registry_managed.go:44` and `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2191` name the consumer's check of the served signature beside the registry's §13.4 check of the stored one, and the `migrationObjectReadTimeout` field comment (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:1757`) names both of the deadline's readers. The comments that state every batch resource travels as a presigned reference whenever an object store is configured are restated to say that a resource the row holds inline travels inline: `pkg/registry/server/batch_load.go:43-49`, `:57-62`, and `:129-133`, `sdks/podium-py/podium/client.py:338-347`, `sdks/podium-ts/src/index.ts:544-547`, `:556-558`, and `:606-609`, and `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2402-2404`.

What admission does with each class the first-start pass leaves untouched, where a signer is configured:

| Class | Admission outcome |
|:--|:--|
| `signature_unverified` | Refused with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` while it is still at the previous release's digest, because admission recomputes the hash first, and with `materialize.signature_invalid` when its stored hash is the framed digest |
| `unreproducible`, and any row still at the previous release's digest | Refused with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` |
| `body_missing` | Refused with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` whether or not the object is restored. The pass leaves the row at the previous release's digest and, unlike `body_unavailable`, does not hold its marker back (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:351-354`, `:407-411`), so it records completion, and a restored object then reproduces the framed digest, which differs from the stored one. Restoring the object admits only a row whose stored hash is already the framed digest. The repairs are a new version of the artifact, restoring the backup the upgrade order takes with the object in place, or, on a migration target, re-running the migration into an empty target store. A row the wrong-root guard reclassified follows the wrong-root row below instead |
| `body_unavailable`, from a failed or timed-out object read | Refused with `registry.unavailable` while the body cannot be read, and with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` once it can, because the row is still at the previous release's digest; the next start rewrites it, because this class holds the pass's marker back |
| `body_unavailable`, reclassified by the wrong-root guard | The guard turns every `body_missing` row into `body_unavailable` when no object read in the pass returned a body, which is the pattern of an object store opened at the wrong root or bucket (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:257-258`, `:273-289`). While the object store still reports the body absent, admission refuses the row with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`, because a body the store reports absent is a hash refusal (SPEC-10), and it refuses every other object-held row served from that store with the same code. The repair is to point the object store at the right root or bucket and restart: the class holds the pass's marker back (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:356-361`, `:407-411`), so that start runs the pass again, and the pass rewrites and signs the rows whose bodies it now reads |

With signing off, a `signature_unverified` row whose stored hash is the framed digest is admitted, and every other row in the table is refused as the table states.

- **When it does not fire.** A registry that serves a row its check should have refused would load an altered row into every consumer, and TEST-14's store-edit arms observe it. A registry that refuses a row it should have admitted fails the load closed, and TEST-14's admitted arms and the suites' ingested fixtures observe it. A walk that followed a row's pin before admitting that row would answer a tampered ancestor pin with `registry.not_found` or `registry.invalid_argument`, and TEST-14's ancestor-pin arms observe it; a walk that compared a content-hash reference before the child's signature check would change the code a row failing both carries, and TEST-14's check-order arm observes it. `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidationSkipsAdmission` pins that a HEAD or a matching conditional GET reads no object and serves no content. A bypass consulted before the §6.3.1 load-scope check would answer a caller outside its scope with a 200 HEAD or a 304 carrying the content hash, and `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidationKeepsLoadScope` and `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidationOutsideScopeIsDenied` observe it. A bypass that set no `RedactKeys` would write the three eligible keys unmasked to the registry's audit stream on every revalidation, and `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidatedReadEventRedactsKeys` observes it. A stored field that the column table omits, or disposes of wrongly, lets a store edit steer what the registry serves, and `TestAdmit_EveryRecordFieldIsDisposed` fails when a field is omitted. If the source were chosen on `Size` alone, as it is today, a ref whose `Inline` is set would be presigned at an object admission never read. `TestDataPlane_BatchLoadPresignsOnlyObjectHeldResources`, `TestResourceDelivery_InlineRefAboveCutoffServedInlineWithObjectStore`, and TEST-14's replaced-object batch arm observe it.

### AUDIT-1: derive the edit sites rather than enumerate them

This proposal falsifies a fixed set of claims that the tree asserts in comments, doc pages, spec sentences, test names, changelog paragraphs, chart values, and manual scenarios. A file asserting one of them and carrying no edit ships a statement that contradicts the code beside it, and no part of `make coverage-gate` reads a comment, a test name, or a doc sentence for a factual claim, so nothing mechanical catches it. The claims are enumerated here once, and each names how a file asserting it is found:

| Claim the tree asserts | Falsified by | Found on |
|:--|:--|:--|
| `medium-and-above` is a policy value, or the verification default, or a policy that reads `sensitivity` | SPEC-1, SPEC-3, SPEC-6, CODE-5, CODE-13 | A |
| One policy value decides both whether a signature is required and whether the artifact is in scope | SPEC-1, CODE-1 | A |
| `noop` is the signature-provider default, a `noop` envelope verifies, and `podium verify --provider registry-managed` loads no public key | CODE-2, CODE-5 | A |
| An unset `PODIUM_SIGN` means no signer, so ingest signing is opt-in | SPEC-5, CODE-4 | A |
| The standalone bootstrap writes `defaults.verify_signatures: never` | SPEC-5, CODE-6 | A |
| The registry-managed key is scoped per organization or per tenant, is rotated quarterly or through a secret backend, Sigstore-keyless is a registry signing mode an operator selects, and signing with a transparency log is a capability the clustered tier adds | SPEC-1, SPEC-7 | B1 |
| A published configuration for a `podium-mcp` consumer is complete while naming no signing variable | SPEC-8, CODE-5 | B2 |
| A chart render or install that names no signing value succeeds | CODE-8 | B3 |
| A missing signature is reported as `materialize.signature_invalid`, and a stored row's signature is repaired by replaying its ingest or by a key-rotation command | CODE-3, SPEC-1, SPEC-5 | B4 |
| An upgraded consumer's load of an unsigned row, live or from a stale cache bucket, fails with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` | CODE-3, CODE-5 | B4 |
| A registry whose store is not the default SQLite store starts with no signing configuration, and the backup set is the store and object storage alone | SPEC-5, CODE-4 | B5 |
| During an object-storage outage `load_artifact` returns every manifest inline and only bundled-resource fetches fail | SPEC-10, CODE-14 | B6 |
| Signature verification runs only in the consumer, so `podium sync`, an SDK, or a consumer under `never` loads a row whose stored signature is missing or does not verify, or whose stored bytes do not reproduce its content hash | SPEC-10, CODE-14 | B6 |
| The consumer is the party that refuses a row the first-start rewrite left untouched, a row signed under a lost or rotated key, or a row altered in the metadata store, so the outcome depends on the consumer's binary or policy | SPEC-10, CODE-14 | B4, B6 |
| `PODIUM_MIGRATION_OBJECT_READ_TIMEOUT` bounds only the first-start rewrite's object-storage reads | SPEC-10, CODE-14 | B6 |
| A resource above the 256 KB inline cutoff is always delivered by presigned URL or in `large_resources`, or, with an object store configured, every batch resource travels as a `presigned_url` | SPEC-10, CODE-14 | B7 |
| `replaced_by` is surfaced only with the deprecation warning, or is recovered from the stored manifest only for a deprecated row | SPEC-10, CODE-14 | B7 |

The deliverables in the second column are the only ones that falsify a claim. A file that a finder returns and that none of them reaches is recorded as asserting nothing that changes, with the reason.

The edit sites are derived rather than listed, on two axes over that inventory. Axis A finds a file by the identifiers a claim names. Axis B finds a file that asserts a claim in prose that names no identifier, or that asserts one by omitting a variable the claim requires, which is the case no token pattern reaches.

Axis A produces the audit set at file granularity:

```
grep -rlE 'PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES|verify_signatures|VerifySignatures|PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER|sign\.Noop|Noop\{\}|noop:|"noop"|--provider noop|PODIUM_SIGN([^A-Z_]|$)|--sign |signMode|registrySigner|medium-and-above|MediumAndAbove|needsVerification|EnforceVerification|Sensitivity' \
  pkg cmd internal test spec docs deploy CHANGELOG.md docker-compose.yml
```

Axis B runs one finder per claim it covers. B1, B3, B4, B5, B6, and B7 are line-granular. B2 is file-granular, because the claim it covers is asserted by omitting a variable and no line carries the omission. Each returns a set small enough to read in full.

**B1, the key-scope and signing-mode claim.** A sentence describing the registry-managed key's scope or its rotation, offering Sigstore-keyless as a registry signing mode, or presenting signing with a transparency log as something the clustered tier adds, states it in prose and names no variable. The finder reaches `site/src`, because the landing page's tier list (`site/src/content/landing.ts:391`) states the tier claim outside `docs/`:

```
grep -rnE 'per-org key|per-org registry-managed|keypair per tenant|tenant signing key|managed by the registry|rotated quarterly|secret backend|[Ss]igstore-keyless|signing with a transparency log|[Ss]igning and transparency log' \
  pkg cmd internal test spec docs deploy site/src CHANGELOG.md README.md
```

**B2, the consumer-configuration claim.** This claim is asserted by omission, so it is found structurally: every file that documents or configures a `podium-mcp` process is read for the consumer-side signing variables SPEC-8 and DOC-1 stage, and a file that starts the bridge while naming none of them asserts that its recipe is complete.

```
grep -rlE 'podium-mcp|PODIUM_HARNESS' docs spec test/manual-validation.md deploy README.md
```

**B3, the bare-render claim.** The finder returns every site that renders or installs the chart. The surrounding prose is reached through the site rather than by a token of its own: a line this finder returns is read together with the paragraph it sits in and, when the line sits inside a fenced code block, with the prose paragraphs that follow that block up to the next heading, and a sentence in any of them stating what an unparameterized render produces, or listing the variables the chart renders, takes the same repair as the render site. The fenced-block arm exists because an install command sits in a code block whose own "paragraph" is the block, while the sentences describing what the command produces follow it: on `docs/deployment/clustered.md` B3 returns `:113`, inside the block at `:103-119`, and the claims are at `:121` and `:123`.

```
grep -rnE 'helm (template|install|upgrade)' docs spec test deploy README.md | grep -v 'signing.secretName'
```

**B4, the signature-code, signature-repair, and unsigned-row-code claims.** A page that tells a host or an operator which code an unsigned load reports, or how a stored signature is repaired, states it by naming the old code or the retired repair:

```
grep -rnE 'signature_invalid|content_hash_mismatch|rotate-key|replayed once' docs deploy spec CHANGELOG.md README.md test/manual-validation.md
```

**B5, the backup-scope and store-only-start claim.** A page describing what a deployment backs up, or a restore procedure that starts a registry on a fresh store, asserts that the store and object storage are the whole durable state and that such a registry starts with store settings alone:

```
grep -rnE 'Backup and [Rr]estore|Test restores|fresh Postgres|RPO' docs deploy spec README.md
```

The store-only-start half of the claim is also asserted by a page that tells an operator how to select or move the store and names no signing setting, and such a page need carry none of the tokens above. B5's second arm returns every line that selects a store in `docs/`:

```
grep -rnE 'PODIUM_SQLITE_PATH|PODIUM_REGISTRY_STORE|type: postgres|against Postgres' docs
```

A returned line is read with the paragraph it sits in, and, when it sits inside a fenced code block, with the prose that follows that block up to the next heading, on B3's terms. The arm is scoped to `docs/`, because the chart's store selection (`deploy/helm/podium/values.yaml`, `templates/deployment.yaml`) is reached by B3 and CODE-8, and §13.12's store rows sit beside the `### Signing` subsection SPEC-5 adds.

**B6, the admission claims.** Stored-row admission (SPEC-10, CODE-14) names no identifier the tree carries today, so every claim it falsifies is found in prose, together with the one variable whose description it widens. The claims are an outage description that has `load_artifact` return every manifest inline, a sentence placing signature verification in the consumer alone, a sentence making the consumer the party that refuses a stored row, and a description of `PODIUM_MIGRATION_OBJECT_READ_TIMEOUT` as bounding the first-start rewrite alone. The finder is line-granular, and a returned line is read with its paragraph on B3's terms:

```
grep -rnE "Object-storage outage|returns inline manifest bodies|runs in (each|the) consumer|[Vv]erification runs in|runs no signature check|policy decides|upgraded consumer|[Tt]he code the consumer reports|the consumer received|for authored artifacts|tampered (content|artifact)|fails a consumer's|PODIUM_MIGRATION_OBJECT_READ_TIMEOUT" \
  pkg cmd internal test docs deploy spec site/src README.md CHANGELOG.md
```

On the tree this document was written against, the claim-asserting lines B6 returns, and where each is handled, are these:

- `deploy/runbook.md:52` and `:57`; `docs/deployment/progressive-adoption.md:21` and `:80`; `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md:166` and `:217`; `docs/reference/error-codes.md:118` and `:121`; `docs/reference/cli.md:767`; and `CHANGELOG.md:68` and `:76`. DOC-1 stages all of them.
- `CHANGELOG.md:113` and `:130` sit in the "Rolling the consumers" and "Clear each consumer's cache" paragraphs, which the Relationship to proposal 0027 gives 0027, and carry the disposition "0027's passage, same release".
- `spec/13-deployment.md:207` sits inside the bullet SPEC-5 deletes, and `spec/13-deployment.md:416` is the row SPEC-10 replaces.
- `internal/serverboot/rehash.go:111` and `test/e2e/boot_rehash_test.go:142` are already named by CODE-14's comments bullet and TEST-14's e2e bullet.
- `pkg/sign/registry_managed.go:44` and `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2191` cite §4.7.9 for "verification runs in the consumer". S13 restates each to name the consumer's check of the served signature beside the registry's §13.4 check of the stored one. If CODE-5 has already removed the block `main.go:2191` sits in, the comment goes with the block.
- `internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:1757`, the `migrationObjectReadTimeout` field comment, which S13 restates to name both readers.

The lines that stay true are `spec/11-verification.md:43`, and the lines that describe the first-start rewrite's own reads or the variable's reader: `spec/13-deployment.md:83`, `internal/serverboot/rehash.go:36` and `:250`, `internal/serverboot/rehash_test.go:823`, `internal/serverboot/webui_sign_config_test.go:119` and `:138`, `internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:2139`, and `CHANGELOG.md:78`.

The remaining hits match a phrase in an unrelated sense and are dismissed on the first read: `docs/authoring/bundled-resources.md:44`, where the materialization pipeline runs in the consumer process; `test/manual-validation.md:3158`, which MAN-1 stages; `cmd/podium-mcp/cache_mode_test.go:292` and `:310`, which describe a consumer's stale bucket; `cmd/podium-mcp/materialize_pipeline_test.go:223`, `test/integration/mcp_materialize_pipeline_test.go:86`, and `test/e2e/auth_signed_load_journey_test.go:59`, `:94`, and `:98`, which tamper with bytes a stub or the consumer's own input serves; and `test/e2e/plugin_spi_test.go:24`, `:1314`, and `:1316` and `test/e2e/server_operations_test.go:504`, which are skipped cases whose skip reasons concern the harness.

**B7, the resource-delivery and `replaced_by` claims.** CODE-14's resource-source rule serves a resource held inline on the manifest record inline at any size, and on each batch item on a deployment with an object store, and its derivation of `ReplacedBy` returns a declared `replaced_by` on every load, which SPEC-10's §4.1, §7.2, §7.6.2, and §4.7.4 edits state. A sentence stating the size-only delivery rule, the object-store-only batch rule, or the deprecated-only `replaced_by` rule names no identifier the tree's other finders match. The finder is line-granular, and a returned line is read with its paragraph on B3's terms:

```
grep -rnE 'replacedByOf|Surfaced when .load_artifact. returns the deprecation warning|surfaces the upgrade target alongside|upgrade target when the|[Ll]arger resource|Above it, the response carries a URL|inline cutoff arrives as a presigned reference|above, presigned URL|never streams bytes for resources above|resources? above the (inline )?cutoff|travels? as (a )?presigned|With an object store configured the resource|arrive as a presigned URL|presigned URL above it' \
  pkg cmd internal test docs deploy spec site/src README.md CHANGELOG.md
```

On the tree this document was written against, B7's hits are handled as follows:

- `spec/04-artifact-model.md:76` and `:844`, and `spec/07-external-integration.md:30`, are the sentences SPEC-10 replaces.
- `pkg/registry/core/core.go:1873`, `:1878`, and `:1886` are `replacedByOf` and its caller, which CODE-14 deletes. `test/e2e/http_api_test.go:1632` and `test/e2e/frontmatter_schema_test.go:950` name it, and `pkg/registry/core/core.go:1414`, the `LoadArtifactResult.ReplacedBy` field comment, limits the target to a deprecated artifact. CODE-14's deletions bullet restates all three comments.
- `pkg/registry/server/server.go:574`, `pkg/registry/server/batch_load.go:58` and `:129`, and `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2403` are the comments CODE-14's resource-source bullet restates.
- `docs/reference/http-api.md:228`, `docs/consuming/handling-artifact-responses.md:49` and `:51`, `docs/consuming/custom-via-sdk.md:96`, `docs/consuming/browsing-the-catalog.md:147`, `docs/authoring/bundled-resources.md:56`, `docs/getting-started/how-it-works.md:300`, `docs/getting-started/concepts.md:267`, and `docs/authoring/frontmatter-reference.md:91` are staged by DOC-1.
- `docs/assets/diagrams/materialization-consumer-views.svg:127-128` and its ASCII fallback, `docs/consuming/handling-artifact-responses.md:42-43`, summarize the delivery on a deployment whose ingest and serve share an object store, which the shipped `PODIUM_OBJECT_STORE=filesystem` default (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:2155`) gives. They stay true under the shipped defaults, and the page's own paragraph at `:51`, which DOC-1 restates, carries the exception. They take no edit.
- `pkg/sync/server.go:116` and `pkg/sync/server_source_test.go:223` describe the entries of `large_resources`, which stay presigned URLs. `pkg/registry/server/resource_delivery_test.go:27` and `:145`, `test/e2e/bundled_resources_test.go:403`, and `test/e2e/standard_stack_parity_test.go:423` describe a fixture or assert a row ingested with an object store, whose large resources stay presigned. `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md:29` describes storage growth rather than delivery. None asserts a claim in the inventory, and each takes no edit.

A hit on either axis is read against the claim inventory. A file that matches a pattern and asserts no claim in it takes no edit and is recorded as such.

- **What it decides, and on what state.** For each file either axis returns, whether the file asserts a claim in the inventory, and which step falsifies it. The state is the working tree at the moment the step runs.
- **Every site that sets and clears that state.** The deliverables that make a claim false are the ones the inventory's second column names, and no others: SPEC-1, SPEC-3, SPEC-5, SPEC-6, SPEC-7, SPEC-8, SPEC-10, CODE-1, CODE-2, CODE-3, CODE-4, CODE-5, CODE-6, CODE-8, CODE-13, and CODE-14. Each file in the audit set is assigned to the step carrying one of them, or is recorded as asserting nothing that changes, with the reason. A non-test Go file goes to the step that lands its deliverable; where that deliverable is spec-only, so its step has already run by S9.5, the file goes instead to the first code step that lands a deliverable in its package, which is S10 for `pkg/sign`. A non-test Go file or a test file whose claim CODE-14 falsifies goes to S13. A test file goes to the step whose deliverable reddens it, a file under `docs/` or `site/src/`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `deploy/runbook.md`, or `test/manual-validation.md` to S17, a file under `deploy/helm/` to S15, and `docker-compose.yml` to S16. A file under `spec/` is a confirmation rather than an assignment, because S1 through S9 precede S9.5 and every spec edit this proposal makes is staged in a spec-amendment section; a residue is an additional spec edit, committed on its own ahead of the code steps. On the tree this document was written against the spec-only arm has one occupant, `pkg/sign/registry_managed.go:14-19`, whose doc comment restates the per-org path, the per-tenant keypair, and the secret-backend rotation that SPEC-1 replaces; it lands in S10's share, restated to match the rewritten §4.7.9.
- **Callers and interfaces.** There are none. No Go code, no helper, and no interface is added, and `sign.Provider` is untouched.
- **When it does not fire.** A file that asserts a claim in the inventory and takes no edit ships a false statement, which is the defect this item exists to prevent and which every automated gate passes.
- **Test.** The item has no test, and none is appropriate: this is an obligation on the commits that carry the falsifying deliverables, with a mechanical exit condition at S17, and it is no runtime behavior, so it has no level that owns it.

The audit runs once, at S9.5, and records the file-to-step assignment. S10, S12, S13, S14, S15, S16, and S17 apply their assigned shares, and an empty share is a recorded outcome like any other. CODE-1 rides S10, which already depends on S9.5, so its share needs no additional step. S17 closes by re-running both axes and confirming that every hit either axis returns is true under the shipped defaults or carries a recorded no-edit disposition. B1, B3, B4, B5, B6, and B7 return lines, so the hit is the line. B2 returns file names, because its claim is asserted by omission and re-running it returns the same files whatever the pages say, so its disposition is read rather than re-matched: every file it returns that starts a `podium-mcp` process names `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER` and `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, or records why it configures no bridge. No deliverable carries an exception to this exit condition.

On the current tree axis A returns 108 files. Among them, five assert a falsified claim and appear in no edit list in this document, which is what the enumeration approach missed across five rounds: `internal/serverboot/signing.go:10-11`, `internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:899-902` and `:1762-1764`, and `pkg/registry/ingest/orchestrator.go:66-68` all state that ingest signing is disabled unless `--sign registry-key` is set, which CODE-4 falsifies, and `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2172` names `Noop` the default, which CODE-5 falsifies. The `Noop` doc comment at `pkg/sign/sign.go:74-76` asserts the same default and axis A returns it too, but CODE-2 already names that sentence, so it is a site the sweep confirms rather than one it discovers. `pkg/sign/policy_validate_test.go:11-12` and `pkg/sign/sign.go:46-49` enumerate the accepted values, which CODE-13 changes, so both take CODE-13's edit in S14. Files that hold a still-true statement take no edit and are recorded as such. Axis A also returns files whose only match is an unrelated no-op (`pkg/notification/notification.go:57`, `internal/serverboot/wiring_helpers_test.go:41`); those are dismissed on the first read.

On B1's pattern before Pass 16 added its tier arm and `site/src`, axis B returned 44 lines across 23 files on B1, 16 files on B2, and 15 lines on B3, and the figures in this paragraph are that run's. Seven of B1's files and ten of B2's carry no axis-A token, which is the share axis A cannot reach. The claim-asserting lines among them are `pkg/sign/registry_managed.go:14-19`, whose doc comment states a per-org path, an Ed25519 keypair per tenant managed via a secret backend, and rotation handled by that backend, all three of which SPEC-1 replaces, and which appears in no edit list in this document because the assignment rule above routes it to S10's share; `spec/09-extensibility.md:26` and `spec/14-common-scenarios.md:123` and `:180`, which SPEC-7 and SPEC-1 already stage; `docs/reference/cli.md:689` and `docs/deployment/progressive-adoption.md:81`, which state the same per-organization and per-tenant key scope in the documentation; `docs/consuming/configure-your-harness.md`, whose contract table at `:29-32` and whose per-harness `env` blocks at `:151`, `:199`, `:242`, `:439`, and `:478` name no signing variable, so a harness configured from its published recipe refuses to start with `config.signature_provider_unavailable`; and `deploy/helm/podium/values.yaml:31`, whose comment states that a bare `helm install` boots, which CODE-8 falsifies. B1's remaining four files match on the Sigstore provider's own identity (`pkg/sign/sigstore.go`, `pkg/sign/sigstore_id_test.go`, `pkg/audit/anchor.go`, `cmd/podium-mcp/coverage_gaps_test.go`), which this proposal leaves in place as a `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER` value, and B2's remaining files name the binary without configuring it (`README.md`, `deploy/Dockerfile`, `docs/about/contributing.md`, `docs/about/status.md`, `docs/getting-started/quickstart.md`); all of those are dismissed on the first read. B1's tier arm, added in Pass 16, returns `README.md:139`, `docs/overview.md:72`, `docs/deployment/index.md:15`, `docs/getting-started/how-it-works.md:230`, `docs/deployment/local.md:162`, and `site/src/content/landing.ts:391`, each of which lists signing with a transparency log among the clustered tier's additions, and B1's existing arms also return `docs/deployment/clustered.md:35`, which calls Sigstore-keyless preferred and the registry-managed key a fallback, and `docs/deployment/single-node.md:156`, which ties transparency-log anchoring to Sigstore-keyless signing; DOC-1 stages all of them. B5 returns `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md:89`, `:93`, `:95`, and `:98` and `spec/13-deployment.md:70` and `:75`; the backup list and the drill's step 1 are staged by DOC-1, the §13.3 list by SPEC-5, and the RPO lines assert nothing that changes. B5's second arm returns `docs/deployment/integrations.md:15`, `:28`, `:30`, and `:31`, `docs/deployment/progressive-adoption.md:19` and `:21`, `docs/deployment/vector-backends.md:81`, `docs/deployment/clustered.md:123`, and `docs/deployment/index.md:4`. The metadata-store section of `integrations.md` (`:28-31`), `progressive-adoption.md:19`, and the clustered configuration example in `vector-backends.md` (the block at `:75-106` that `:81` sits in) each describe a store selection that CODE-4's persistence refusal refuses with no signing setting, and DOC-1 stages all three; `progressive-adoption.md:21` and `clustered.md:123` are already staged by DOC-1; `integrations.md:15` is the backend table's row, whose sentence-level claim is the section DOC-1 stages; and `index.md:4`, the page description, asserts nothing that changes. Axis A returns no file under `deploy/` and does not return `docker-compose.yml`, so the chart's share comes from B3, `deploy/runbook.md` comes from B4 and goes to S17, and `docker-compose.yml`'s share is empty. Among the lines B4 returns, the claim-asserting ones are `deploy/runbook.md:121` and `:129-130`, `docs/deployment/progressive-adoption.md:80`, and `docs/consuming/handling-artifact-responses.md:253`, which DOC-1 stages, `spec/04-artifact-model.md:902`, which sits inside the span SPEC-1 replaces, and the §6.9 row SPEC-4 replaces. `spec/11-verification.md:43` names `materialize.signature_invalid` for tampered content, which stays true, and the remaining hits are read at S9.5. The `content_hash_mismatch` arm's claim-asserting lines are `CHANGELOG.md:70`, `:115`, `:123`, and `:133`, all inside 0026's upgrade note: `:70` is staged by DOC-1's `CHANGELOG.md` bullet, and `:115`, `:123`, and `:133` sit in the "Rolling the consumers" and "Clear each consumer's cache" paragraphs, which carry the disposition "0027's passage, same release"; `test/manual-validation.md:7345` and `:7453` sit in S64 and are staged by MAN-1's S64 bullet, which restates the **Goal**'s third clause (`:7344-7345`) and replaces step 5's **Expect** block (`:7451-7456`), because the registry's admission refuses that load before any consumer check runs even with `PODIUM_SIGN=none`; `spec/13-deployment.md:85` is the sentence SPEC-10 replaces; and `spec/06-mcp-server.md:412` and `CHANGELOG.md:446` describe the consumer's check itself and take no edit. `docs/reference/error-codes.md:121` attributes the code to the consumer alone, which CODE-14 falsifies, and DOC-1's `error-codes.md` bullet stages its registry-side source.

## Edge cases and accepted failure modes

| Case | Observable outcome | Where it is stated |
|:--|:--|:--|
| A response declaring `sensitivity: low` with a signature that does not validate | Refused with `materialize.signature_invalid` under any policy above `never`. Previously loaded cleanly | SPEC-1; pinned by TEST-1 and TEST-2 |
| An unsigned artifact the registry labels `sensitivity: low` | Refused with `materialize.signature_missing` under `always`, and loaded under `never`. No policy value reads the label | SPEC-1's policy paragraph; `docs/deployment/progressive-adoption.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-1 |
| A consumer configured with `medium-and-above`, in the environment or in `sync.yaml` | Refuses to start with the invalid-policy error naming `never` and `always`, and naming the file when a `sync.yaml` supplied the value. The operator picks `always`, or `never` on the terms the Decision on the policy split states | CODE-13; SPEC-3's §6.2 row; `CHANGELOG.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-13 |
| A child declaring `extends:` whose effective sensitivity is higher than its own | Verified the same as any other artifact. The policy does not read the value | SPEC-1's policy paragraph |
| A response that fails the §6.6 step-2 verification and whose `sandbox_profile` is unsupported | Refused with the verification's code. Previously the sandbox gate answered first | SPEC-1's policy paragraph; CODE-3; pinned by TEST-2 |
| The local audit stream of a load the §6.6 step-2 verification refuses | Records no `artifact.loaded` event. The registry's own stream still records the call, so the load is not invisible | SPEC-1's policy paragraph; CODE-3; `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-2 |
| The local audit stream of a load the §4.4.1 sandbox or runtime gate refuses | Records the `artifact.loaded` event, unchanged from today. The read event runs above both gates and below the verification | CODE-3; `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-2 |
| The local audit stream of a load whose inline-resource decode, or a large-resource fetch that runs above the verification, fails | Records no `artifact.loaded` event, where today it records one, so a `materialize.invalid_base64` or `materialize.fetch_failed` load leaves no local trace and no cache write. The registry's own stream still records the call. During an object-storage outage that is every large-resource load, because CODE-3 keeps the fetch above the verification | CODE-3; `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-2 |
| A registry that enables signing after its first start on this release | The registry's admission refuses every row stored unsigned with `materialize.signature_missing` until a new version of it is ingested; the Decision on the policy split states what `never` covers. There is no in-place repair (OQ-5) | `CHANGELOG.md` per DOC-1; SPEC-5's §13.12 subsection; SPEC-10; CODE-14; pinned by TEST-14 |
| Consumers upgraded before the registry | Per CODE-5's table, on whether the consumer resolves material: nothing resolves and the bridge is refused with `config.signature_provider_unavailable`; a distributed key or a local key file resolves and each still-unsigned row fails with `materialize.signature_missing`. The remedy is the same either way, which is to roll the registry first, so its first start signs the rows the rewrite writes before it serves | CODE-5's resolution table; `CHANGELOG.md`'s "Rolling the consumers" paragraph per proposal 0027's DOC-1 |
| A machine whose `~/.podium/sync.yaml` already carries `verify_signatures: never` | Keeps verifying nothing. No code rewrites an operator's config. The bridge logs one startup line naming the file and the key to remove, and the upgrade notes name the edit | CODE-5's startup warning; CODE-6; `CHANGELOG.md` and `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-5's unit case and TEST-6's e2e case |
| A machine whose `~/.podium/sync.yaml` was written by `podium init` before its first `podium serve` | Starts and loads. The file is left exactly as `podium init` wrote it, and the bridge resolves the verification key from the registry's own key file rather than from that file. This is the §14.1 journey and its §14.3 graduation | CODE-5's key resolution; SPEC-1's consumer paragraph; pinned by TEST-6 |
| A machine with a stale `verify_signatures: never` that also sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` | The environment wins, so the resolved policy is above `never` and CODE-5's table applies unchanged: the bridge starts where a key resolves and is refused where none does | CODE-5; SPEC-1's consumer paragraph |
| A consumer in `offline-only` holding a cache warmed before the upgrade | Refuses those artifacts with no in-product recovery until the cache directory is cleared, because a bucket written before the upgrade is keyed under the pre-framing digest and fails the §6.6 step-2 checks. 0026 already mandates that clearing in this release | `CHANGELOG.md`'s "Clear each consumer's cache" paragraph per proposal 0027's DOC-1; proposal 0026's DOC-1 item 4 |
| `podium verify --provider noop` | Always refuses. It previously reported success for a value the CLI could compute itself | `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-9 |
| `podium sign` or `podium verify` with no flags on a standalone machine | Signs and verifies. The provider defaults to `registry-managed`, and CODE-5 resolves both halves from the registry's key file through `sign.KeyFilePath`, which carries a `private:` line beside its `public:` one | CODE-5; SPEC-1's `podium verify` and `podium sign` sentence; pinned by TEST-9 |
| `podium sign` or `podium verify` with no provider flag and no resolvable key for the half the command needs | Fails naming `config.signature_provider_unavailable`, by CODE-5's CLI arm: `podium verify` needs the public key on the §4.7.9 order and `podium sign` needs the key file's private key, so a machine carrying `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` and no key file verifies and does not sign. The CLI resolves once per invocation and has no policy to waive the refusal. `--provider noop` still signs the placeholder and no longer verifies it | SPEC-1's `podium verify` and `podium sign` sentence; CODE-5's CLI arm; the `podium sign` and `podium verify` sections of `docs/reference/cli.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-9 |
| A standalone registry started with `PODIUM_SIGN=none` | No key file is generated, so on a home where no earlier start generated one nothing resolves and the bridge is refused (CODE-5's registry-managed unresolved row). The operator sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` on that machine | CODE-5; CODE-6; SPEC-5's §13.12 subsection; pinned by TEST-6 |
| A consumer on a machine that once ran a standalone registry, now pointed at a remote registry with no `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` | Starts, because the local key file resolves, and refuses every load with `materialize.signature_invalid`. Fail-closed, and the operator supplies the remote registry's key in `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, which §4.7.9 resolves ahead of the key file, so the loads then succeed | CODE-5; SPEC-1's consumer paragraph; pinned by TEST-5's `TestSignedArtifact_StaleLocalKeyRefusesTheLoad`, whose second arm pins the remedy |
| A consumer whose `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is set but does not decode to an Ed25519 public key, on a machine whose key file resolves | Refused at start with `config.signature_provider_unavailable`, naming the variable. The set variable is authoritative and the key file is not consulted, so a mistyped remote key never starts the bridge on a stale local key | CODE-5's resolution table; SPEC-1's consumer paragraph; SPEC-4's §6.9 row; pinned by TEST-5's malformed-variable case and TEST-9 |
| A standalone registry started with `PODIUM_SIGN=none` on a home whose earlier default-mode start generated a key file | The bridge starts, because the stale key file resolves, and every load of a row stored unsigned is refused with `materialize.signature_missing`. Signing is on by default, so this is the likelier of the two `PODIUM_SIGN=none` machines. The operator sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` | CODE-6's `PODIUM_SIGN=none` paragraph; pinned by TEST-6 |
| A remote registry started with `PODIUM_SIGN=none` against a consumer on the `always` default | Such a registry publishes no key, so on a consumer that never ran a registry nothing resolves and the bridge is refused; where a key does resolve, the bridge starts and each load fails with `materialize.signature_missing`. CODE-5's table, on the same two rows as the upgrade-order case above. The remedy is `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` on that consumer, or signing on that registry | CODE-5's resolution table; SPEC-5's §13.12 subsection |
| A §6.4 workspace-overlay artifact under `always` | Loads. The overlay's bytes come from the consumer's own filesystem and carry no registry signature, and the policy does not apply to them | SPEC-1; pinned by TEST-6 |
| A registry whose key file is lost after the §13.4 first-start rewrite has completed | The start is not refused: `refuseGeneratedSigningKey` returns as soon as the rewrite's marker is set (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:522-528`), so the loader generates a fresh keypair and the registry starts and signs new ingests under it, with nothing logged about the retired key. Every row the retired key signed is then refused by the registry's admission with `materialize.signature_invalid`. The repair is the one SPEC-5's §13.12 text states. A row ingested under the fresh key in the interval is then refused in turn and loads again only at a new version | SPEC-5's §13.3 bullet; `docs/deployment/single-node.md` and `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md` per DOC-1 |
| A rotated registry-managed signing key | Every prior envelope stops verifying, permanently, and the registry's admission refuses each such row for every reader, with the code CODE-14's class table gives a `signature_unverified` row at the framed digest. 0026's first-start rewrite does not re-sign such a row on its first run or on a rerun SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph describes, and no other path re-signs a stored row, so each artifact loads again only once a new version of it is ingested, and every version stored before the rotation stays refused. There is no in-place repair | SPEC-1's rotation paragraph; SPEC-10; CODE-14's class table; OQ-2; pinned by TEST-14 |
| An envelope carrying no `key_id` against a consumer that pinned one | Verifies. The pin is skipped when either side is empty, which is unchanged here | SPEC-3's `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_KEY_ID` row; OQ-2 |
| A `sigstore-keyless` envelope | Verified against the configured trust root, with no signer-identity check and no transparency-log entry check, and refused once the certificate's validity window has passed. No registry signing mode produces one | SPEC-1's keyless bullet; carried to the follow-up proposal in Non-goals |
| `podium sync` and both SDKs | They verify no signature at any setting, which is unchanged. The registry's admission applies to every row they load. A body altered in object storage after admission reaches them unchecked, as CODE-14's bullet on the paths that serve no admitted content states | `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md` and `docs/reference/error-codes.md` per DOC-1 |
| A deployment that never set `PODIUM_SIGN` or `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, with the SQLite store in its default directory | Starts signing. The keypair is generated on first run beside the store, in `<home>/.podium/standalone/`, so the key and the store survive or are lost together | SPEC-5's §13.12 subsection |
| A deployment that never set `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, whose store is Postgres or a SQLite file outside `<home>/.podium/standalone/` | Refused at start, naming `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` and `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, with no key written and no row rewritten. The operator sets the key path on the store's persistent storage or turns signing off | CODE-4's persistence refusal; SPEC-5's §13.12 subsection; `CHANGELOG.md` and `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-4 |
| The published container image (the repository-root `Dockerfile`, `ghcr.io/lennylabs/podium-server`) run outside the Helm chart and the compose stack, with no `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` | With its default SQLite store, the key and the store both live under `/home/nonroot/.podium/standalone/`, so a recreate with no volume loses both together and strands no signature, and a volume over that directory keeps both. With a Postgres store, or `PODIUM_SQLITE_PATH` on a volume elsewhere, the start is refused by CODE-4's persistence refusal before any key is generated | CODE-4; SPEC-5's §13.12 subsection; `CHANGELOG.md` and `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-4 |
| `resources/read` on `podium://artifact/<id>` for an unsigned artifact under `always`, or for one whose record fails the §6.6 step-2 verification | Refused with `materialize.signature_missing`, `materialize.signature_invalid`, or the verification's code, and no text is returned. The mirror fetches the artifact's large resources before it verifies, so a failed fetch refuses the read with `materialize.fetch_failed`. Previously the mirror returned the manifest text unchecked under every policy | SPEC-1's consumer-policy paragraph; CODE-12; pinned by TEST-2 and TEST-7 |
| A multi-replica standard deployment that never set `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` | Every replica is refused at start by CODE-4's persistence refusal, because the shared store is Postgres, so no replica generates a key of its own. A deployment that sets the path to a different file per replica still signs under different keys, and a consumer holding one verification key refuses some loads with `materialize.signature_invalid`. The operator mounts one key or sets `PODIUM_SIGN=none` | SPEC-1's shared-key paragraph; SPEC-5's §13.12 subsection; `docs/deployment/clustered.md` per DOC-1 |
| The shipped `docker-compose.yml` evaluation stack | Signing is pinned off, so a recreate does not mint a fresh key against a store whose rows an earlier key signed. A consumer pointed at it sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` | CODE-11; `docs/deployment/clustered.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-11 |
| A store holding signed rows, upgraded with no signing variable set and no key file at `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` | The start is refused by the §13.4 generated-key rule, which the default now reaches, or first by CODE-4's persistence refusal when `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` is unset and the store is outside the default key's directory. The operator restores the key that signed the rows; `PODIUM_SIGN=none` is refused as well while signed rows await the rewrite | §13.4; `CHANGELOG.md` per DOC-1 |
| A store holding signed rows, upgraded with a key file present that is not the key that signed them | The start is not refused: `refuseGeneratedSigningKey` keys on the file's absence. The rewrite pass classifies each row `classSignatureUnverified`, logs one line per row, leaves the row at its stored hash, and records completion. The registry's admission then refuses every such artifact with the code CODE-14's class table gives a `signature_unverified` row still at the previous release's digest. Restoring the right key alone does not repair it, because the row stays at its stored pre-framing hash and the pass does not run while its completion record is present (SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph). Restoring the backup the upgrade order takes, which carries no rewrite marker, and starting with the key that signed the rows runs the rewrite again and re-signs them, at the cost of every write made since that backup, as 0026's upgrade note already states (`CHANGELOG.md:87-91`). A new version of each affected artifact is the repair only where that backup or that key no longer exists | CODE-14's class table; `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md` and `CHANGELOG.md` per DOC-1; the pass outcome pinned by TEST-4, and the load code by TEST-14's pre-framing-digest arms |
| A target registry started after `podium admin migrate-to-standard` without the source's signing key | A Postgres target with no `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` is refused first by CODE-4's persistence refusal. Otherwise the start is refused by the generated-key rule only when the target has no key file at all. A target holding any other key file, including one an earlier start generated or a chart-mounted Secret created from a different key, is not refused, and the pass classifies every copied signed row `signature_unverified` and leaves it untouched (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:199-204`). A row the source had already framed keeps the framed digest and the source's envelope (`cmd/podium/admin_migrate.go:358`), so placing the source's key at the target afterwards repairs it with no new version. A row a never-upgraded source still held at the pre-framing hash stays at that hash, and placing the source's key alone does not repair it. Recreating the target store empty and re-running the command with the source's key at the target's `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` repairs it, because the command clears the target's rewrite marker and the next start rewrites and re-signs the copied rows (`docs/reference/cli.md:627-629`, `CHANGELOG.md:96-108`); a new version is the repair only where the source store or its key no longer exists. The operator places the source's key file at the target's `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, replacing any key file already there, before the target's first start after the migration; on a chart deployment the Secret is created from the source's key file | §13.4; the migration pages per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-12 |
| A Helm install or upgrade with no signing Secret named | The render fails with a message naming `signing.secretName`, before any pod starts. The operator creates the Secret or sets `signing.mode=none` | CODE-8; `docs/deployment/clustered.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-8 |
| Replicas of one Helm release | All sign under the one mounted key, so an envelope verifies whichever replica minted it | CODE-8 |
| A row altered in the metadata store in a field CODE-14's column table marks covered, checked, or derived, or a chain member so altered | The outcome CODE-14's column table gives for the field's disposition. An edit that changes the admitted bytes, or a checked value's agreement with them, is refused with the code SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph states, including its in-range pin limit. A derived field is served with the value the admitted bytes state. Two edits to a covered or checked field are admitted: setting or clearing a ref's `Inline` while the source admission then reads holds the same bytes, which changes only the source the resource-source bullet serves from, and a `Signature` edit on a registry with no signer, which is served as stored. With `PODIUM_SIGN=none` a row altered together with its hash is admitted, and before proposal 0027 lands a consumer under `always` holding a key still refuses it. On a signing registry, a party that writes an unsigned row with its framed hash, deletes the §13.4 rewrite's completion record from the same store, and waits for a restart has the next start's rewrite sign that row, after which admission and a consumer under `always` both accept it; the start logs the rewrite's summary line (SPEC-10's limit, OQ-14) | SPEC-10; CODE-14; `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-14 |
| A row's selection columns edited in the metadata store by a party without the signing key: its artifact ID, version, layer, `ingested_at`, `deprecated`, or `deleted_at`, including a signed row's bytes, hash, and envelope copied under another artifact ID or version | Admitted, and served under the identity the row's columns state. Admission guarantees that a served row's content is content the registry signed, on a signing registry, or content that reproduces its stored hash, without one. It does not guarantee that the content was ingested under that ID and version. A consumer under `always` verifies the same envelope over the same hash and accepts it as well. Binding the ID requires a signed value that names it, which this proposal does not stage | SPEC-10; CODE-14's column table; OQ-13; pinned by TEST-14 |
| An object-storage outage, for an artifact any row of whose `extends:` chain, parents included, holds a bundled-resource body in object storage | A full load is refused with `registry.unavailable`, including a child whose own bodies are all inline, because admission reads every chain row's object-held bodies (SPEC-10). A HEAD and a matching conditional GET are answered without admission. The runbook entry DOC-1 stages states the manifest-body channel's outcome | SPEC-10; CODE-14; `deploy/runbook.md` per DOC-1; pinned by TEST-14 |
| A row whose resources above the cutoff are stored inline, ingested while no object store was configured and served by a registry with an object store | Every resource is served inline on `load_artifact` and on each `artifacts:batchLoad` item, above the cutoff included. Today both paths presign a key ingest never wrote, and the consumer's fetch fails | SPEC-10's §4.1 and §7.2 edits; CODE-14's resource-source bullet; pinned by TEST-14 |
| A merged `extends:` child, between this proposal and proposal 0027 | The consumer's recomputation covers the child's pre-merge bytes, served as `raw_frontmatter`, and not the merged frontmatter the §4.4.1 gates read and the cache stores. The registry builds that frontmatter from rows it admitted. 0027's delivery record covers it | SPEC-1's policy paragraph; SPEC-10 |
| The served `manifest_body`, between this proposal and proposal 0027 | Not covered by the consumer's recomputation, which hashes the served `ARTIFACT.md` bytes. The registry serves it from the admitted bytes. 0027's delivery record covers it | SPEC-1's policy paragraph; SPEC-10 |
| The §7.6.2 prefetch, between this proposal and proposal 0027 | Unchanged: it writes buckets from the batch envelope without verification. A cache-served load runs `verifyServedArtifact`, so a warmed bucket for an artifact carrying bundled resources or declaring `extends:`, or one with no signature under `always`, is refused, and an `offline-only` consumer reports that refusal. 0027 removes the prefetch in the same release | CODE-3; proposal 0027's OQ-8 |

## Testing

**TEST-1 · unit, `pkg/sign/` and the two suites that verify a `Noop` envelope.** `pkg/sign/sign_test.go` and `pkg/sign/policy_validate_test.go` are rewritten, because both express their expectations through the two behaviors CODE-1 and CODE-2 remove.

CODE-2's blast radius is every caller of `Noop.Verify`, and `EnforceVerification` is one of two ways to reach it: `cmd/podium/sign.go`'s `verifyCmd` calls `provider.Verify` directly (`cmd/podium/sign.go:126`), so a CLI round trip over a `noop` envelope goes red without touching the policy function at all. TEST-9 carries the CLI cases, including `TestVerifyCmd_RoundTripWithNoop`.

Two files outside `pkg/sign` sign with `sign.Noop{}` and then assert that `EnforceVerification` under `PolicyAlways` returns nil, so CODE-2 turns both red. Both move onto a `sign.RegistryManagedKey` fixture, which keeps the round trip each exists to prove, in the style `cmd/podium-mcp/cache_mode_test.go` already uses:

- `test/conformance/reference_visibility_test.go:158` and `:191-193`, part of the §11 conformance suite.
- `pkg/registry/core/extends_served_record_test.go:101` and `:115-118`.

- `TestEnforceVerification_PresentSignatureVerifiedRegardlessOfSensitivity`: `medium-and-above`, `sensitivity: low`, and a signature that does not validate returns `ErrSignatureInvalid`. **Fails against today's code**, which returns nil before reaching the provider. `// Spec: §4.7.9`. It pins the split while the value still exists, and TEST-13 deletes it with the value; the other cases here drop their sensitivity argument in the same commit.
- `TestEnforceVerification_MissingSignatureUnderAlways`: `always`, `low`, and an empty signature returns `ErrSignatureMissing`. It carries the `// Matrix: §6.10 (materialize.signature_missing)` citation the existing file holds.
- `TestEnforceVerification_NeverSkipsAPresentInvalidSignature`: `never` with an invalid signature returns nil.
- `TestEnforceVerification_UnknownPolicyFailsClosed`: an unrecognized policy with an empty signature returns `ErrSignatureMissing`, and with an invalid signature returns `ErrSignatureInvalid`. **Fails against today's code** in its second half, where the existing case asserts a `noop:` envelope passes.
- `TestNoopVerifyRefusesItsOwnSignature`: `Noop{}.Verify` refuses the value `Noop{}.Sign` produced for the same hash. **Fails against today's code.**
- `TestNoopSignUnchanged`: `Sign` still returns `"noop:" + hash`, which the §8.3 audit anchor depends on.

**TEST-2 · unit, `cmd/podium-mcp/`.** The cases form a table over `deliverLoadArtifact`, and each observes the order through the code it returns. A case whose response carries a signature under a policy above `never` uses a `sign.RegistryManagedKey` fixture and supplies the public half on the terms CODE-5's in-process-construction bullet states: through `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` at S11, and as `config.verifier` from S14.

- `TestDeliverLoadArtifact_ManifestGatesRunAfterVerification`: a response whose record fails the §6.6 step-2 verification and carries an unsupported `sandbox_profile` returns the verification's code rather than the sandbox error. **Fails against today's code**, which returns the sandbox error; after CODE-3 it passes, and it stays as the pin on the constraint SPEC-1 states.
- `TestDeliverLoadArtifact_NoAuditEventOnVerificationRefusal`: a load refused by the §6.6 step-2 verification emits no `artifact.loaded` event on the local sink. **Fails against today's code.** A second case in the same function asserts the complementary scope CODE-3 states: a load whose bytes match and whose `sandbox_profile` is unsupported emits the event and then returns `materialize.sandbox_unsupported`, because the read event stays above the §4.4.1 gates. It passes against today's code and is the regression pin on that half of the predicate.
- `TestDeliverLoadArtifact_RuntimeGateRunsAfterVerification`: the runtime gate's two rows, on a `config` whose `hostPython` is `3.9.0` and a response whose frontmatter declares `runtime_requirements` `python: ">=3.11"`, the fixture `cmd/podium-mcp/runtime_policy_test.go:41-50` uses against `enforceRuntimePolicy` directly. With a record that fails the verification the load returns the verification's code. **Fails against today's code**, which calls `enforceRuntimePolicy` at `cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:1588`, above `verifyContentHash` at `:1606`, and returns `materialize.runtime_unavailable`; after CODE-3 it passes, and it stays as the pin. With a record that verifies the load emits the `artifact.loaded` event and then returns `materialize.runtime_unavailable`, the regression pin on the read event staying above the runtime gate. The sandbox cases above do not pin this gate, because it is a separate call, and the existing runtime tests call it directly rather than through `deliverLoadArtifact`, so they cannot see the order.
- `TestDeliverLoadArtifact_NoAuditEventOnResourceFetchFailure`: a load whose large-resource fetch fails returns `materialize.fetch_failed` and emits no `artifact.loaded` event on the local sink. **Fails against today's code**, which emits the event above the fetch. It pins the suppressing class CODE-3 names for a fetch that runs above the verification, so the reorder's widening of the suppressed set is asserted rather than discovered during an outage.
- `TestDeliverLoadArtifact_MissingSignatureCarriesItsOwnCode`: an enforcing policy with an empty `signature` returns an error whose leading code is `materialize.signature_missing`. **Fails against today's code**, which leads with `materialize.signature_invalid`. `// Matrix: §6.10 (materialize.signature_missing)`
- `TestResources_ReadRefusesAnUnsignedArtifactUnderAlways` in `cmd/podium-mcp/resources_test.go`: CODE-12's mirror, over the file's existing stub, with `verifyPolicy: sign.PolicyAlways` and a response carrying no `signature`, returns an error leading with `materialize.signature_missing` and no `contents`. **Fails against today's code**, which returns the text. The missing signature is answered before the provider is used, so the case needs no `verifier` at S14. `// Spec: §4.7.9`
- `TestResources_ReadRefusesAFailedVerification` in the same file: `verifyPolicy: sign.PolicyNever` and a served record that fails the §6.6 step-2 verification returns an error leading with the verification's code and no `contents`. **Fails against today's code.** `// Spec: §6.6`
- `TestResources_ReadReconstitutesAPresignedBody` in the same file: `verifyPolicy: sign.PolicyNever`, a response carrying `manifest_body_url` in place of `manifest_body`, the stub serving the body at that URL, and a served record that verifies over that body. The read returns `frontmatter + body` with the fetched body text. **Fails against today's code**, which returns the frontmatter with an empty body. `// Spec: §5.0`
- `TestResources_ReadVerifiesOverBundledResources` in the same file: `verifyPolicy: sign.PolicyNever`, a served record carrying one inline resource with `resources_base64` set and one `large_resources` link whose body the stub serves at the linked URL, with a `content_hash` computed over the manifest, the decoded inline bytes, and the linked body. The read returns the text, which pins that the method decodes and fetches before it recomputes, because a recomputation without either resource refuses the read under `never`. A second arm serves the linked body with its bytes altered and asserts an error leading with `materialize.fetch_failed` and no `contents`. `// Spec: §5.0`, `// Spec: §6.6`


The e2e cases enumerated below set an enforcing `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` explicitly, supply no provider and no key, and load an artifact the filesystem server stored unsigned. They belong to neither of the audit's blanket camps: a blanket `never` would erase the behavior each exists to prove, and none is a signing suite that supplies a key the way `test/e2e/signed_artifact_helpers_test.go` does. Each takes the corrections stated after the list, and each keeps its subject:

- `assertUnsignedHighRefused` (`test/e2e/governance_adoption_test.go:959`, its doc comment at `:953-958`, and the caller comment at `:743`), a shared helper called from `:744`, `:1000`, and `:1042`. Its assertion on `materialize.signature_invalid` becomes `materialize.signature_missing`, and the doc comment and the caller comment, which both name the invalid code, follow. It is the pinned exit criterion of the progressive-adoption narrative DOC-1 rewrites, so it is restated rather than relaxed.
- `TestArtifactResponse_SignatureInvalid` (`test/e2e/artifact_response_test.go:749`), whose fixture is an unsigned `sensitivity: low` artifact under `always`. Its assertion becomes `materialize.signature_missing` and its name follows, because the code it now observes is the missing-signature one.
- `TestGovernance_MediumAndAboveBlocksUnsignedMedium` (`test/e2e/governance_adoption_test.go:551`) and `TestGovernance_AlwaysPolicyRejectsLowUnsigned` (`test/e2e/governance_adoption_test.go:1103`), which assert the substring `signature` and survive CODE-3 unchanged. Both tighten onto `materialize.signature_missing`, so the cell they exercise is asserted rather than matched loosely. TEST-13 deletes the first with the value, because the value is its subject, and the second keeps pinning the refusal.

The first correction is the code CODE-3 renames at S11, listed per case above.

The second is the fixture's signing state, at S12. Each of these cases boots its registry through `startServer`, which runs `serve --standalone --layer-path <dir>` and passes no `--sign` (`test/e2e/helpers_test.go:431-437`). After CODE-4 an unset `PODIUM_SIGN` resolves to `registry-key`, and the boot path hands the resulting signer to the filesystem bootstrap ingest (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:903-910` passes `signProvider.Sign` as `ingestSigner`, `:1002` passes it to `bootstrapLayerPath`, and `:510` sets it as the ingest request's `Signer`), so from S12 onward each fixture carries an envelope minted under the server's own generated key. A present signature is verified under any policy above `never` (CODE-1), so against an arbitrary verification key each assertion would observe `materialize.signature_invalid` and the behavior the case exists to pin, an unsigned artifact refused under an enforcing policy, would no longer be reachable from its fixture. Each case therefore starts its server unsigned, through one helper added beside `startServer` in `test/e2e/helpers_test.go`:

```go
// startServerUnsigned is startServer with ingest signing off. The registry
// signs at ingest by default (§13.10), so a case whose subject is an unsigned
// artifact states that here rather than inheriting a signed fixture.
func startServerUnsigned(t testing.TB, registry string) *serverProc
```

- **State it reads.** It reads its arguments alone. It calls `startServerArgs` with `HOME=t.TempDir()` and `PODIUM_SIGN=none` in the environment and the same `serve --standalone --layer-path` arguments `startServer` builds, and it holds no state of its own.
- **Callers.** The callers are the four cases above, plus `TestGovernance_UnsetVerifySignaturesLoadsLow`, which the audit assigns to S12 because it reads the resolved policy against an unsigned artifact and would otherwise observe a signature refusal instead, and `TestStandardDeploy_MCPVerifySignaturesDefault`, which the audit assigns to S14 because it loads no artifact until the disposition there gives it one. `TestCLI_VerifyPositionalArtifactUnsigned`, which TEST-9 moves onto an unsigned registry at S12, is the CLI-side case with the same need and takes the same helper. No other case calls it, and no interface changes.
- **When it does not fire.** A case that keeps `startServer` gets a signed fixture, so its missing-signature assertion fails with `materialize.signature_invalid` naming the code it observed, which is what identifies the omission. `assertUnsignedHighRefused`'s doc comment records that the flag is what keeps its fixture unsigned, because "the filesystem bootstrap attaches no signature" stops being true at S12.
- **Test.** The four cases above are the test: each asserts a missing-signature code that only an unsigned fixture produces. TEST-4's zero-flag e2e case is the complement, asserting that a server started with no signing variable serves a non-empty `signature`.

The third is the verification material CODE-5 requires at S14. Each of these cases sets an enforcing policy, so its bridge needs a provider that can verify or it refuses to start. Each supplies `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` holding a registry-managed public key in its own environment rather than in a shared helper, so no case inherits verification material it does not assert on. The key matches nothing the fixtures serve and does not need to, because the bridge then starts and the assertion observes the missing-signature refusal each case exists to prove. `TestGovernance_AlwaysPolicyRejectsLowUnsigned` and `assertUnsignedHighRefused` pass it in `progMCPEnv`'s variadic `extra` (`test/e2e/governance_adoption_test.go:69-78`) beside the policy they already pass there, and `assertUnsignedHighRefused`'s policy moves from `medium-and-above` to `always` in the same step, under CODE-13. `TestArtifactResponse_SignatureInvalid` builds its environment by appending onto `mcpServerEnv` (`test/e2e/artifact_response_test.go:756`), and it is the only `mcpServerEnv` call site that sets its own `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES`, so it stops building on that helper and states its own environment: the registry URL, a cache directory, `PODIUM_HARNESS=none`, the materialize root, `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=always`, and the key. No case then depends on which of two `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` entries in one environment slice the child process reads.

`test/integration/mcp_audit_trace_test.go` depends on the order CODE-3 reverses: its stub serves a deliberately bogus `content_hash` and its comment (`:35-37`) states that the `artifact.loaded` event is emitted before content-hash verification, which is what the test reads out of the local sink (`:98-100`). After the reorder that load writes no `artifact.loaded` line. The stub is corrected to serve a record that passes the §6.6 step-2 verification CODE-3 runs, so the load succeeds and the trace-id assertion still has an event to read, and the comment is rewritten to state the new order.
