**TEST-3 · unit; the newly registered §6.10 cells.** One annotated test per cell added in CODE-7, each asserting the code a client observes: `config.signature_provider_unavailable` in `cmd/podium-mcp/` on a startup refusal under an enforcing policy with no verification material; `ingest.sign_failed` in `pkg/registry/ingest/` with a signer that returns an error, asserting the artifact lands in `res.Rejected` while the rest of the batch is accepted; and `config.invalid_sign_mode` in `internal/serverboot/`, where the existing assertions live in `internal/serverboot/webui_sign_config_test.go:66` and `test/e2e/server_flag_behavior_test.go:195` and gain the `// Matrix: §6.10 (config.invalid_sign_mode)` annotation. `matrix-audit` fails without all three. Each lands in the step CODE-7 names for its cell, so no annotated test asserts behavior its step has not yet built: `ingest.sign_failed` in S11, `config.invalid_sign_mode` in S12, and `config.signature_provider_unavailable` in S14 beside CODE-5.

**TEST-4 · unit and e2e; the producer default.** In `internal/serverboot/`, `registrySignerFor("")` returns a provider, `registrySignerFor("none")` returns nil, and any other value refuses startup with `config.invalid_sign_mode`. **The first two fail against today's code.** The fixtures that leave `PODIUM_SIGN` unset to mean no signer set `none` instead: `internal/serverboot/rehash_test.go`, `internal/serverboot/rehash_boot_test.go`, `test/e2e/boot_rehash_test.go`, `TestRegistrySignerFor` in `internal/serverboot/webui_sign_config_test.go`, and `cmd/podium/serve_flags_test.go`.

The standard-stack e2e boots take a shared key rather than `none`, because one of them is the migrate-to-standard edge row itself. `TestLifecycle_MigrationChainFilesystemStandaloneStandard` starts its stage-2 standalone server with `HOME` set and no `PODIUM_SIGN` (`test/e2e/lifecycle_migration_chain_test.go:84-88`), so from S12 that server generates a key under its home and signs the ingested row. `admin migrate-to-standard` then clears the target's rewrite marker (`cmd/podium/admin_migrate.go:362`), and the standard target boots through `msStartStandardServer` (`test/e2e/lifecycle_migration_chain_test.go:141`), whose environment sets `HOME=` a fresh `t.TempDir()` and no signing variable (`test/e2e/standard_stack_parity_test.go:109-144`). `refuseGeneratedSigningKey` finds signing enabled, the key file absent, the marker unset, and a signed row (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:506-547`), refuses the start, and `startServerArgs` fails the test with `server exited before ready` (`test/e2e/helpers_test.go:421`). The same Postgres serves `TestStandardStackParity_AuthorToConsumer` (`test/e2e/standard_stack_parity_test.go:262`) and `TestLargeResourceDataPlane_S3PresignRefresh` (`test/e2e/large_resource_data_plane_test.go:86`), so a cleared marker beside any signed row refuses every later standard boot on a fresh `HOME` as well. Neither file matches axis A's pattern, so TEST-4 names them. The disposition lands in S12:

- `test/e2e/standard_stack_parity_test.go` gains `msSigningKeyPath(t testing.TB) string`, which returns the path of an Ed25519 key file in the format `readOrCreateEd25519` writes, a `private:` line and a `public:` line at mode `0o600`. The keypair is `ed25519.NewKeyFromSeed` over a fixed 32-byte seed declared as a constant in the file, so every run of every test binary signs under the same key. It writes the file on its first call, guarded by a package-level `sync.Once`, into a directory `TestMain` (`test/e2e/envload_test.go:14`) creates with `os.MkdirTemp` before `m.Run` and removes after `m.Run` returns and before `os.Exit`, since today it passes `m.Run()` straight to `os.Exit`, and it fails the calling test if the write failed. The file exists before any server that names it starts, so `refuseGeneratedSigningKey` never finds it absent.
- `msStartStandardServerEnv` adds `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH=` + `msSigningKeyPath(t)` to the environment it builds, ahead of `extraEnv`, so every standard-stack boot on the shared database signs under one key and none generates its own.
- The lifecycle chain's stage-2 standalone start passes the same `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH=` + `msSigningKeyPath(t)`, so the migrated row is signed under the key the target holds, which is the documented key-copy step in effect, and the target's pass classifies it migrated rather than `signature_unverified`.

When the helper is not used, a standard boot generates its own key under a fresh `HOME`, and the lifecycle chain fails at its target start with `server exited before ready` naming the generated-key refusal, which is what observes the omission. The seed is fixed because the database persists across runs. CI's Go job runs `go test ./...` and then `go test ./test/e2e/...` against the same Postgres service (`.github/workflows/test.yml:92-98`, `:113-124`), and a developer's local Postgres persists between runs. `TestStandardStackParity_AuthorToConsumer` publishes a fixed artifact ID and version on every run (`test/e2e/standard_stack_parity_test.go:272-275`) and requires HTTP 200 from `/v1/load_artifact` (`:322-325`). On a second run, ingest finds the stored row at the same content hash and short-circuits as idempotent (`pkg/registry/ingest/ingest.go:759-765`), so the row keeps the envelope the first run minted. Under a per-run key, S13's admission would refuse that load with `materialize.signature_invalid`; under the fixed key the envelope verifies and the load succeeds. A local persistent database that holds rows a run before S12 stored unsigned is recreated once, because from S13 admission refuses those rows with `materialize.signature_missing`; CI's service container starts empty on every job. The lifecycle chain is the test that pins the helper, and the parity and data-plane tests pin that it does not disturb them.

The boot refusal case asserts the error names `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, and a new case in `rehash_boot_test.go` starts with no signing variable set, no key file, and a signed stored row, and asserts the generated-key refusal, which is the default's new reach. `newBootFixture` places the store at `<home>/podium.db` (`internal/serverboot/rehash_boot_test.go:41`), outside `<home>/.podium/standalone`, so on that fixture the persistence refusal, which `run` calls first, would answer the start and the generated-key refusal would never run. The case therefore sets `PODIUM_SQLITE_PATH` to `<home>/.podium/standalone/podium.db`, the store the default key sits beside, and seeds its signed row there, so the persistence refusal passes and the start reaches `refuseGeneratedSigningKey`. Both refusals name `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, so the case asserts `names no file`, the text only the generated-key refusal carries (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:542`). A second new case in the same file starts with a key file present that did not sign the stored rows and asserts that the start is *not* refused, that the pass classifies those rows `signature_unverified` and records completion, and that the rows keep their stored hash: the accepted failure mode the edge-case table records. A third new case, `TestRun_StoreOutsideTheKeyDirectoryNeedsAKeyPath`, pins the container-image edge row with no signing variable set. Its first start runs on `HOME` A with the default SQLite store, which lives beside A's default key, so it starts, generates a key under A, and signs its row. A second start on a fresh `HOME` B with `PODIUM_SQLITE_PATH` naming A's store is refused by the persistence refusal, naming `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` and `PODIUM_SIGN=none`; it generates no key under B, and the stored row keeps its hash and signature. A third start on `HOME` B with `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` naming A's key file starts, and the row's signature verifies under that key. The case lands in S12 with the rest of TEST-4, because it calls no function CODE-10 creates. A unit table over `refuseUnpersistedSigningKey` in a new `internal/serverboot/signing_test.go` covers each arm of CODE-4's predicate: signing off, a set key path, a memory store, and a SQLite store in the default directory return nil; a SQLite store in another directory and a Postgres store return the error, which names `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` and `PODIUM_SIGN=none` and carries no DSN; and a home `os.UserHomeDir` cannot resolve returns an error. **The refusing arms fail against today's code**, where no such refusal exists.

The persistence refusal reaches every test that starts a registry through `run` with signing on, no `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, and a store outside the default key's directory, once CODE-4 lands. The applier derives the set with `grep -rlE 'PODIUM_SQLITE_PATH|PODIUM_REGISTRY_STORE|sqlite_path|type: *postgres' test internal cmd --include='*_test.go'`. A case whose subject is not signing sets `PODIUM_SIGN=none`; the standard-stack boots take `msSigningKeyPath` above; a file whose cases start no registry takes a recorded no-edit disposition. On the current tree the finder returns `test/e2e/declarative_layers_test.go`, `server_operations_test.go`, `standalone_server_test.go`, `config_permutations_test.go`, `ambient_env_guard_test.go`, `server_ops_rolling_upgrade_test.go`, `standard_deployment_test.go`, `lifecycle_journeys_test.go`, and `registry_config_format_test.go`; `cmd/podium/serve_test.go`, `serve_ui_test.go`, `serve_layer_path_test.go`, and `serve_flags_test.go`; `internal/serverboot/search_defaults_test.go`, `yaml_extra_test.go`, `backend_config_test.go`, and `coverage_gaps_boot_test.go`; `test/e2e/boot_rehash_test.go` and `internal/serverboot/rehash_boot_test.go`, which already move to `none` above; `test/e2e/standard_stack_parity_test.go` and `lifecycle_migration_chain_test.go`, which take `msSigningKeyPath`; and `test/chart/render_test.go` and `test/e2e/deployment_compose_test.go`, which start no registry. A unit case writes a keypair through `loadOrGenerateRegistrySigner` against a fixture `HOME` with `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` unset, resolves the path through `sign.KeyFilePath` with the same input, and reads both halves back through `sign.PublicKeyFromKeyFile` and `sign.PrivateKeyFromKeyFile`, asserting each matches the provider's own half (CODE-10). It is the one case in which the writer, the path resolver, and both readers are visible together, and CODE-10 and TEST-6 point at it rather than restating it. This case alone lands in S14, beside CODE-10, because `sign.KeyFilePath`, `sign.PublicKeyFromKeyFile`, and `sign.PrivateKeyFromKeyFile` do not exist until CODE-10 creates them and a case calling them at S12 would fail to compile the whole `internal/serverboot` package; the rest of TEST-4 lands in S12. `test/e2e/`: a zero-flag standalone server ingests a fixture layer and `load_artifact` returns a non-empty `signature`. **Fails against today's code.** The e2e case runs on Linux and macOS, and the existing signing tests skip on neither.

**TEST-5 · unit and e2e; the consumer defaults.** `cmd/podium-mcp/config_env_test.go` gains cases for the `always` fallback and the provider default, and `TestLoadConfig_VerifierResolution`, one case per row of CODE-5's resolution table, asserting the refusal's code or the resolved verifier's `ID()`. The case for the `registry-managed` row whose variable is set and decodes runs on a hermetic `HOME` whose key file at the `sign.KeyFilePath` default holds key A, with `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` set to key B, and asserts that the resolved `verifier` is a `sign.RegistryManagedKey` whose `PublicKey` is B. A resolving key file is present in that case because the row states that the key file is not read, and the case pins the §4.7.9 order, which an implementation reading the file first would fail. The malformed-variable row's case runs on the same kind of hermetic `HOME`, whose key file at the `sign.KeyFilePath` default holds a usable key A, with `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` set to `!!!not base64`, and asserts that `loadConfig` refuses with `config.signature_provider_unavailable` naming the variable. It pins that a set variable is authoritative, which an implementation falling through to the file on a malformed value would fail. The `never` rows assert a nil verifier, which is what pins that no material is read under `never`. **All fail against today's code**, where the defaults are the other values, no `config.verifier` field exists, and the provider is constructed per load rather than resolved once. The existing `sync.yaml`-precedence cases are extended rather than replaced, because an operator-set `never` must still win. The same file gains the cases for CODE-5's stale-`never` warning and for the §7.5.2 scope resolution that CODE-5's test bullet states, and `cmd/podium/clientconfig_test.go` gains the `podium config show` case the same bullet states. The warning cases read the line through `captureStderr`, and the warning's positive case and both scope cases clear `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` with `t.Setenv("PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES", "")` so the `sync.yaml` read is reached. The second scope case runs from a subdirectory of the workspace, which is what pins the walk-up.

CODE-5 puts the refusal inside `loadConfig`, so every existing case that calls `loadConfig()` and requires it to succeed goes red under the flipped defaults. Their disposition: `hermetic(t)` (`cmd/podium-mcp/config_env_test.go:36-43`) also sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never`, which covers every case in `cmd/podium-mcp/config_env_test.go` whose subject is something other than the signature policy. A case whose subject is the signature policy states its own `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES`, which for the stale-`never` warning's positive case is the empty string, and supplies verification material of its own rather than a policy value alone when the policy it asserts is above `never`, because setting the policy is what arms the refusal: `hermetic` pins `HOME` to a fresh `t.TempDir()`, so `sign.KeyFilePath("")` resolves a nonexistent `<tmp>/.podium/standalone/registry-signing.key`, and under the flipped `registry-managed` default no provider value rescues such a case, since `noop` never verifies and `sigstore-keyless` needs a readable trust root. The cases in that camp are `TestLoadConfig_VerifySignaturesEnvOverridesSyncYAML` (`:280-299`), which asserts that an explicit `always` beats a `sync.yaml` `never`; `TestLoadConfig_VerifySignaturesDefaultsToMediumAndAbove` (`:303-314`), which TEST-5 renames and re-points at the `always` fallback; and `TestLoadConfig_AcceptsKnownVerifyPolicies` (`:83-95`), which calls `hermetic(t)`, then sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` to each accepted value in turn and requires `loadConfig` to succeed, so its `always` arm meets the unresolved `registry-managed` row. At S14 its loop becomes `never` and `always` (TEST-13 drops `medium-and-above` in the same commit), and every arm sets the key, which the `always` arm needs and the `never` arm ignores. Each sets `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` to a base64 Ed25519 public key beside the policy it asserts on, so `loadConfig` returns and the assertion reads the resolved policy rather than the startup refusal. `cmd/podium-mcp/main_helpers_test.go` calls `hermetic` only in `TestLoadConfig_MissingRegistryErrors`, so `TestLoadConfig_BadCacheModeErrors`, `TestLoadConfig_Defaults`, and `TestLoadConfig_TokenEnvIndirection` set `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` themselves. `TestBuildSignatureProvider` in the same file (`:475-481`) loops over `{"", "noop", "sigstore-keyless", "registry-managed"}` requiring a nil error from each, which the empty-name split turns red: the empty name moves out of the accepting loop and into an assertion that `buildSignatureProvider("")` returns an error, beside the existing `"unknown"` assertion. The empty name is not the only entry that moves, because `buildSignatureProvider`'s own contract changes for two more of them. The accepting loop keeps `noop` alone, which is the one recognized name that still resolves with no material configured. `buildSignatureProvider("sigstore-keyless")` with `PODIUM_SIGSTORE_TRUST_ROOT_PEM_FILE` unset and `buildSignatureProvider("registry-managed")` with `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` unset and no key file each become an error assertion beside the `""` and `"unknown"` ones. Both of those arms gain a hermetic fixture rather than running against the developer's own `HOME`: the case sets `HOME` to a `t.TempDir()`, so `sign.KeyFilePath("")` resolves a path that does not exist, and it drops `t.Parallel()` because it mutates the environment. The matching success arms supply material through `t.Setenv`, the `sigstore-keyless` one writing a trust-root file and naming it, the `registry-managed` one setting a base64 Ed25519 public key. `TestLoadConfig_VerifierResolution` covers the policy axis on top of this, because `buildSignatureProvider` takes no policy argument and `resolveVerifier` is what refuses `noop`. What moves in the caller is `cmd/podium-mcp/cache_mode_test.go:213-217`, which sets `verifier` on the `config` it builds instead of relying on `t.Setenv("PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY", ...)` being read at delivery time, while `:257-261` is unchanged because a missing signature is answered before the provider is used. `TestBuildSignatureProvider_RegistryManagedVerifyKey` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main_helpers_test.go:490-526`) keeps its subject and gains a hermetic `HOME`, `t.Setenv("HOME", t.TempDir())` at its top, so its malformed-variable arm (`:522-525`) runs against a home with no key file rather than the developer's own, where a `podium serve` under CODE-4's default has generated one. Under the authoritative-variable rule that arm errors whatever the home holds; the hermetic home keeps the test independent of the machine, and the malformed-variable case in `TestLoadConfig_VerifierResolution` is the one that places a resolving key file beside the malformed value. `test/e2e/`: `TestSignedArtifact_NoVerifyKeyRefusesUnderTheDefaults` drives the binary with no signature environment at all and asserts a non-zero exit naming `config.signature_provider_unavailable`; the subprocess coverage caveat applies, so it runs under `GOCOVERDIR`. `TestSignedArtifact_StaleLocalKeyRefusesTheLoad` is its complement, and the case the stale-local-key edge row cites: a `HOME` carrying a registry-signing key file an earlier standalone run generated, a bridge on that home pointed at a registry signing under a different key, and `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` unset. Assert that the bridge starts, because `resolveVerifier` reads only whether key material resolves, and that the load is refused with `materialize.signature_invalid`. A second arm restarts the bridge on the same home with `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` set to the remote registry's public key and asserts that the same load succeeds, which pins the stale-local-key edge row's remedy and, at the e2e level, the §4.7.9 order that puts the variable ahead of the key file. It runs under `GOCOVERDIR` for the same reason.

**TEST-6 · unit and e2e; the standalone journey.** The e2e cases live in `test/e2e/`, on Linux and macOS, and the bootstrap unit cases live in `internal/serverboot/standalone_bootstrap_test.go`.

The server and the bridge run under different homes in this harness: `startServer` pins the server to `HOME=t.TempDir()` (`test/e2e/helpers_test.go:436`) while `mcpExec` reaches `runBin`, which pins the bridge to `cmdharness.IsolatedHome(t)` (`:134`), and `cmdharness.Run` sets `PODIUM_NO_AUTOSTANDALONE=1` (`internal/testharness/cmdharness/cmdharness.go:52`), which suppresses the bootstrap for anything it launches. The journey cases therefore start the server through `startServerArgs` with `HOME` set to `cmdharness.IsolatedHome(t)` and with `PODIUM_NO_AUTOSTANDALONE` unset for that server process, so the bootstrap runs and writes into the home the bridge reads. A helper beside `startServer` carries that wiring so the cases below share one definition of it.

- `TestStandaloneBootstrap_WritesNoPolicyLine`: a fresh `HOME`, a zero-flag `podium serve`, then assert `~/.podium/sync.yaml` carries `defaults.registry` and no `verify_signatures` key and no key material of any kind. **Fails against today's code.**
- `TestStandaloneBootstrap_SignNoneRefusesTheBridge`: the same start with `PODIUM_SIGN=none` writes `defaults.registry` and no policy line and generates no key file; a bridge started against that home with no signature environment exits non-zero naming `config.signature_provider_unavailable`, and the same bridge with `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` starts and loads. A second arm pins the other outcome CODE-6 states: start the same home once with signing on so a key file is generated and the fixture's artifacts are ingested signed, then restart with `PODIUM_SIGN=none` and a `--layer-path` fixture carrying one artifact ID the first start never ingested, so `ingest.Ingest`'s unchanged-content-hash short-circuit does not fire and that row is stored unsigned. Assert that the bridge starts on the resolved stale key, that loading the new row is refused with `materialize.signature_missing`, and that a row the first start signed still loads. Stating the new artifact is necessary: the restart reuses the same `HOME` and therefore the same store, `ingest.Ingest` short-circuits on an unchanged content hash before the signing block (`pkg/registry/ingest/ingest.go:759-764`, `:784-794`), and `PutManifest` enforces the §4.7 immutability invariant, so without it every row on that home is still the signed row the first start wrote and the asserted code is unreachable.
- `TestStandaloneFirstRun_LoadsUnderTheAlwaysDefault`: the case drives the journey. A zero-flag server starts with a `--layer-path` fixture, and a `load_artifact` through `podium-mcp` with no signature environment, against a `medium` artifact, must load. This is the guard that catches the consumer defaults landing without the producer default or without the shared key resolution.
- `TestStandaloneOverlay_LoadsUnderTheAlwaysDefault`: a `.podium/overlay/` record loads under the `always` default with no signature anywhere, pinning the §6.4 carve-out SPEC-1 states.
- `TestStandaloneInitWrittenHome_LoadsUnderTheAlwaysDefault`: a `HOME` whose `~/.podium/sync.yaml` was written by `podium init --global` and carries `defaults.registry` alone, then a zero-flag `podium serve` with a `--layer-path` fixture, then a `load_artifact` through `podium-mcp` with no signature environment, against a `medium` artifact. Assert the file is byte-for-byte unchanged and that the load succeeds. **Fails against today's code**: today an unset `PODIUM_SIGN` is no signer, so the registry stores the row unsigned, while the resolved policy on that home is the `medium-and-above` default and a `medium` artifact is therefore refused with the missing-signature error. After CODE-4 the row is signed and after CODE-5 the consumer resolves the registry's key file through `sign.KeyFilePath`, so the load succeeds. The sensitivity is stated for the same reason it is stated on `TestStandaloneFirstRun_LoadsUnderTheAlwaysDefault`: at the default `low` sensitivity `medium-and-above` skips verification and the case is green today. The byte-for-byte assertion alone pins nothing, because `writeFileIfAbsent` already returns without writing when the file exists (`internal/serverboot/standalone_bootstrap.go:144-147`); it is kept as the guard on CODE-6 not gaining a mutator.
- `TestStandaloneBootstrap_PreservesAnExistingNever`: a pre-existing `~/.podium/sync.yaml` carrying `verify_signatures: never` is not rewritten, the load still succeeds without verification, and the bridge logs CODE-5's warning naming the file and `defaults.verify_signatures`. It pins the accepted failure mode rather than leaving it undocumented, and TEST-5 carries the unit companion that asserts the line's presence and its absence when the environment supplied the `never`.

The unit cases in `internal/serverboot/standalone_bootstrap_test.go` need one addition and no restatement. `TestBootstrapStandaloneFiles_PreservesExisting` (`:87-111`) keeps asserting byte-for-byte preservation and stays green, because nothing writes into an existing file. One new case asserts that the bootstrapped body carries `defaults.registry` and nothing else, under a `signMode` of both the zero value and `none`. The round trip over the writer, `sign.KeyFilePath`, and both readers is TEST-4's unit case and is not repeated here. `pkg/sign` carries the malformed-file, missing-file, and unresolvable-home cases.

**TEST-7 · e2e; the existing signed-artifact suite.** `TestSignedArtifact_LowSensitivitySkipsVerification` (`test/e2e/signed_artifact_primitive_test.go:94`) is renamed to `..._LowSensitivityStillVerifiesAPresentSignature` and re-pointed: an untampered low-sensitivity artifact still loads, and one whose served attestation a tamper helper changed is refused with `materialize.signature_invalid`. **Fails against today's code** in its second half, which loads the tampered artifact. It runs under the suite's `medium-and-above` environment until TEST-13 moves every `f.Env(t, "medium-and-above")` call in the file to `always`. The suite's fixtures already set the provider and key explicitly, so the default flip does not disturb them.

The same file gains `StripSignature()`, which serves an empty signature and leaves every other field as constructed, and `TestSignedArtifact_ResourcesReadVerifies` pins CODE-12 through the spawned bridge on the fixture's `Env(t, "always")`, which names the provider and the key. Each arm runs on its own `newSignedArtifactFixture`, so no tamper carries into the next arm. An untouched artifact's `resources/read` returns its text; after a tamper that invalidates the signature it returns an error leading with `materialize.signature_invalid`; after a tamper of the body under the signature as constructed it returns the §6.6 step-2 verification's code; and after `StripSignature` it returns `materialize.signature_missing`. Proposal 0027, which lands after this proposal, re-expresses the tamper helpers over its delivery record. **Every refusal arm fails against today's code**, which returns the text in every arm. `// Spec: §5.0`, `// Spec: §4.7.9`


**Audit before implementing S12.** Two changes reach these suites, one step apart, so the audit runs once before S12 and its disposition is applied in two steps.

At S12 the breakage is per-artifact. CODE-1 and CODE-2 landed in S10, so a present signature is verified under any policy above `never` and `Noop.Verify` refuses every input, while the consumer defaults are still `noop` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:286`) and `medium-and-above` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:351`) until CODE-5 lands in S14. CODE-4 then makes a zero-flag `startServer` registry sign every fixture it bootstraps, so from S12 every load through `podium-mcp` of an artifact such a registry serves is refused with `materialize.signature_invalid` whatever the fixture's sensitivity is. The predicate for this half is: every `podium-mcp` process that loads an artifact, through `load_artifact` or, from S11, through the `resources/read` mirror CODE-12 routes through the same verification, from a registry started without `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, and that sets no `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never`.

At S14 the breakage is the startup refusal CODE-5 adds, so the fixture's sensitivity does not select the affected set and neither does whether a standalone server was booted. The predicate for that half is: every process that starts `podium-mcp`, and every in-process call to `loadConfig`, without `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` and without resolvable verification material. CODE-5 puts the refusal inside `loadConfig` itself, so the in-process callers are in the set even though they start no process. Enumerate both across `cmd/podium-mcp/`, `test/e2e/`, `test/integration/`, and `test/manual-validation.md` before making the flip. The shared e2e helper supplies `PODIUM_REGISTRY` and `PODIUM_CACHE_DIR` alone (`test/e2e/command_tutorial_test.go:34-36`), so every `mcpExec` call site reached through it is in the set, and `hermetic(t)` sets `HOME`, `USERPROFILE`, and `PODIUM_CONFIG` alone (`cmd/podium-mcp/config_env_test.go:36-43`), so every case that calls it and then requires `loadConfig` to succeed is in the set as well.

The disposition: `mcpServerEnv` gains `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never`, which covers every call site whose bridge environment comes from that helper, and a suite whose subject is signing supplies the key explicitly as `test/e2e/signed_artifact_helpers_test.go` already does. That blanket edit lands in S12, beside CODE-4, because S12 is the step that first refuses those loads; landing it in S14 leaves every `mcpServerEnv`-based suite red from S12 until S14. It does not reach the many e2e bridge spawns that pass an inline slice or another helper to `mcpExec`, and the e2e-finder bullet below carries those. The tests whose assertions encode the old defaults are restated rather than merely unblocked, the cases the flip leaves passing for a reason their own text no longer states are restated with them, and each carries the step its edit lands in and the gate state after that step:

- **S12, then S14.** `test/e2e/governance_adoption_test.go:245-267` `TestGovernance_UnsetVerifySignaturesLoadsLow`, whose comment states that no `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` is in the env and which asserts no signature error. It builds its environment inline, so the blanket `mcpServerEnv` edit does not reach it, and at S12 its `startServer` fixture becomes signed while the resolved policy is still `medium-and-above` over the `noop` provider, which refuses the load. At S12 it moves onto `startServerUnsigned`, which keeps its unset-policy subject intact because an unsigned `low` artifact under `medium-and-above` requires no signature. At S14 the unset variable resolves to `always`, so the case becomes an assertion that an explicit `never` loads a low-sensitivity artifact, and its name follows.
- **S14.** `test/e2e/standard_deployment_test.go:982-1002` `TestStandardDeploy_MCPVerifySignaturesDefault`. Its header at `:982` and its trailing comment at `:1000-1001` state the old `medium-and-above` default, while the body issues `initialize` alone against an inline environment that sets no policy (`:986-995`) and asserts only that the response carries `serverInfo` (`:996-999`), so no code under those two comments reads the resolved default. S12 does not reach it: the case loads no artifact, so its fixture's signing state is unobservable to it and CODE-4 cannot produce a signature refusal in it. S14 does reach it, because CODE-5 refuses a bridge whose resolved policy is above `never` and whose provider resolves no key, and `runBin` pins `HOME` to a fresh `cmdharness.IsolatedHome(t)` (`test/e2e/helpers_test.go:132-135`, `internal/testharness/cmdharness/cmdharness.go:95`), so `sign.KeyFilePath` resolves nothing, the process exits before answering `initialize`, and `rpcResult` finds no response on `res.Stdout`. The case keeps the resolved default as its subject and gains what makes that default observable: its registry starts through `startServerUnsigned`, its inline environment gains `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` holding a base64 Ed25519 public key so the bridge starts, and it gains the `load_artifact` call against the low-sensitivity unsigned artifact its trailing comment only describes, asserting `materialize.signature_missing` under the resolved `always` default. The header and the trailing comment are restated to name `always` in the same edit.
- **S14.** `cmd/podium-mcp/config_env_test.go:303-314` `TestLoadConfig_VerifySignaturesDefaultsToMediumAndAbove`, renamed and re-pointed at `always` by TEST-5, which also gives it the verification material an enforcing policy now requires.
- **S12.** `test/e2e/mcp_no_workflow_test.go:93-104`, which requires a normal start with no standalone bootstrap and no medium or high fixture. It builds its environment through `mcpServerEnv` (`test/e2e/mcp_no_workflow_test.go:93`), so the blanket edit reaches it and it needs no edit of its own; a re-derivation that finds it building its env inline gives it the same `never` explicitly, under the e2e-finder rule below.
- **S12 or S14, by the rule this bullet states.** The `test/e2e/` bridge spawns whose environment does not come from `mcpServerEnv`. The applier derives them with `grep -ln 'mcpExec(' test/e2e/*_test.go`, reads every `mcpExec` call in each returned file, and applies the rule: a case whose subject is not signing, whose bridge environment is an inline slice or a helper other than `mcpServerEnv`, and which no other bullet here, TEST-2, TEST-5, TEST-6, TEST-7, TEST-9, or TEST-12 dispositions, gains `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` in its own environment slice or in the file helper that builds it. The edit lands at S12 when the case loads an artifact, through `load_artifact` or `resources/read`, from a registry started without `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, which is the S12 predicate above, and at S14 otherwise, which is where the startup refusal reaches the rest: `runBin` pins `HOME` to `cmdharness.IsolatedHome(t)` (`test/e2e/helpers_test.go:131-135`), so no key resolves under the `always` default. A file helper shared by cases in both camps takes the edit at S12. A file that also calls `mcpServerEnv` is still read whole, because a case in it can build its environment another way. On the current tree the helper-built and inline spawns the rule selects sit in `test/e2e/deployment_modes_test.go` (`:186`, `:213`, and `TestDeployment_MCPLoadArtifactMaterializes` at `:231`, the §11 deployment-mode case, which loads from `startServer` and so takes the edit at S12); `discovery_search_test.go`, whose file helpers `brEnv` and `brMatEnv` (`:34-43`) are also called by `bundled_resources_test.go:1140` and `:1158` and `cli_reference_test.go:592`, so the two helpers take the edit at S12; `core_concepts_test.go` (`:298`, `:316`, `:352`, and `:366`); `readme_claims_test.go` (`:515`, `:526`, `:541`, and `:556`); `plugin_spi_test.go` (`:938-945`); `artifact_extends_test.go` (`:866-872`); `manifest_body_test.go` (the slices at `:78` and `:121`); `mcp_bridge_misc_test.go` (`:18-23`); `sdk_clients_test.go` (`:628`); `boot_rehash_test.go`'s `loadRehashSkill` (`:129-131`), whose registry TEST-4 moves to `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, so it takes the edit at S14; and the files the finder returns that call `mcpServerEnv` nowhere and that no other disposition names, which are `filesystem_sync_test.go`, `multilayer_journeys_test.go`, `overlay_domain_merge_test.go`, `standalone_server_test.go`, and `vector_backend_config_test.go`, each read against the rule. `governance_adoption_test.go`, `mcp_local_audit_redaction_test.go`, `signed_artifact_primitive_test.go`, `standard_deployment_test.go`, and `server_operations_test.go` are returned too and keep the dispositions this bullet list, TEST-2, TEST-7, TEST-12, and TEST-13 give them, with the rule applied to any spawn in them those dispositions do not name. When a selected case is missed, it goes red at the step its predicate names, with `materialize.signature_invalid` at S12 or a bridge exit naming `config.signature_provider_unavailable` at S14, which is what observes the omission, and S17 re-runs the finder.
- **S14.** The in-process `loadConfig` callers, whose disposition TEST-5 carries: `hermetic(t)` also sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never`, which covers every case whose subject is something else, and the cases whose subject is the signature policy state their own `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES`, the stale-`never` warning's positive case as the empty string, and supply verification material of their own beside a policy above `never`, because under such a policy a `hermetic` home resolves no key. That reaches every case in `cmd/podium-mcp/config_env_test.go`, which calls `hermetic` throughout. `cmd/podium-mcp/main_helpers_test.go` appears in no other edit list in this proposal and calls `hermetic` only in `TestLoadConfig_MissingRegistryErrors` (`:152`), so `TestLoadConfig_BadCacheModeErrors`, `TestLoadConfig_Defaults`, and `TestLoadConfig_TokenEnvIndirection` each set `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` themselves.
- **S14.** Every `test/integration/` file that spawns the bridge through `buildMCP` (`test/integration/mcp_test.go:390`). Each builds `cmd.Env` itself, either from nothing but the registry and cache variables, with no `HOME` (`test/integration/mcp_materialize_test.go:34-39`, `test/integration/mcp_materialize_pipeline_test.go:195-200`), or by appending onto `os.Environ()`, so none resolves verification material hermetically and CODE-5's startup refusal stops each process before it answers. The in-process `registryharness` those files serve from does not run the serverboot signing path, so S12 does not reach them. Each file's spawned environment gains `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` at S14, because no integration file's subject is signing; `test/integration/mcp_audit_trace_test.go` already sets it (`:65`) and needs no edit. On the current tree the set is `mcp_cache_offline_test.go`, `mcp_error_envelope_test.go`, `mcp_framing_test.go`, `mcp_health_test.go`, `mcp_materialize_pipeline_test.go`, `mcp_materialize_test.go`, `mcp_offline_status_test.go`, `mcp_overlay_domain_test.go`, `mcp_overlay_roots_test.go`, `mcp_overlay_watch_test.go`, `mcp_protocol_test.go`, `mcp_session_correlation_test.go`, and `mcp_test.go`, all under `test/integration/`; the applier re-derives it with `grep -l 'buildMCP(' test/integration/*.go` and gives every returned file the same edit.
- **S11, S12, and S14, as TEST-2 splits them.** The unsigned-load e2e cases enumerated under TEST-2, which either build their environment through `progMCPEnv` or append an explicit policy onto `mcpServerEnv`, so the blanket `never` edit does not decide their policy. Each supplies `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` and starts its registry unsigned, on the terms TEST-2 states. `TestArtifactResponse_SignatureInvalid` stops building on `mcpServerEnv` there, so after the blanket edit no `mcpServerEnv` call site carries a second `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` entry.
- **S12.** The `progMCPEnv` call sites that set no policy at all: `TestGovernance_SandboxEnforcementBlocks` (`test/e2e/governance_adoption_test.go:904`), whose subject is the sandbox refusal, and `TestGovernance_SandboxInformationalWithoutEnforcement` (`:941`), which asserts a clean load. The helper carries no policy, so the blanket `mcpServerEnv` edit reaches neither, and at S12 both fixtures become signed while at S14 the resolved policy becomes `always` with the `registry-managed` provider, which refuses the bridge at startup for want of a key. Each passes `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` in `progMCPEnv`'s `extra` at S12, which is the camp its subject belongs to and which closes both breakages for them. The helper itself gains nothing, so a case that asserts a signature outcome still states its own policy and key.
- **S14.** `test/e2e/auth_signed_load_journey_test.go`, whose subject is a signed load under the binary's own default. It does not go red: its fixture is a stub registry supplying `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER=registry-managed` and `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` (`test/e2e/signed_artifact_helpers_test.go:185-193`), so CODE-5's resolution returns a verifier, and its `medium` signed fixtures behave identically under `medium-and-above` and `always`. What it loses is its stated subject: `signedDefaultEnv` strips `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` so the binary exercises its own default (`:34-48`), and the file header and the `// Spec: §6.2` citation (`:19-27`) name that default as `medium-and-above`, which SPEC-3 makes false against the spec `speccov-drift` maps the citation to. The case keeps the unset-policy form, and the header, the `signedDefaultEnv` doc comment, and the `§6.2` citation are restated to name `always`. The default's value itself stays pinned by TEST-5's `loadConfig` case, and at the e2e level by `TestStandardDeploy_MCPVerifySignaturesDefault` once its S14 disposition adds the load that makes the resolved default observable, so this case pins the signed-load journey under whatever default is in force rather than the default's value.

`test/manual-validation.md` runs `podium-mcp` in scenarios that export no verify key; MAN-1 carries those.

**TEST-8 · unit; the chart's signing key.** The cases live in `test/chart/render_test.go`, in the style of `TestChart_RuntimeKeysPathNamesAFileInsideTheMount`, and skip when `helm` is absent as the file's other cases do. A render with `signing.secretName` set carries `PODIUM_SIGN=registry-key`, a `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` naming a file inside the mount, a read-only mount, and a volume from that Secret. A render with default values fails and the error names `signing.secretName`. A render with `signing.mode=none` carries `PODIUM_SIGN=none` and no signing mount, volume, or path variable. A render with an unknown mode fails.

Every existing `render` and `renderErr` call site in the file sets no signing value, so each fails the render once `signing.secretName` is required, whether or not it passes other overrides, and each passes `signing.secretName` so that what it observes is still its own subject: `TestChart_RuntimeKeysPathNamesAFileInsideTheMount` (`:72`), both calls in `TestChart_FilesystemObjectStoreRequiresItsVolume` (`:95` and `:103`), `TestChart_DefaultInstallOverridesNoSecretSuppliedKey` (`:119`), `TestChart_BundledPostgresNameStaysDistinct` (`:147`), `TestChart_ExternalDatabaseInstallKeepsItsEnvBlock` (`:195`), and both renders in `TestChart_RegistryContainerCarriesAStartupProbe` (`:275` and `:293`). The `renderErr` call at `:95` is the one that asserts a refusal: two refusals now compete in that render, and passing `signing.secretName` there keeps the refusal the case reads the object-store one, whose message names `objects.enabled` (`:99-101`). `TestChart_DefaultInstallOverridesNoSecretSuppliedKey` keeps its meaning with the value set, because `signing.secretName` names a Secret and adds no container env key that envFrom would supply.

**TEST-9 · unit and e2e; the CLI sign and verify surface.** The CLI tests that pin the noop default and the noop acceptance assert exactly what CODE-2, CODE-4, and CODE-5 remove. The set is AUDIT-1's test-file share rather than the list below, which is that share on the tree this document was written against; the applier re-derives it, and a file the re-derivation returns that is absent below takes the same treatment. Every file in the share carries a stated disposition, including the statement that it needs no edit, so a later reader does not stage one. The tamper negative controls among them are gutted by the flip rather than reddened, which is the failure a disposition list exists to catch, so each is listed with the fixture that keeps it meaningful. The level `.claude/rules/test-coverage.md` requires for a CLI change is the CLI, and `docs/reference/cli.md` is the D-cli doc-coverage page DOC-1 edits.

- `TestSignCmd_PositionalArtifactResolvesAndSigns` (`cmd/podium/sign_test.go:35-49`) signs and asserts nothing about verification, so it passes `--provider noop` explicitly and keeps its `spec: §4.7.9` citation where it is.
- `TestVerifyCmd_PositionalArtifactVerifiesStoredSignature` (`:53-64`) serves a `noop:` envelope off the stub and requires exit 0, so its exit-0 assertion becomes a refusal assertion in S10 and the case moves onto a `registry-managed` fixture with a key file in S14, once CODE-5 gives the CLI a key to resolve. It keeps its `spec: §4.7.9` citation.
- `TestSignVerifyCmd_ContentHashFormRoundTrips` (`:97-115`) signs and then verifies the envelope it just produced, requiring exit 0 on the verify half, so `--provider noop` keeps the sign half green and turns the verify half red. It takes the same disposition as `TestGovernance_VerifyRoundTrip` below: the verify half becomes a refusal assertion in S10, and the case moves onto a `registry-managed` key fixture in S14.
- `TestVerifyCmd_PositionalArtifactRejectsTamperedSignature` (`cmd/podium/sign_test.go:68-79`) stubs a stored `noop:` envelope over a mismatched hash and asserts exit 1. The flip does not turn it red; it guts it. `loadSignatureProvider("registry-managed")` succeeds and returns a keyless `sign.RegistryManagedKey{}` (`cmd/podium/sign.go:176-181`), so the load block at `:121-125` is not taken and `provider.Verify` (`:126`) returns `ErrRegistryManagedUnavailable`, which exits 1 through the same `verify failed:` branch (`:127-128`) a genuine mismatch takes. Neither the exit code nor the message distinguishes an unavailable provider from a mismatched envelope, so the case would pass while asserting nothing about tampering. What restores its subject is the S14 move onto a `registry-managed` key fixture whose stored envelope is signed over a different hash: the provider then resolves and only the mismatch fails. The message assertion is added on that keyed form, where the message names the failed verification of a real envelope. It keeps its `spec: §4.7.9` citation, which is the only unit-level pin on the mismatch refusal.
- `TestCLI_VerifyTampered` (`test/e2e/cli_reference_test.go:1876-1884`) signs with the default provider and verifies the envelope against a different hash. After the flip `podium sign --content-hash` exits non-zero printing nothing (`cmd/podium/sign.go:59-64`), so `sig` is empty and `verifyCmd` returns 2 on the usage guard (`:116-119`) rather than 1 on a failed verification. It takes the same disposition: a `registry-managed` key fixture for both halves, asserting exit 1 and the verification-failure message. It is the only CLI e2e pin on the mismatch refusal that signs its own envelope.
- `TestGovernance_VerifyMismatchedSignature` (`test/e2e/governance_adoption_test.go:671-687`) runs `podium verify --provider noop` over the envelope `noop:sha256:wrong_hash` against `sha256:abc123def456` and asserts exit 1 with `verify failed` and `signature_invalid`. CODE-2 guts it rather than reddening it: `Noop.Verify` refuses every input with `ErrSignatureInvalid`, so the case passes whether or not the hash mismatches. It moves onto a `registry-managed` key fixture, a key file on a hermetic `HOME` or `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` naming its public half, with an envelope that key signed over a different hash, so the assertions observe a real verification failure. The move lands in S14, because the CLI resolves no `registry-managed` key before CODE-5, and its doc comment at `:668-670` follows.
- `TestVerifyCmd_RoundTripWithNoop` (`cmd/podium/admin_happy_paths_test.go:246-283`) signs with `--provider noop` and then requires `verifyCmd` with `--provider noop`, the same hash, and the produced envelope to exit 0 (`:263-270`), before asserting exit 1 on a tampered hash (`:272-281`). Under CODE-2 `Noop.Verify` refuses every input, so `provider.Verify` fails (`cmd/podium/sign.go:126-129`) and the exit-0 half goes red. The case never calls `EnforceVerification`, so TEST-1's predicate does not select it. It takes the same disposition as `TestSignVerifyCmd_ContentHashFormRoundTrips`: the exit-0 half becomes a refusal assertion in S10, and in S14 both halves move onto a `registry-managed` key fixture, keeping the exit-0 assertion on the matching hash and the exit-1 assertion on the tampered one. Its doc comment at `:245`, which names the noop provider, follows.
- `TestLoadSignatureProvider` (`cmd/podium/cli_helpers_test.go:103-134`) is the CLI twin of the bridge's `TestBuildSignatureProvider` and asserts the parts of `loadSignatureProvider`'s contract CODE-5 changes (`cmd/podium/sign.go:163-185`). Every call gains the `keyUse` argument CODE-5 adds; the rows other than `registry-managed` pass `keyForVerify`, which those arms ignore. Its `{"", false, "sign.Noop"}` row goes red when the `case "", "noop"` arm splits, so it moves to an error assertion beside the `"unknown"` row, asserting the `unknown signature provider:` error. Its `{"registry-managed", false, "sign.RegistryManagedKey"}` row runs against the developer's own `HOME` today and, once CODE-5 resolves key material, fails wherever no key file exists there, which includes CI. It splits into four arms on a hermetic home, `t.Setenv("HOME", t.TempDir())` with `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` and `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` cleared: `keyForVerify` with no material, asserting an error naming `config.signature_provider_unavailable`; `keyForSign` with `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` set to a valid public key and no key file, asserting the same error, which pins that the sign half never reads the variable; and, after writing a key file carrying a `private:` and a `public:` line at the `sign.KeyFilePath` default, `keyForVerify` and `keyForSign` each returning a `sign.RegistryManagedKey` that holds the half its use names. The test stays non-parallel, because it mutates the environment. The `noop`, `NOOP`, `sigstore-keyless`, and `"unknown"` rows keep their assertions. The disposition lands in S14 with CODE-5.
- A test file in AUDIT-1's set that needs no edit carries a recorded no-edit disposition rather than silence, so a later reader does not stage one. `cmd/podium/flags_after_positional_test.go` is the worked example: every `signCmd` and `verifyCmd` invocation in it passes `--provider bogus` (`:159`, `:169`, `:187`, `:197`) and asserts the `unknown signature provider: bogus` refusal that `loadSignatureProvider`'s default arm returns (`cmd/podium/sign.go:183`) before any `Sign` or `Verify` call, and the `signableArtifact` fixture's `noop:` envelope (`:150`) is never verified, so neither the provider-default flip nor `Noop.Verify`'s refusal reaches it. Switching these calls to `--provider noop` would destroy the assertion each case exists to make.
- `test/e2e/cli_reference_test.go:1837-1867`: `TestCLI_Sign` and `TestCLI_SignPositionalArtifact` pass `--provider noop`, and the header sentence stating that the noop provider is the default is rewritten to name `registry-managed`. `TestCLI_VerifyPositionalArtifactUnsigned` is rebuilt on a registry started with `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, because a standalone server now signs at ingest.
- `test/e2e/cli_reference_test.go:1869-1875` `TestCLI_VerifyValid` signs with the default provider and then verifies the envelope it produced, requiring exit 0, so it takes the round-trip disposition: the verify half becomes a refusal assertion in S10, and the case moves onto a `registry-managed` key fixture in S14.
- `test/e2e/governance_adoption_test.go:646-663` `TestGovernance_VerifyRoundTrip` passes `--provider noop` and requires exit 0 plus `verify ok`; the verify half becomes a refusal assertion in S10, and the case moves onto a registry-managed key in S14.

New cases: `podium sign` and `podium verify` with no provider flag and no resolvable key exit non-zero naming `config.signature_provider_unavailable`; `podium verify --provider noop` refuses an envelope `podium sign --provider noop` just produced; `podium verify` with no flags on a standalone-bootstrapped `HOME` succeeds against the key file the registry generated; `podium sign` with no flags on that same `HOME` produces an envelope that `podium verify` with no flags accepts, which is the case that pins both halves resolving through `sign.KeyFilePath`'s home default when `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` and `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` are both unset; and `podium verify` on a `HOME` whose key file holds key A, with `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` set to key B, exits 0 over an envelope B signed and exits 1 over one A signed, which pins the public half's §4.7.9 order at the CLI; and `podium verify` on a `HOME` whose key file holds key A, with `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` set to `!!!not base64`, exits non-zero naming `config.signature_provider_unavailable` and the variable, which pins the authoritative-variable rule at the CLI. Three further cases pin that each command resolves only the half it needs: `podium sign --content-hash` on a `HOME` whose key file resolves, with `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` set to `!!!not base64`, exits 0 and prints an envelope the key file's public half verifies; `podium sign --content-hash` on a hermetic `HOME` with no key file, with `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` set to a valid public key, exits 1 with stderr naming `config.signature_provider_unavailable` and the key path; and `podium verify --content-hash --signature` on a hermetic `HOME` with no key file, with `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` set to the public half of a key the test generated, exits 0 over an envelope the test signed with that key through `sign.RegistryManagedKey`, which is the remote-consumer setup.

**Which step each of these lands in.** The predicate is what turns the case red. A case that requires a *successful* verification through the `noop` provider, whether it names the provider or inherits it as today's default, goes red on CODE-2 alone, so its disposition lands in S10 beside CODE-2 rather than in S14: `TestVerifyCmd_PositionalArtifactVerifiesStoredSignature`, `TestSignVerifyCmd_ContentHashFormRoundTrips`, `TestVerifyCmd_RoundTripWithNoop`, `test/e2e/cli_reference_test.go:1869-1875` `TestCLI_VerifyValid`, and `TestGovernance_VerifyRoundTrip`. Each of these five drives the CLI, which resolves its provider through `loadSignatureProvider` and gets a keyless `sign.RegistryManagedKey{}` for `registry-managed` (`cmd/podium/sign.go:176-181`), so `Sign` and `Verify` both return `ErrRegistryManagedUnavailable` (`pkg/sign/registry_managed.go:76-77` and `:102-103`) until CODE-5 resolves key material in that file. Their S10 disposition is therefore the refusal-assertion form alone: the exit-0 half asserts the refusal `Noop.Verify` now returns, and the move onto a keyed `registry-managed` fixture lands in S14 with CODE-5, where the exit-0 assertion returns on a matching hash. `TestCLI_VerifyPositionalArtifactUnsigned` goes red on CODE-4, so it lands in S12 with the producer default. Everything else in this item is reached by the provider-default flip CODE-5 makes and lands in S14: `TestVerifyCmd_PositionalArtifactRejectsTamperedSignature` and `TestCLI_VerifyTampered`, which the flip guts rather than reddens, `TestGovernance_VerifyMismatchedSignature`, which CODE-2 guts at S10 and whose keyed fixture needs CODE-5, `TestLoadSignatureProvider`, `TestSignCmd_PositionalArtifactResolvesAndSigns`, the `TestCLI_Sign` header sentence, and the new cases above.

**TEST-11 · e2e; the evaluation stack's signing pin.** The case lives in `test/e2e/deployment_compose_test.go`. `TestDeployCompose_RegistryServiceWiring` parses `docker-compose.yml` and asserts a named map of registry environment variables (`:86-113`), so the pin lands in that map as `"PODIUM_SIGN": func(v string) bool { return v == "none" }`. **Fails against today's `docker-compose.yml`**, whose registry environment block names no `PODIUM_SIGN` (`docker-compose.yml:126-150`). Without it CODE-11's pin is the one behavior change in this proposal with an operator-observable consequence and no assertion, and a later edit that drops it reintroduces the fresh key per recreate silently.

**TEST-12 · e2e; the documented key procedures.** DOC-1 adds runnable commands to two pages the doccov manifest already maps, and each gets the D-slug case that executes it as written. Both land in S17 with the page text, run on Linux and macOS, and run under `GOCOVERDIR` because the subject is the spawned binary.

- `TestStandardDeploy_SigningKeyFileFromAStandaloneStart` in `test/e2e/standard_deployment_test.go` (D-clustered) runs the key-generation commands `docs/deployment/clustered.md` states exactly as the page writes them, including the `env -i` scratch `HOME`. The test's own `HOME` is a `t.TempDir()` that already holds a pre-existing unsigned standalone store, produced by an earlier `PODIUM_SIGN=none` standalone start on that home with a `--layer-path` fixture. The case stops the server and asserts that the key file exists at mode `0600`, that `sign.PrivateKeyFromKeyFile` and `sign.PublicKeyFromKeyFile` both accept it and return halves of one keypair, that the test home's `.podium/standalone/podium.db` and `.podium/standalone/objects` are byte-for-byte what they were before the command ran, and that the command created no file under the test home. Byte equality is asserted rather than the rows' signatures and the rewrite marker, because it holds whatever state the pre-existing store's marker is in and it catches any open that writes. A second arm runs the page's extraction command against the key file and starts a `podium-mcp` bridge whose `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` is the command's output, and a third arm starts a registry whose `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` names that file inside a directory made read-only, which is the chart's mount; the case asserts that the registry starts and that the bridge's `load_artifact` under the `always` default serves a non-empty `signature` and loads, which pins the extraction's output format against the verifier. The `kubectl create secret` and `helm install` lines are not executed here; TEST-8's render cases and S46 cover them, and the file's Known-gaps header gains a line recording that they need a cluster.
- `TestStandaloneServer_MigrateToStandardWithTheSourceKey` in `test/e2e/standalone_server_test.go` (D-single-node) follows `TestStandaloneServer_MigrateToStandardSQLite` (`test/e2e/standalone_server_test.go:1166`): a default-mode source server signs its fixture, and `admin migrate-to-standard` writes a SQLite target. A target start on a fresh `HOME` with no key file is refused, naming `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, and writes no key; the refusal is CODE-4's persistence refusal when the target's SQLite file is outside that home's `.podium/standalone/`, and the generated-key rule otherwise, and the assertion holds for either. The case then runs the copy step `docs/deployment/single-node.md` states, placing the source's key file at the target's `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, starts the target again, and asserts that it starts and that `load_artifact` serves the migrated artifact with a `signature` that verifies under the source's public half. The refused-first order is required: the refusal runs ahead of the key loader and the pass, so the refused start writes no key and leaves the target's rewrite marker unset (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:503-505`), and the second start is still the first start after the migration.

When either documented procedure regresses, in the file format, the path, the mode, the isolation of the key-generation start, the extraction, or the copy step, its case goes red, which the render tests and the unit round trip cannot observe because neither drives the documented commands.

**TEST-13 · unit, conformance, and e2e; the deleted policy value.** It lands with CODE-13.

- `pkg/sign/policy_validate_test.go`: the valid set is `never` and `always`, and `ValidPolicy("medium-and-above")` is false. **Fails against today's code.** The `EnforceVerification` cases in `pkg/sign` drop the sensitivity argument, and `TestEnforceVerification_PolicyMediumAndAbove` (`pkg/sign/sign_test.go:40`) and TEST-1's `TestEnforceVerification_PresentSignatureVerifiedRegardlessOfSensitivity` are deleted, because their subject is the value.
- `cmd/podium-mcp/config_env_test.go`: `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=medium-and-above` makes `loadConfig` refuse with an error naming `never` and `always`; a home-global `sync.yaml` carrying `verify_signatures: medium-and-above`, with the variable unset, refuses naming both values and that file's path; the loop over accepted values in `TestLoadConfig_AcceptsKnownVerifyPolicies` (`:84`) drops the value and becomes `never` and `always`, and in the same S14 commit every arm sets `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` to a base64 Ed25519 public key, the disposition TEST-5 gives the cases whose subject is the policy, because `hermetic` pins `HOME` to an empty directory and the `always` arm otherwise meets CODE-5's startup refusal. **The first two fail against today's code.**
- `cmd/podium-mcp`: `TestLoadArtifactFromOverlay_PolicyMediumAndAboveAllowsLowSensitivity` (`cmd/podium-mcp/overlay_signature_test.go:104`) is deleted, and the overlay cases at `:138` and the `cache_mode_test.go` constructions at `:215` and `:259` move to `sign.PolicyAlways`, keeping their subjects: an overlay record loads under every value, and a cache-served high-sensitivity skill verifies.
- `test/conformance/reference_visibility_test.go:213` moves to `PolicyAlways` and drops the sensitivity argument, and keeps asserting that a tampered signature on the `medium` fixture is refused.
- `test/e2e`: `TestGovernance_MediumAndAboveBlocksUnsignedMedium` is deleted. Every other `medium-and-above` in `governance_adoption_test.go`, `signed_artifact_primitive_test.go`, `signed_artifact_helpers_test.go`, `auth_signed_load_journey_test.go`, `standard_deployment_test.go`, and `server_operations_test.go` becomes `always` in code and in the comments that state the value set. A new case beside the existing invalid-policy one (`governance_adoption_test.go:590`) starts the bridge with `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=medium-and-above` and asserts exit 2 with a message naming `never` and `always`. **Fails against today's code**, which starts the bridge.

The applier re-derives the set with `grep -rlE 'medium-and-above|MediumAndAbove' cmd pkg internal test`, which is axis A's pattern narrowed to the value, and gives every returned file one of the dispositions above.

**TEST-14 · unit, integration, and e2e; stored-row admission.** It lands with CODE-14. Each case carries `// Spec: §13.4`.

- **Unit, `pkg/registry/core/admit_test.go`.** `TestAdmit_RefusesAndAdmitsStoredRows` writes rows directly to `store.Memory` with an in-memory object store and a `sign.RegistryManagedKey` signer, and covers these arms, each asserting the sentinel with `errors.Is` and a message that names only the requested ID:
  - an ingested row: admitted, and each field CODE-14's column table marks derived equals the value parsed from its bytes;
  - bytes altered, hash unchanged: `ErrContentHashMismatch`;
  - a row at the pre-framing digest carrying an envelope minted under another key over that digest, which is the `signature_unverified` class the first-start pass leaves at its stored hash (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:199-205`): `ErrContentHashMismatch`, which pins the hash-before-signature order, because the row fails both checks and an implementation that verified the signature first would return `ErrStoredSignatureInvalid`;
  - an empty stored hash: `ErrContentHashMismatch`;
  - an object-held body altered in the object store, hash unchanged: `ErrContentHashMismatch`, which pins that admission reads object storage;
  - an object-held body absent: `ErrContentHashMismatch`; an object store whose `Get` errors or blocks past the deadline, and a registry with no object store: `ErrUnavailable`;
  - an inline ref whose `ContentHash` alone is pointed at another stored object's hash, an inline ref whose `Size` alone is edited, and an object-held ref whose `Size` alone is edited, each with `Inline`, the row's bytes, and the row's hash untouched: `ErrContentHashMismatch` each, which pins that admission binds both ref columns whatever the body's source;
  - bytes altered and the hash recomputed over them, envelope left in place: `ErrStoredSignatureInvalid`; the same with the envelope cleared: `ErrStoredSignatureMissing`; an envelope minted under another key over the correct hash: `ErrStoredSignatureInvalid`;
  - with no signer, bytes altered and the hash recomputed: admitted, which pins the hash-only mode;
  - each field CODE-14's column table marks derived (`Body`, `Type`, `Sensitivity`, `Deprecated`, `ReplacedBy`, and `AuditRedact`) edited alone: admitted, and the returned row carries the parsed value in each;
  - a sealed row given a second ref under an existing path, naming another stored object and carrying that object's hash and size, placed first and then last: `ErrContentHashMismatch` each;
  - a sealed, signed row's `ArtifactID` and `Version` rewritten to another ID and version, with bytes, hash, and envelope unchanged: admitted, which pins the selection edge row and OQ-13's default;
  - a merged child whose parent row's bytes are altered with the parent's hash unchanged, and one whose parent row carries an envelope under another key: refused, with a message that names the child and not the parent;
  - the stored parent pin cleared on a child declaring `extends:`, a pin added to a row declaring none, a pin pointed at another artifact ID, a pin pointed at a version outside a declared exact reference, and a pin pointed at a parent version whose hash a declared content-hash reference does not name: `ErrContentHashMismatch` each, with a message that names only the requested ID;
  - on a three-level chain whose requested child and middle row are sealed and correctly pinned, the root row, which declares no `extends:`, given a pin toward a row the store does not hold, and the same root given a pin pointed back at the requested child: `ErrContentHashMismatch` each, with a message that names only the requested ID, which pins that `admitChain` admits an ancestor before it follows that ancestor's pin, where today's walk returns `ErrNotFound` and `ErrInvalidArgument`;
  - a child declaring a content-hash `extends:` reference whose pinned parent's hash is not the one named, and whose own envelope is minted under another key: `ErrStoredSignatureInvalid`, which pins SPEC-10's order, because the content-hash comparison is check (4) and runs after the child's signature check (3);
  - a child whose pin agrees with a declared exact reference naming a version the store does not hold, and a sealed two-row chain whose rows declare each other and carry agreeing pins: `ErrNotFound` and `ErrInvalidArgument` respectively, each with a message that names only the requested ID;
  - a pin pointed at another stored parent version that satisfies a declared minor reference: admitted and merged over that version, which pins the accepted edge row;
  - a sealed row with empty frontmatter and an empty pin: admitted, with its stored derived fields served; the same row with a pin set: `ErrContentHashMismatch`.
- **Unit, `pkg/registry/core/admit_test.go`, the column table.** `TestAdmit_EveryRecordFieldIsDisposed` holds CODE-14's column table as a map from struct-qualified field name to disposition: a `store.ManifestRecord` field is keyed `ManifestRecord.<Name>`, including `ManifestRecord.Resources`, and a `Resources[].<Name>` row is keyed `ResourceRef.<Name>`, so `ManifestRecord.ContentHash` and `ResourceRef.ContentHash` are distinct entries. It fails when a field that `reflect.VisibleFields` returns for `store.ManifestRecord` or `store.ResourceRef` is missing from the map, and when the map names a field that does not exist, so a field added to either type fails the test until it is disposed. `TestLoadArtifact_ReadEventRedactsDerivedKeys` seals a row in `store.Memory` whose `AuditRedact` column is empty while its manifest declares `audit_redact: [ssn]`, loads it, and asserts that the emitted `artifact.loaded` event carries `ssn` in `RedactKeys`, which pins the deletion of the read-event fallback. `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidatedReadEventRedactsKeys` in the same file calls `LoadArtifact` with a `Revalidate` option that reports a bypass, on a sealed row whose `AuditRedact` column is empty while its manifest declares `audit_redact: [version]`, and on a sealed `extends:` child that declares no `audit_redact` over a parent that declares `[version]`. For each it asserts that the one emitted `artifact.loaded` event carries `version` in `RedactKeys` beside the `version` context key, which pins `revalidationRedactKeys` on a bypassed read. A third arm seals a child whose manifest declares an exact `extends:` version the store does not hold, with its pin naming that version, and asserts that the bypassed call returns `ErrNotFound` with a message that names only the requested ID. That row's pin agrees with its bytes, so a full load also returns `ErrNotFound`; the arm pins the message construction and makes no claim about a row with an ancestor pin its bytes do not declare, whose bypassed and full-load sentinels differ as CODE-14 states. It fails against CODE-14's bypass without the key set, which emits the event with no `RedactKeys`, and it also carries `// Spec: §8.2`. `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidationKeepsLoadScope` in the same file seals a row, calls `LoadArtifact` with a `Revalidate` option that reports a bypass, first for an identity whose `Scopes` are active and do not cover the artifact (`podium:load:other/*`) and then for one whose load scope names the artifact with a `@version` pin that excludes the resolved version, and asserts for each `ErrNotFound`, one `visibility.denied` audit event, and that the call's `artifact.loaded` event, which `LoadArtifact`'s deferred emit writes on every call including a denied one (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1463-1480`), carries `ResultSize` 0 and no `content_hash` key in its `Context`, which `assemble` sets only after `assembleResult` succeeds (`:1530-1541`); this pins that `assemble` consults the option after the §6.3.1 scope check; it also carries `// Spec: §6.3.1`. Each carries `// Spec: §13.4`.
- **Unit, the deadline-bounded object read.** `getWithDeadline`'s existing cases follow the function to wherever CODE-14's object-read blank places it, and the first-start pass's tests keep passing against it.
- **Integration, `pkg/registry/server`.** `TestNewFromFilesystem_AdmitsObjectHeldBody` builds a filesystem registry with a resource above `objectstore.InlineCutoff` and `WithObjectStore`, loads it, and asserts HTTP 200 with the resource in `large_resources`; it then alters the object's bytes in the object store and asserts `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`, which pins that `NewFromFilesystem` hands its object store to admission. `TestResourceDelivery_InlineRefAboveCutoffServedInlineWithObjectStore` in `pkg/registry/server/resource_delivery_test.go` seals a row whose ref above `objectstore.InlineCutoff` holds its bytes in `Inline`, as an ingest with no object store writes it, and serves the row through a registry and a server given an empty object store, the registry through `WithAdmission`. It asserts HTTP 200 with the resource in `resources` and absent from `large_resources` on the single load, and in `inline` with no `presigned_url` on the batch item. This pins that both serve paths choose the source from the admitted ref's `Inline` and not from the server's object store. Today's code presigns a key the store does not hold. It carries `// Spec: §13.4`, `// Spec: §7.2`, and `// Spec: §7.6.2`.
- **Integration, `test/integration/`.** `TestLoadArtifact_InadmissibleRowAnswersItsCode` asserts on raw response bodies that the single load (status 500) and the batch item carry `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`, `materialize.signature_invalid`, and `materialize.signature_missing` for the three refusal classes, that a row sealed at the pre-framing digest with an envelope minted under a key other than the registry's signer, which is the `signature_unverified` row the wrong-key-at-upgrade edge row describes, answers `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` on the single load and the batch item through a registry given its signer through `WithAdmission`, which pins the hash-before-signature order at the level a client observes, that a merged child refused for its parent's row carries neither the parent ID nor a `sha256:` digest, that a child whose stored pin is cleared, redirected to another artifact, or added to a row declaring no `extends:` answers `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` on the single load and the batch item, that a child whose parent row, declaring no `extends:`, is given a pin toward a row the store does not hold, and one whose parent row is given a pin pointed back at the child, each answer `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` on the single load and as the batch item's error envelope, with a body that carries neither the parent ID nor the tampered pin, where today's walk answers `registry.not_found` or `visibility.denied` and `registry.invalid_argument`, and, through a registry and server both given an object store, that an ingested row whose small inline ref has its `ContentHash` pointed at another stored object answers `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` on the batch item, that the ingested row with the object stored under its small inline ref's key replaced by other bytes yields a batch item that carries the ref's admitted bytes inline and no `presigned_url`, which pins that the batch path presigns no object admission did not read, and that the same row with that ref's `Size` raised above `objectstore.InlineCutoff` and its `ContentHash` pointed at another object answers the same on the single load, which before this change presigned on the stored size alone. `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidationSkipsAdmission` serves an object-held row through a registry whose object store is a switchable wrapper that counts reads and whose `Get` fails while the switch is set. With `Get` failing, it asserts that a full GET answers `registry.unavailable`, retryable, and that a GET whose `If-None-Match` does not match answers the same. It asserts that a HEAD answers 200 with `X-Podium-Content-Hash` and a matching `If-None-Match` answers 304, both reading no object. It then restores the wrapper's `Get`, alters the row's stored bytes with its hash unchanged, and asserts that the HEAD still answers the stored hash and reads no object, while the full GET reads the object, reaches admission's hash comparison, and answers `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`, which pins that a revalidation serves no content. Finally it clears the row's stored content hash and asserts that a GET carrying `If-None-Match: *` answers `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` and not 200, which pins that the bypass is taken for a GET only when the handler answers 304. `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidationOutsideScopeIsDenied` serves a sealed row through a server whose identity resolver returns the token's `Scopes`, as `test/integration/auth_org_isolation_test.go:64-67` builds one, and for a token whose load scope does not cover the artifact (`podium:load:other/*`) and for one whose `@version` pin excludes the resolved version sends a HEAD and a GET whose `If-None-Match` carries the row's published ETag. It asserts that each answers the status and code a full GET from the same token answers, never 304, and carries no `X-Podium-Content-Hash`, `X-Podium-Version`, or `ETag` header, and that the registry records `visibility.denied` and an `artifact.loaded` event with `ResultSize` 0 and no `content_hash` key in its `Context`. It carries `// Spec: §13.4` and `// Spec: §6.3.1`. `TestLoadArtifact_ChainOutageRefusesInlineChild` seeds an `extends:` child whose own bundled resources are all inline over a parent holding an object-held body, behind the same switchable wrapper, and with `Get` failing asserts that a full GET of the child answers `registry.unavailable` and that a HEAD of the child answers 200 and reads no object, which pins that admission reads every chain row's object-held bodies and that the staged runbook's outage impact is stated per chain.
- **E2e, `test/e2e/`.** `TestE2E_StoreEditedRowIsRefused` starts `podium serve --standalone` with the default signing mode, lets the boot ingest sign a fixture, stops the server, rewrites the row's `frontmatter` and `content_hash` in the SQLite file with the hash computed through `version.CanonicalContentHash` over the altered bytes, restarts, and asserts that `load_artifact` through `mcpExec` and `podium artifact show` are both refused with `materialize.signature_invalid`. `TestE2E_BootRehashesStoredContentHashes` (`test/e2e/boot_rehash_test.go`) keeps its `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` assertion, which the registry now returns, and its comment names the registry's §13.4 admission as the refusal. `TestHTTPAPI_LoadNonDeprecatedArtifactReturnsReplacedBy` in `test/e2e/http_api_test.go` starts a SQLite-backed registry through `startServer` (`test/e2e/helpers_test.go:431`) on a fixture whose artifact declares `replaced_by:` without `deprecated: true`, loads it through `/v1/load_artifact` and through `/v1/artifacts:batchLoad`, and asserts that each response's `replaced_by` equals the declared target and that neither carries a `deprecation_warning`. Today `replacedByOf` returns the empty SQL column for a non-deprecated row (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1886-1888`), so the test fails against today's code and pins the removal of that divergence between the deployment modes. It carries `// Spec: §4.7.4` and `// Spec: §13.4`.
- **The fixture disposition.** The fixtures that write a manifest record without ingest and then load it are found by writer: every `PutManifest(` call site, every `manifests` `INSERT`, and every `ManifestRecord{` literal in the `_test.go` files under `pkg/`, `internal/`, `cmd/`, and `test/`, including `internal/serverboot`, `test/bench`, and `test/verification`, whose record a load reaches as the requested row or a chain member. Each takes its content hash, and under a signer its envelope, from `storetest.Seal(t testing.TB, rec store.ManifestRecord, objects objectstore.Provider, signer sign.Provider) store.ManifestRecord`, a new helper in `pkg/store/storetest` that assembles each bundled-resource body as admission does, from `ref.Inline` when it is non-nil and otherwise from `objects` under `strings.TrimPrefix(ref.ContentHash, "sha256:")`, sets each inline ref's `ContentHash` and `Size` from its `Inline` bytes and each object-held ref's `Size` from the body it read, sets `ContentHash` to `"sha256:" + version.CanonicalContentHash(rec.Frontmatter, rec.SkillRaw, bodies)`, and, for a non-nil signer, sets `Signature` to the signer's envelope over it. It calls `t.Fatalf` when a body is object-held and `objects` is nil, when an object read fails, when an object-held body's hash differs from its ref's `ContentHash`, which names the key the body was read from and so cannot be rewritten, and when the signer fails, so a fixture can never be sealed over bytes admission would not read. `pkg/store/storetest` already imports `testing`, and neither `pkg/objectstore` nor `pkg/sign` imports `pkg/store/storetest`, so the helper adds no import cycle. A fixture whose record holds an object-held body passes the object store it put the body in, and the registry that serves it is built with `WithAdmission` and that same object store, per the object-store disposition below, because a registry without one refuses the body as unavailable whatever its hash. Fixtures whose records reach only search, `/v1/dependents`, or the store keep their values. A fixture with empty or unparseable frontmatter and an empty pin needs no manifest, because CODE-14 treats it as declaring no `extends:` and serves its stored columns. Where its bodies are all inline, such as `test/integration/auth_org_isolation_test.go:160-163`, `storetest.Seal` alone makes it admissible. Where it holds an object-held body, such as `pkg/registry/server/data_plane_test.go:54-61` and the parent row at `test/integration/store_retention_objects_test.go:97-103`, it is sealed with the object store holding that body and served by a registry built with `WithAdmission` and that object store, per the object-store disposition below. The cases named below are the ones whose assertions change or whose seed is not a literal:
  - `pkg/registry/server/load_artifact_session_test.go:24` and `internal/serverboot/identity_gateway_integration_test.go:151`, whose hashes come from a helper parameter and a concatenation;
  - `test/integration/auth_org_isolation_test.go` (`:162`, `:211`): the acme and globex records get distinct bytes, and the served-`content_hash` assertion compares against `storetest.Seal`'s hash for acme's record;
