  - `internal/serverboot/webui_auth_integration_test.go` (`:228`, `:413`);
  - `test/bench/latency_test.go` (`:35`, `:78`): the seed seals each record outside the timed loop;
  - `TestLifecycle_InPlaceSQLiteUpgradePreservesArtifactsAndAudit` (`test/e2e/lifecycle_journeys_test.go:321`, `:330`): the legacy row stores the sealed hash over empty frontmatter, an empty body, and no resources, admission treats its empty manifest as declaring no `extends:`, and the test keeps its HTTP 200 assertion.
  - `TestDataPlane_BatchLoadReturnsPresignedResources` (`pkg/registry/server/data_plane_test.go:118`), which asserts a `presigned_url` on `scripts/run.py`, a ref whose `Inline` the fixture sets (`:59`). It is renamed `TestDataPlane_BatchLoadPresignsOnlyObjectHeldResources` and annotated `// Spec: §7.6.2` and `// Spec: §13.4`. It asserts that `scripts/run.py` carries `inline` equal to the fixture's small bytes and no `presigned_url`, and that `data/big.bin` carries a `presigned_url` and its `content_hash`. It fails against today's code, which presigns both.
- **The object-store disposition.** The fixture finder selects by writer, and a test that ingests an object-held body through `ingest.Ingest` writes no record itself, so a second finder selects by wiring and by resource ref: `grep -rlE 'WithObjectStore|ResourcePut:|ResourceRef\{' --include='*_test.go' pkg internal cmd test`. The `ResourceRef\{` arm selects a file that writes a ref with no `Inline` although it never names an object store, whose registry admission would otherwise refuse as unavailable. In every file it returns, each registry built with `core.New` that serves a row with an object-held body, whether ingested or written directly, gains `.WithAdmission(nil, <the object store its bodies were put in>, <the §13.12 default read timeout>)` in the S13 commit, and a directly written record among them is sealed with that object store. A registry built through `server.NewFromFilesystem` needs no edit, because CODE-14 wires its object store. A file that builds no registry needs none. On the tree this document was written against the finder returns, with these dispositions:
  - `core.New` with `server.WithObjectStore`, each taking `WithAdmission`: `test/integration/data_plane_test.go` (ingested, `:46-72`), `test/integration/manifest_body_test.go`, `pkg/registry/server/data_plane_test.go` (a direct `PutManifest` with an object-held `data/big.bin`, `:45-66`, also sealed with its object store), `pkg/registry/server/resource_delivery_test.go`, `pkg/registry/server/manifest_body_test.go`, `pkg/registry/server/manifest_body_live_test.go`, `pkg/registry/server/options_test.go`, and `pkg/registry/server/identity_verify_test.go`; a registry in these files whose rows hold no object-held body needs no edit;
  - `server.NewFromFilesystem` with `WithObjectStore`, no edit: `pkg/registry/server/large_resources_test.go` and `test/e2e/http_api_test.go` (`TestHTTPAPI_LoadArtifactLargeResource` at `:462` and `TestHTTPAPI_ObjectsServesBytes` at `:1369`);
  - `core.New` with no object store, over a directly written object-held body: `test/integration/store_retention_objects_test.go`, whose parent row at `:97-103` holds `data/parent.txt` in `objStore` (`:67`) and whose registry at `:111-114` is a bare `core.New`; its directly written rows are sealed with `objStore`, and its registry gains `.WithAdmission(nil, objStore, <the §13.12 default read timeout>)`, so the pre-sweep and post-sweep loads of the child admit the parent's body;
  - an object-held body that was never put into any store: `pkg/registry/core/resources_test.go`'s `TestLoadArtifact_ReturnsResourceRefs` (`:22-35`), whose `data/big.bin` ref has no `Inline` and a placeholder hash. The test puts a body into an `objectstore.NewMemory()` under that body's hash, sets the ref's `ContentHash` and `Size` from it, seals the record with that object store, and builds its registry with `.WithAdmission(nil, <that object store>, <the §13.12 default read timeout>)`, keeping its subject, that an object-held ref surfaces in `Resources`;
  - no registry built, no edit: `pkg/registry/ingest/resources_test.go`, `pkg/registry/server/coverage_gaps_test.go`, which calls `inlineBytes` and `presignResource` on a bare `&Server{}`, and `internal/serverboot/rehash_test.go`, which drives the first-start pass and loads through no registry.
  The list records what the finder returns on one tree; the applier re-runs the finder and applies the rule to each file it returns.

## Manual validation

`test/manual-validation.md` ends at S64 today, which proposal 0026 added. This proposal lands before proposal 0027, so its scenarios are S65 through S69, and 0027's follow from S70. The numbers follow landing order and are reassigned at application time if that order changes. Each follows the document's per-scenario isolation block, numbered steps, and per-step **Expect** block.

- **S65 · First run verifies with no configuration.** The scenario runs `podium serve` on a clean `HOME`, then a `podium-mcp` load. The human reads `~/.podium/sync.yaml` for the absence of `verify_signatures`, confirms that the file carries no key material, runs `ls -l "$PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH"` and reads `-rw-------`, and reads the successful load. It catches the consumer defaults shipping without the producer default or without the shared key resolution, which an automated suite passes when its fixtures set the key explicitly.
- **S66 · Signing on an existing registry signs no stored row.** Ingest with `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, then restart with the default signing mode. The first start under `PODIUM_SIGN=none` recorded the §13.4 rewrite as complete, so the restart signs no stored row, and the registry's stored-row admission refuses the earlier version. Load it through `podium-mcp` and through `curl` on `/v1/load_artifact`, and read `materialize.signature_missing` in both error envelopes. Run `podium layer reingest`, load the earlier version again the same two ways, and read the same refusal. Read the stored `signature` column for that version from the SQLite file with `sqlite3`, and read the empty string. Then raise the artifact's `version:` in its `ARTIFACT.md`, re-ingest, load the new version and read a non-empty `signature` on it, and load the earlier version and read `materialize.signature_missing` again. It catches an implementation that claims the re-ingest alone repairs the store, and a registry that serves a row stored unsigned once signing is on.
- **S67 · `podium verify --provider noop` refuses.** The scenario catches a reintroduced permissive `Noop.Verify` at the surface an operator types.
- **S68 · A stale `never` is announced.** A machine carrying `verify_signatures: never` from an earlier standalone run loads without verification and prints the startup warning naming the file. The human reads the bridge's stderr. It catches the warning regressing to silence, which no assertion on a load result can see.
- **S69 · The registry refuses a row edited in its store.** Start `podium serve --standalone` with the default signing mode on a fixture carrying three bundled resources, a text file under 256 KB, a binary file under 256 KB, and a file above 256 KB, load the artifact through `podium-mcp` and through `curl` on `/v1/load_artifact`, and copy the consumer cache the first load wrote (`cp -R "$WORK/cache" "$WORK/cache-warm"`). Stop the server and run three edits against the SQLite file, restarting and loading after each and restoring the row before the next: append a byte to the row's `frontmatter`; set its `signature` to `''`; and restart with `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` naming a freshly generated key file, then again with the original. Each edit leaves the stored `content_hash` unchanged. After each edit, load through `curl`, and through `podium-mcp` with `PODIUM_CACHE_DIR` naming a new empty directory, so the bridge holds no copy to revalidate.
  - Expect, for the `curl` and the empty-cache `podium-mcp` loads: the human reads each response's error envelope, `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`, then `materialize.signature_missing`, then `materialize.signature_invalid` on every load under the fresh key and a successful load under the original key, each naming only the artifact.
  - After the first two edits, also load through `podium-mcp` with `PODIUM_CACHE_DIR` naming a fresh copy of `$WORK/cache-warm`. Expect a successful load of the original bytes: the default `always-revalidate` bridge sends a HEAD, the registry answers the unchanged stored hash without admission (§13.4), and the bridge serves the copy it fetched through the first, admitted load (OQ-11).
  - Before the first edit, with the server serving the unedited row, run `curl -s -X POST "$PODIUM_REGISTRY/v1/artifacts:batchLoad" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"ids":["<id>"]}'` with the fixture's artifact ID. Expect: the human reads the item's `resources` array. The small text resource carries `inline` equal to its file's bytes and no `presigned_url`. The small binary resource carries `inline` with `inline_base64: true` and no `presigned_url`. The resource above 256 KB carries a `presigned_url` and its `content_hash`, and no `inline`.

  Catches a registry that checks the hash and skips the signature, one that trusts the `signature` column's absence, and a refusal that reaches the MCP bridge but not a direct HTTP reader, which a unit test over `assembleResult` cannot distinguish from the served response. It also records that a revalidating consumer keeps serving its admitted copy while the stored hash is unchanged, so a refusal is observed through a full load. The batch step catches a batch item that presigns a small resource whose object admission never read, and a regression of the `inline` reference the `CHANGELOG.md` `Changed` entry announces to third-party clients.

The existing passages listed below stop holding and are repaired in the same change.

- **The per-scenario isolation block.** The block at `test/manual-validation.md:48-59` redirects server and client state into `$WORK` and states that "the run never touches the real `~/.podium`", while exporting neither `HOME`, `PODIUM_SIGN`, nor `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`. Under the producer default every `podium serve` in the document writes into the operator's real home twice over: `registrySigningKeyPath` falls back to `~/.podium/standalone/registry-signing.key` (`internal/serverboot/signing.go:22-31`) and `readOrCreateEd25519` generates the keypair there (`internal/serverboot/audit_anchor.go:182-189`). The block gains `export REAL_HOME="$HOME"` ahead of the `PODIUM_BIN` export, `export PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH="$WORK/registry-signing.key"`, the operator's resolved Go locations as `export GOPATH="$(go env GOPATH)" GOMODCACHE="$(go env GOMODCACHE)" GOCACHE="$(go env GOCACHE)" GOENV="$(go env GOENV)"`, the operator's Docker CLI configuration as `export DOCKER_CONFIG="${DOCKER_CONFIG:-$HOME/.docker}"`, and, on a line after all of those that expand `$HOME`, `export HOME="$WORK/home"` with `mkdir -p "$HOME"`, so the generated key and the bootstrapped `sync.yaml` both land inside `$WORK`. The order matters: `PODIUM_BIN` reads `$HOME` on `:49` and has to resolve against the operator's real home, which is what `REAL_HOME` captures before the override, and the Go and Docker exports have to be evaluated before it too. The Go toolchain resolves `GOPATH`, and with it `GOMODCACHE`, from `$HOME` when they are unset, `GOCACHE` from the user cache directory under `$HOME`, and its settings file from `GOENV` under the user config directory, and the block sets none of them today (`test/manual-validation.md:48-58`). Under the new `HOME` alone, every scenario step that runs `go run ./tools/minttoken` would start from an empty module cache, re-download the module graph into `$WORK/home/go` or fail offline, and leave a module cache Go writes read-only, so the scenario's `rm -rf "$WORK"` cleanup would fail with permission errors. The Docker CLI resolves its configuration, its current context, and its CLI plugins from `$HOME/.docker` unless `DOCKER_CONFIG` is set, and on a Docker Desktop macOS install the `compose` plugin exists only as a link under `~/.docker/cli-plugins`, with no system plugin directory, so under the new `HOME` alone `docker compose` fails with `docker: unknown command: docker compose`. `make services-up` and `make services-down` run `$(DOCKER_COMPOSE)`, which defaults to `docker compose` (`Makefile:187`, `:193-198`), so every step that brings the service stack up or down, including the cleanups, would fail as written. Every scenario starts with this block (`test/manual-validation.md:44`), so the `HOME` export reaches every passage in the document that resolves the operator's real home, and all of them are repaired in the same change under one mechanical predicate rather than a list a later addition escapes: **every `~` or `$HOME` occurrence inside a numbered scenario step, or inside the prose a step quotes, that names the checked-out repository becomes `$REAL_HOME`, every scenario step that relies on the operator's `~/.gitconfig` states its git identity inline, every scenario step that runs the Go toolchain runs under the Go exports the block now carries, and every scenario step that runs the Docker CLI (`docker`, `docker compose`, `make services-up`, `make services-down`, or `kind`) runs under the block's `DOCKER_CONFIG` export.** The occurrences that predicate reaches on the current file are enumerated below; the applier re-derives the repository-path set with `grep -n '~/projects/podium\|\$HOME/projects/podium' test/manual-validation.md`, the Docker set with `grep -n 'docker\|make services-\|kind ' test/manual-validation.md`, and applies the predicate to whatever each returns. This is the same derivation AUDIT-1 applies to the flipped defaults, on the same terms: the enumeration records the set on one tree and the command is what the applier runs. `test/manual-validation.md` is in AUDIT-1's set, so its scenario text asserting a flipped default is dispositioned under that item alongside the passages this predicate returns.

  - The repository-path occurrences in scenario steps that source `test.env` or bring the service stack up: `test/manual-validation.md:563`, `:925`, `:926`, `:1005`, `:1006`, `:1089`, `:1090`, `:1392`, `:1399`, and `:1417`, together with the prerequisite prose at `:556` that tells the operator to run the same `source` line. Each becomes `$REAL_HOME/projects/podium/...`. Without this repair `source ~/projects/podium/test.env` silently sources nothing, so `OPENAI_API_KEY`, the Postgres DSN, and the S3 credentials stay unset and the scenario runs against the wrong backends, and `cd ~/projects/podium && make services-up` fails at the `cd`. The path rewrite alone does not make the stack steps run: `make services-up` then reaches `docker compose`, which needs the block's `DOCKER_CONFIG` export, as the Docker bullet below states.
  - The steps that run the Docker CLI: `make services-up` at `test/manual-validation.md:925`, `:1005`, and `:1089`; `docker compose down -v && make services-up` at `:1392` and `docker compose ps -a` at `:1399`; the `docker exec` and `docker inspect` calls at `:1014`, `:1058`, `:1098`, `:1129`, `:1393`, and `:1396`; S21's `docker stop` step at `:1495`; the `make services-down` cleanups at `:966`, `:1452`, and `:1523`; the Keycloak `docker run`, `docker exec`, and `docker rm` steps from `:4199` onward; and S46's `docker build`, `kind create cluster`, `kind load docker-image`, `kind delete cluster`, and `docker rmi` at `:4737-4740` and `:4888-4889`. The block's `DOCKER_CONFIG` export reaches every one of them, so none takes an edit of its own, and the applier confirms each hit the Docker re-derivation returns runs after the block.
  - S36's step 2, which writes repository evidence into the checked-out tree through `~/projects/podium/test/fixtures` and reads it back through `$HOME/projects/podium/...` (`test/manual-validation.md:2848`, `:2851`, and `:2852`). All three spellings become `$REAL_HOME/projects/podium/...`, so the captured AD FS discovery document the step exists to commit lands in the repository instead of in the throwaway directory.
  - The steps that run the Go toolchain: the `go run ./tools/minttoken` calls at `test/manual-validation.md:812`, `:833`, `:834`, `:872`, `:873`, `:1910`, `:1924`, `:2454`, `:2468`, and `:2469`. The block's Go exports reach every one of them, so none takes an edit of its own; the applier re-derives the set with `grep -n 'go run\|go build\|go test\|go install' test/manual-validation.md` and confirms each hit runs after the block.
  - The `cd ~/projects/podium` at `test/manual-validation.md:21` is in the build section that runs before any scenario and before the block, so it resolves against the operator's real home already and stays as it is.

  The two `git commit` calls at `test/manual-validation.md:1221` and `:1239` pass no identity flags and stand in a scenario that exports none, so they resolve `user.email` and `user.name` from the operator's `~/.gitconfig` and abort with the unconfigured-identity error under an empty `$WORK/home`. Each gains the `-c user.email=alice@acme.com -c user.name=alice` pair that `:1491` already passes, which is the pair the scenario's own first commit uses (`:1206`). The commit at `:2313` needs no edit and is recorded here so a later reader does not stage one: its scenario exports `GIT_AUTHOR_NAME`, `GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL`, `GIT_COMMITTER_NAME`, and `GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL` in its own Prerequisites block (`test/manual-validation.md:2291-2292`), which satisfies git's identity resolution under an empty `$WORK/home`, and those values are the `podium-bot` identity the scenario states it sets deliberately (`:2287-2288`). Adding the flag pair there would override that identity for no reason. A scenario whose subject is an unsigned registry exports `PODIUM_SIGN=none` in its own steps, and S19, S64, and S66 are the scenarios that do.
- **S19, the signing scenario.** Step 5 starts a second registry deliberately "without `--sign`" (`test/manual-validation.md:1315-1328`) while `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` from step 2 is still exported, so under the new default that server signs under the key the consumer trusts and the load the scenario expects to be refused succeeds. It starts with `PODIUM_SIGN=none` and its own `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`. The Expected block's unsigned-artifact bullet (`:1354-1357`) pins `materialize.signature_invalid` with a `signature_missing:` message and becomes `materialize.signature_missing`. Step 4's key extraction, `awk '/^public:/{print $2}'` (`:1306`), is the format CODE-10 reads and stays as it is, and step 2's server-log assertion is re-checked against the new default.
- **S64, the first-start rewrite scenario.** Its subject is 0026's unsigned content-hash path, and its **Expect** blocks hold only for an unsigned row. Every `podium serve` in it starts with no `PODIUM_SIGN` (`test/manual-validation.md:7383`, `:7441`, `:7470`, `:7480`, `:7508`, and `:7517`), so under CODE-4 the step-3 ingest signs the row over the framed hash. Step 5 then writes the pre-upgrade digest into the stored `content_hash` (`:7440`) beside that envelope, and three **Expect** blocks go wrong. Step 5's load is refused by the signature gate, which runs above the content-hash check (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:1578` against `:1606`), with `materialize.signature_invalid` instead of the pinned `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` text (`:7453`). Step 6's target start runs the pass with a signer, the copied row's signature does not verify over the old digest, and the pass classifies it `signature_unverified` and leaves it (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:199-204`, `:343-346`), so the summary no longer reads the pinned `rehash: 1 rewritten, 1 already migrated, 0 signature_unverified` (`:7496`). Step 7 fails the same way against `:7525`. Every `podium serve` in S64, including both target starts, exports `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, and every `podium-mcp` load in it sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never`, With CODE-14 in place, step 5's load is refused by the registry's stored-row admission before the consumer's check runs. Step 5 keeps its commands and its `grep -c rehash` expectation, and its **Expect** block (`:7451-7456`) replaces the verbatim `recomputed … does not match served …` line: the printed error begins with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`, names `close-reporting/variance`, and contains no `sha256:` digest, and `$WORK/srv2.log` records the admission refusal with the artifact ID and its version. "A successful load here means the consumer is not recomputing the digest." becomes "A successful load here means the registry is not checking stored-row admission." The **Goal**'s third clause (`:7344-7345`), **Covers** (`:7347-7350`), and step 5's lead paragraph (`:7422-7432`) attribute the refusal to the registry's §13.4 stored-row admission. S64's text carries none of axis A's tokens and matches no axis-B finder, so the audit's read of `test/manual-validation.md` has no line to select it by, and it is named here.
- **S38's step 1.** It today reads "Run the isolation block, then add the signing-key override. Without it `--sign registry-key` writes into the operator's real `~/.podium`" and exports `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` itself (`test/manual-validation.md:3165-3169`). The hazard is no longer specific to that flag, and the block now carries the override, so the step keeps its number and its position and becomes "Run the isolation block", with its duplicated export and its `--sign registry-key` condition removed.
- **The signing-mode guidance.** The "Watch out for" paragraph stating that `podium serve` "enables ingest signing only through `--sign registry-key`" (`test/manual-validation.md:3155-3158`) becomes false and states the new default: signing is on unless `PODIUM_SIGN=none` or `--sign none` turns it off.
- **Every scenario that runs `podium-mcp` without exporting a verify key.** Each either exports `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` or supplies the key, on the predicate the audit before S12 states.
- **S46, the chart-install scenario.** CODE-8 makes `signing.secretName` a `required` value for every render, so every chart invocation in the scenario fails at render time until it names a Secret, on the same terms TEST-8 applies to `test/chart/render_test.go`. The scenario gains a step, before the install, that runs the key-generation commands DOC-1 states in `docs/deployment/clustered.md`, with the scratch directory under `$WORK`, and then creates the Secret in the kind cluster from the resulting file. The commands carry their own `env -i` scratch `HOME`, so the step does not rely on the isolation block to keep the start off the operator's store. Each of the scenario's chart invocations then passes `--set signing.secretName=<name>`: the `helm template` at `test/manual-validation.md:4800`, the `helm install` at `:4811`, and the two `helm upgrade` calls at `:4831` and `:4867`. `:4800` passes it so that the render reaches the API server and the only refusal its **Expect** block can read is still the `envFrom[0].secretRef.name: Required value` one it exists to catch, rather than the competing `signing.secretName` refusal. `:4867` passes it so that step 7's negative control still reaches a pod in `CrashLoopBackOff` logging `config.identity_provider_unverified`, rather than failing at render time before any pod starts. The scenario's prose is reached by AUDIT-1's B3 finder, which returns every render site across `test/manual-validation.md`, `docs/`, and `deploy/`, and by a predicate on top of it, because B3 matches a line carrying `helm template`, `helm install`, or `helm upgrade` and a sentence stating what a bare render produces need carry none of those: **every line B3 returns is read together with the paragraph it sits in and, when the line sits inside a fenced code block, with the prose paragraphs that follow that block up to the next heading, and every sentence of those paragraphs stating what an unparameterized chart render produces is restated to say that a bare render now fails and to attribute the rendered values to a render that names a Secret.** This is AUDIT-1's B3 paragraph rule, stated once there and applied here. The predicate is what a later prose addition cannot escape; B3 is what finds the paragraph to apply it to. On the current file B3 returns one line inside the scenario's framing paragraph, `test/manual-validation.md:4720`, and the predicate then reaches that whole paragraph, `test/manual-validation.md:4719-4729`, which states that a bare `helm install` with no overrides renders `PODIUM_REGISTRY_STORE=postgres`, `PODIUM_OBJECT_STORE=s3`, and `PODIUM_IDENTITY_PROVIDER=oidc-jwt` from the secret `existingSecret` names, and then describes the pod that crash-loops on that configuration. Under CODE-8 a bare render fails first, naming `signing.secretName`, so the paragraph states that refusal and attributes the three backend selections, and the crash-loop that follows from them, to a render carrying `--set signing.secretName=<name>` and no other override. The paragraph's conclusion, that the scenario stands up Postgres and an S3-compatible store in the cluster, is unchanged.

## Documentation changes

**DOC-1.** The pages below are the documentation share of AUDIT-1's audit set, taken across both axes: the pages axis A returns under `docs/`, plus the pages axis B's finders return there, `deploy/runbook.md`, which B4 and B6 return and the assignment rule gives S17, and `README.md` and `site/src/content/landing.ts`, which B1's tier arm returns and the assignment rule gives S17. The list records that share on the tree this document was written against. The applier re-derives it rather than trusting the list, and reconciles in both directions: a page the re-derivation returns that is absent below takes the same treatment, and a page listed below that the re-derivation does not return is still edited.

- `docs/deployment/progressive-adoption.md`: the "Month 3: enforce signing for `sensitivity: high`" section, whose step sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=medium-and-above` (`:80`), is restated around the two values: the registry signs every artifact by default, so the stage sets `always`, and `never` fits only a registry running with `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, because a signing registry's §13.4 stored-row admission refuses every row stored unsigned to every reader whatever the consumer's policy, and turning signing on over a store holding unsigned rows makes each of them unloadable until a new version of it is ingested; a deployment that means to sign keeps signing on from Day 0. The section heading drops the sensitivity threshold. The paragraphs describing the default chain are restated as well, including `:21`, a B6 hit, whose statement that signature verification runs in each consumer's MCP server gains that a signing registry's §13.4 admission verifies each stored signature for every reader, and including the claim that every artifact at this stage is `sensitivity: low` so no policy triggers a check, which is exactly what stops being true for a signed artifact. The sentence at `:80` stating that loading an unsigned `sensitivity: high` artifact fails with `materialize.signature_invalid` names `materialize.signature_missing`, a B4 hit. The Day 0 bullet at `:19`, which offers `podium serve --strict` against Postgres and object storage, a B5 hit, states that such a start also sets `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` to a key file on persistent storage, or sets `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, because a registry with signing on, a Postgres store, and no key path is refused at start (§13.12). The same bullet states that choosing `PODIUM_SIGN=none` at Day 0 and turning signing on later makes every artifact ingested before the switch unloadable, for every reader, until a new version of it is ingested, so a deployment that means to sign keeps signing on from Day 0. The "Alternate ordering" bullet at `:122`, which enables signing on day 1 for a high-sensitivity catalog, states that signing is already on from Day 0 by default and carries the same warning for a registry that started with `PODIUM_SIGN=none`. The "Compliance-driven" bullet at `:120`, which jumps from Day 0 to Month 3's signing posture, is restated with it: Month 3's posture is setting `always` against a registry that already signs.
- `docs/deployment/integrations.md`: the metadata-store section (`:28-31`), a B5 hit, gains a sentence after the SQLite and Postgres bullets stating that moving the SQLite file out of `~/.podium/standalone/` with `PODIUM_SQLITE_PATH`, or selecting Postgres, also requires `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` naming a key file on the store's persistent storage, or `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, and that a registry started with signing on and neither is refused at start, naming both (§13.12). The page's introduction (`:9`), which says each backend is selectable through an environment variable or the matching `registry.yaml` key, links to that sentence for the metadata store, because `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` has no `registry.yaml` key (SPEC-5's §13.12 row).
- `docs/deployment/vector-backends.md`: the clustered configuration example (`:75-106`), whose `store:` block selects Postgres at `:81`, a B5 hit, gains a sentence in the paragraph after the block (`:108`) stating that the registry's environment also carries `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` naming the deployment's one signing key file, or `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, because the variable has no config-file key and the registry refuses to start with signing on, a Postgres store, and no key path; it links to the key-generation procedure on `docs/deployment/clustered.md`.
- `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md`: the Backup and restore list (`docs/deployment/operator-guide.md:89-93`), which names Postgres and object storage alone, gains a bullet for the registry signing key, the file at `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` or the Secret the Helm chart mounts there, stating that it is backed up with the store it signs because every row it signed is refused by the registry's §13.4 stored-row admission, to every reader and under any consumer policy, once it is lost, per SPEC-5's §13.3 bullet; the quarterly restore drill's step 1 (`:98-99`) starts the non-production registry with `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` naming a copy of the production key restored from that backup, because a registry with signing on, a Postgres store, and no key path is refused at start by CODE-4's persistence refusal, and the drill thereby also exercises the key backup; the paragraph after the drill (`:111`) is restated whole, because CODE-5 falsifies each of its sentences: it states that `podium verify` defaults to `registry-managed` and resolves the public key from `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, or, when that variable is unset, from the `public:` line of the key file at `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, per §4.7.9; that step 5 runs with the production registry's public key in `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, extracted as `docs/deployment/clustered.md` shows; that `--provider noop` is refused for every envelope; and that a Sigstore-signed artifact still takes `--provider sigstore-keyless` with `PODIUM_SIGSTORE_TRUST_ROOT_PEM_FILE`. The sentence that `--provider registry-managed` loads no public key and the detour through a consumer are removed; the sensitivity-enforcement row (`:166`), which names the `medium-and-above` fallback and becomes the `always` fallback with `never` and `always` as the two values, and whose fleet-wide hunt for `never` becomes a one-machine cleanup the bridge now announces, and which, beside its existing statement that `podium sync` runs no signature check (`docs/deployment/operator-guide.md:166`), gains a sentence stating that the Python and TypeScript SDKs verify no signature and do not read `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES`, so the `always` default governs `podium-mcp` alone; a new `### Signing failures` subsection, appended to `## Common operational pitfalls` (`docs/deployment/operator-guide.md:179-189`) after its last bullet, because the page has no troubleshooting section and no `materialize.signature_invalid` bullet other than the escalation bullet at `:217`; every bullet this entry names from here through the persistence-refusal bullet lands in that subsection. It holds a bullet for the `podium-mcp` startup refusal `config.signature_provider_unavailable` and its missing-key cause, per the §6.9 refusal row SPEC-4 stages, and its relation to `materialize.signature_invalid`; a bullet for `materialize.signature_missing` and for which refusals suppress the local read event, stating that a load the §6.6 step-2 verification refuses records no local `artifact.loaded` event, that a load whose inline-resource decode or large-resource fetch fails records none either, so an object-storage outage leaves the local stream silent for every large-resource load where it previously carried one line each, and that a load the §4.4.1 sandbox or runtime gate refuses still records one; a bullet for a registry whose key file is present but is not the key that signed its stored rows, which is not refused at startup, leaves every such row at its stored hash with one log line per row, and is not repaired by restoring the right key alone; the bullet states the repair 0026's upgrade note already gives (`CHANGELOG.md:87-91`), which is restoring the backup the upgrade order takes and starting with the key that signed the rows, so the rewrite runs again and re-signs them at the cost of every write made since that backup, and it states that a new version of each affected artifact is the repair only where that backup or that key no longer exists; and a bullet for a registry whose key file is lost after the §13.4 rewrite has completed, which is also not refused at startup, because the generated-key guard returns once the rewrite's marker is set. Such a start generates a fresh keypair, signs new ingests under it, and logs nothing about the retired key, while every row the retired key signed is refused by the registry's stored-row admission with `materialize.signature_invalid`, to every reader and under any consumer policy, so setting `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never` does not make those rows load. The repair is stopping the registry, restoring the key file from the backup, and starting it again, because the registry loads its signing key once at start. A further bullet states CODE-4's persistence refusal: a registry with signing on and no `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` refuses to start unless its store is the SQLite store beside the default key, the error names `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` and `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, and the published container image run with its default store keeps the key and the store together, as the container-image edge row records. The bullet distinguishes that refusal from the two §13.4 refusals by message, as the `CHANGELOG.md` bullet below does: the persistence refusal says the key would be generated under the process's home, outside the directory that holds the store, fires whether or not the store holds a signed row, and is cleared by setting `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` on the store's persistent storage or setting `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, while only the §13.4 refusals mean that the store holds signed rows and call for the key that signed them.
- `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md`'s hardening checklist gains a paragraph on what stored-row admission does not cover against a party that can write the metadata store without the signing key: it can withhold, relabel, re-layer, or roll back an admissible row, and it can delete the first-start rewrite's completion record so the next start signs the unsigned rows it wrote. The paragraph names restricting write access to the metadata store and its backups as the mitigation, and states both as known limits.
- `docs/deployment/clustered.md`: the **Signing.** bullet (`:35`) is restated whole. Its first sentence, "Sigstore-keyless (preferred) or a registry-managed key (fallback).", becomes a statement that the registry signs at ingest with its registry-managed key by default and that no registry signing mode produces a Sigstore-keyless envelope (§4.7.9), a B1 hit; the sentence naming `medium-and-above` as the typical setting is restated to name `always`, the default, and `never` as the two values; the `helm install` command gains `--set signing.secretName=...`, preceded by the commands that generate a key file with a standalone registry's first start and create the Secret from it, which TEST-12's D-clustered case executes. The key-generation start runs under `env -i` with `PATH`, `HOME` set to a fresh scratch directory (`mktemp -d`), and `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` naming a file in that directory, and the page states why: the first start of this release runs the one-shot §13.4 rewrite on whatever store it opens, so a start on the operator's own home would sign the operator's personal standalone store under the cluster key and spend its one rewrite (CODE-8). The same block gives the extraction of the consumer's verification key, `awk '/^public:/{print $2}' <key file>`, states that the value is the base64 text after the `public:` prefix, without the prefix, which `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` requires because `sign.PublicKeyFromBase64` decodes the whole value (`pkg/sign/registry_managed.go:47-55`), and states that every consumer of the deployment sets it in `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, which the "For each consumer" list (`docs/deployment/clustered.md:190-193`) also names. This is the one page that carries the procedure; `docs/consuming/configure-your-harness.md` and `docs/deployment/single-node.md`'s client-side section link to it; a sentence states that an upgrade of a store holding signed rows uses the key that signed them, and that `signing.mode=none` turns signing off; a sentence states that every registry process serving one store resolves `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` to the same key file, which the chart's Secret mount satisfies and a hand-rolled deployment must arrange, and that a hand-rolled deployment that sets no key path is refused at start; the evaluation-stack paragraph (`docs/deployment/clustered.md:97`) states that `docker-compose.yml` pins signing off and that a consumer of it sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never`; the paragraph that follows the install block and opens "The chart installs with its defaults." (`docs/deployment/clustered.md:121`) states that a render naming no signing Secret fails with the message naming `signing.secretName`, and that the chart installs with its other defaults once `signing.secretName` names a Secret or `signing.mode=none` is set; and the always-rendered variable list in the paragraph after it (`docs/deployment/clustered.md:123`) gains `PODIUM_SIGN`, which renders on every install under both modes, and `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, which renders whenever `signing.mode` is `registry-key`, so an operator who puts either in `existingSecret` is told that the rendered value shadows it. The chart-install scenario in `test/manual-validation.md` gains the same Secret step.
- `docs/consuming/configure-your-harness.md`: the MCP env-var contract table (`:29-32`) gains `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES`, `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER`, `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, and `PODIUM_SIGSTORE_TRUST_ROOT_PEM_FILE` with the new defaults, `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` on the consumer reading SPEC-3's new row states and `PODIUM_SIGSTORE_TRUST_ROOT_PEM_FILE` as the trust root a `sigstore-keyless` consumer requires, and each per-harness recipe's `env` block (the first at `:151-155`) gains the two the consumer of a standard deployment must set. A sentence states that a standalone consumer, running under the same user account as its registry, resolves the verification key from the registry's own key file and sets neither, and that a consumer whose registry runs with a non-default `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` sets that variable too. The `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` row links to the extraction procedure on `docs/deployment/clustered.md` for the value. Without this a consumer configured exactly as documented refuses to start.
- `docs/deployment/single-node.md`: the client-side MCP entry (`:79-89`), for the same reason, with a link to the extraction procedure on `docs/deployment/clustered.md` for a client on another machine, and the Operational-notes backup bullet (`:185`), whose "`registry-signing.key` when `--sign registry-key` is set" condition becomes false: the file is written on every default first run and is absent only under `--sign none` or `PODIUM_SIGN=none`. The bullet also states what losing the key costs, scoped to the window in which the §13.4 guard fires: the key file belongs in the backup; a start before the §13.4 rewrite has completed is refused when the key is missing and the store holds signed rows (`refuseGeneratedSigningKey`, `internal/serverboot/rehash.go:506-547`); once the rewrite has completed that guard returns without refusing (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:522-528`), so a start with the key missing generates a fresh keypair and proceeds, and every row the retired key signed is then refused by the registry's §13.4 stored-row admission with `materialize.signature_invalid`, to every reader and under any consumer policy. The repair is stopping the registry, restoring the key file from the backup, and starting it again, because the registry loads its signing key once at start.
- The migration sections of `docs/deployment/single-node.md` and `docs/deployment/clustered.md`, and `docs/reference/cli.md`'s `podium admin migrate-to-standard` section: a step that places the source's registry signing key at the target's `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, replacing any key file already there, before the target's first start after the migration; on a chart deployment the Secret is created from the source's key file. The pages state both outcomes of skipping it: a target with no key file is refused at start, and a target holding any other key, including one an earlier start generated or a chart Secret created from a different key, starts and classifies every copied signed row `signature_unverified`, leaving it untouched. The outcome then depends on the source's state. A row the source had already framed keeps the framed digest and the source's envelope. While the target runs with the other key, the target registry refuses every such row with `materialize.signature_invalid` to every reader and under any consumer policy, because its §13.4 admission verifies the stored envelope under the target's key. Placing the source's key at the target's `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, replacing the other key, and restarting the target, which loads its key only at start (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:903`), repairs those rows with no new version. A row a source that never started on this release still held at the pre-framing hash stays at that hash, and placing the source's key alone does not repair it. The pages state the repair the migration text already gives (`docs/reference/cli.md:627-629`, `CHANGELOG.md:96-108`): recreate the target store empty and re-run the command with the source's key at the target's `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, because the command clears the target's rewrite marker and the next start rewrites and re-signs the copied rows. A new version of each affected artifact is the repair only where the source store or its key no longer exists. The runnable copy step lives in `docs/deployment/single-node.md`'s migration section alone; the `clustered.md` and `cli.md` sections state the requirement and link to it rather than repeating the command, so TEST-12's one D-single-node case executes the one runnable copy.
- `docs/deployment/single-node.md`'s "What the tier omits" list: the **Transparency-log anchoring.** bullet (`:156`), whose sentence "Sigstore-keyless signing requires public OIDC infrastructure." is a B1 hit, is restated to name transparency-log anchoring as the §8.6 audit-integrity feature that anchors the audit chain head to a public transparency log and needs that log's infrastructure, and to drop the signing clause, because a single-node registry signs with its registry-managed key by default.
- The tier rows that list signing with a transparency log among the clustered tier's additions, all B1 hits on the tier arm: `README.md:139`, `docs/overview.md:72`, `docs/deployment/index.md:15`, `docs/getting-started/how-it-works.md:230`, `docs/deployment/local.md:162`, and `site/src/content/landing.ts:391`. Each replaces "signing with a transparency log" (on the landing page, "Signing and transparency log") with transparency-log anchoring of the audit chain, which §13.11.3 lists among the features that need a remote server (`spec/13-deployment.md:347`), so no row presents signing as a clustered addition while every tier signs with the registry-managed key by default. `README.md` and `site/src/content/landing.ts` fall outside `docs/` and are assigned to S17 by AUDIT-1's assignment rule.
- `docs/deployment/extending.md`: the `SignatureProvider` row, including the claim that registry-side ingest signing is off unless the registry starts with `--sign registry-key`.
- `docs/reference/cli.md`: the `--sign` flag's accepted values, the `--provider` defaults on `podium sign` and `podium verify`, the `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` row (`:768`), which lists `never` and `always (default)`, and the sentence at `:689` stating that the `registry-managed` provider "uses a per-org key managed by the registry", which is the docs mirror of the §4.7.9 sentence SPEC-1 replaces. It is restated to say that the provider uses one Ed25519 keypair per registry deployment, at `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` (default `~/.podium/standalone/registry-signing.key`), shared by every process serving that store and across every tenant it serves, per §4.7.9. The sentence is a B1 hit, on the key-scope claim, and the per-organization scope it states is what makes the shared-key obligations in §13.12, CODE-8's single mounted Secret, and the multi-replica edge row read as unnecessary. `docs/deployment/progressive-adoption.md:81`, which calls the same key "a tenant signing key managed by the registry", is a B1 hit on the same key-scope claim and takes the same restatement; the B1 hits under `docs/` that assert the tier claim are staged in the tier-row bullet below. The `--sign none` description states that a consumer on that machine sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never`, because no key file is generated under `--sign none`, and on a home that carries none the bridge otherwise refuses to start with `config.signature_provider_unavailable`. The `podium sign` (`docs/reference/cli.md:674`) and `podium verify` (`:691`) sections state the key-material contract SPEC-1 makes normative and CODE-5's CLI arm implements: the `registry-managed` provider resolves the public half from `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` when that variable is set, and otherwise from the `public:` line of the key file at `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` (default `~/.podium/standalone/registry-signing.key`); a set variable that does not decode is an error naming it; `podium sign` takes the `private:` line of the same file and does not read `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, and `podium verify` reads no private key; an invocation that cannot resolve the key it needs exits non-zero naming `config.signature_provider_unavailable`; and `--provider noop` signs a placeholder that `podium verify` always refuses. The `--provider` rows (`:687`, `:705`) stop describing `noop` as the fallback. The page's Environment variables table (`:755`) gains rows for `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, and `PODIUM_SIGSTORE_TRUST_ROOT_PEM_FILE`, the last stating the CLI behavior in SPEC-3's row (`podium sign` and `podium verify` read it under `--provider sigstore-keyless` whatever the policy, an unset or unreadable value does not refuse the command, and `podium verify` then refuses each envelope as `materialize.signature_invalid`) and omitting the MCP server's start refusal, beside its existing `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES` row (`:768`), so a bare `podium verify` on a machine with no key file meets a documented refusal.
- `docs/reference/cli.md`'s environment table: the `PODIUM_MIGRATION_OBJECT_READ_TIMEOUT` row (`:767`), a B6 hit, names the stored-row admission check's object-storage reads beside the first-start rewrite's, per SPEC-10's §13.12 row.
- `docs/reference/error-codes.md`: the `materialize.signature_missing` description, which cites the old default policy; the `config.invalid_sign_mode` row (`:84`), which today reads "a value other than `registry-key`" and would tell an operator that the documented way to turn signing off raises the error, restated as a value other than `registry-key` or `none` with an unset variable meaning `registry-key`; the existing `config.signature_provider_unavailable` row (`:88`), amended rather than added, to cover the startup refusal and the missing key path; the `config.invalid` row (`:78`), whose published description enumerates `sync.yaml` `kind: marketplace` malformations and closes by attributing the code to `podium sync --config`, which SPEC-3 and CODE-5 give a second emitter: the row also names an unrecognized `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER` refused at `podium-mcp` startup, and the closing attribution stops reading as the code's only source. The existing code is reused rather than a dedicated one minted, because `config.invalid` is the §6.10 code for a configuration value that is not one of the recognized ones and it is already on the axis (`tools/matrix/matrices.go:91`), so a new code would add a §6.10 cell, a matrix annotation, and a docs row for a refusal an existing code already names. Reusing it is what makes the docs row an amendment rather than an addition. Finally, the page gains spec cross-references on `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` and `ingest.sign_failed`, and the `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`, `materialize.signature_invalid`, and `materialize.signature_missing` rows each state their registry-side source, the §13.4 stored-row admission check, which answers them with HTTP 500 on `load_artifact` and, on each `artifacts:batchLoad` item, as the item's error envelope (`status: "error"`, `retryable: false`) inside the batch's 200 response (`pkg/registry/server/batch_load.go:101`), not retryable on either path, with a message naming only the requested artifact. These rows include `:118` and `:121`, both B6 hits. The consumer-side sentence of the `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` row (`:121`) is kept beside the registry-side one; proposal 0027 retargets that sentence alone to its delivery record, and every other clause of these rows is this proposal's (Relationship to proposal 0027).
- `docs/getting-started/concepts.md` and `docs/deployment/access-control.md`: one line each naming where the policy applies.
- `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md`'s hardening checklist tamper check, the last sentence of the sensitivity-enforcement row (`:166`), a B6 hit, is restated in terms that hold on proposal 0027's tree:
  - A response altered in transit fails at the consumer with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`, and one whose signature was altered fails with `materialize.signature_invalid`.
  - The registry admits each stored row before it serves the row's content (§13.4), recomputing the content hash, and checking each bundled resource's stored hash and size against its bytes, before it verifies the signature, and its refusal reaches every reader's full load, `podium sync` and the SDKs included. A HEAD and a matching conditional GET are answered without admission, so a consumer that already holds the row under an unchanged content hash keeps serving that copy. On any registry, a row whose stored bytes were altered while its stored content hash was left unchanged is refused with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`. On a signing registry, a row altered together with its hash is refused with `materialize.signature_invalid`, and one whose stored signature was removed with `materialize.signature_missing`. A registry with `PODIUM_SIGN=none` serves a row altered together with its hash. Admission checks a row's content and does not attest which row answers a request, so a party that can write the metadata store can withhold a row, relabel an admissible row, or have an older admissible row served in place of a newer one (§13.4).
  - A bundled-resource body held in object storage and altered there is refused by the registry on a full load with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`. The object-storage copy of a resource the row holds inline is never served, because the registry serves that resource inline from the admitted row on `load_artifact` and on each `artifacts:batchLoad` item. An object-held body altered after the registry admitted the row, and a manifest-body object replaced at its key, which admission does not read, fail at `podium-mcp`'s fetch with `materialize.fetch_failed` and a `content hash mismatch` message (`cmd/podium-mcp/large_resources.go:42-47`, `:95-101`). `podium sync` and the SDKs compare no fetched body with its hash and receive such a body unchecked, so an operator who needs that check reads the artifact through `podium-mcp`.
  - The check's test sentence has the operator run the tamper check through a full load, either a direct HTTP `load_artifact` GET with no `If-None-Match` or a consumer whose cache is empty, and expects the refusal from the registry.
- `docs/deployment/operator-guide.md`'s escalation bullet (`:217`), a B6 hit, which reads repeated `materialize.signature_invalid` "for authored artifacts" as a broken signing pipeline or tampering, is restated in terms that hold on proposal 0027's tree, and the "authored artifacts" qualifier is removed:
  - A refusal the registry logs (CODE-14 logs each one, naming the tenant, artifact ID, version, and reason) of `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`, `materialize.signature_invalid`, or `materialize.signature_missing` is checked for an upgrade or key cause first. The operator compares the artifact ID and version against the first start's per-row summary (`CHANGELOG.md:49-51`); a row the summary names is upgrade residue, repaired as the upgrade note states, and is not escalated. The operator also compares the registry's key file with the backed-up key, because admission refuses every row signed under a key the registry no longer holds, and repairs a mismatch as `deploy/runbook.md`'s signature-storm entry states.
  - A registry refusal for a row the summary does not name, under the key that signed the store, means the row's stored bytes were altered, in the metadata store or in object storage, or an object-held body is missing. The operator reads the refusal's logged reason, checks the object store for the row's bodies, and then escalates.
  - A consumer-returned `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`, or `materialize.fetch_failed` naming a large-resource `content hash mismatch`, means a response or an object-storage body was altered after the registry admitted the row, and is escalated.
  - A consumer-returned `materialize.signature_invalid` or `materialize.signature_missing` is first checked against the consumer's configuration: `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER` is `registry-managed`, the key it resolves is the registry's current public key, and the registry does not run with `PODIUM_SIGN=none` under an `always` policy. With that configuration correct, it is escalated as a forged or stripped signature.
- `deploy/runbook.md`'s "Object-storage outage" scenario (`:52-66`), B6 hits at `:52` and `:57`, which §13.2 requires to give detection signals, impact, and mitigation (`spec/13-deployment.md:37`). It is this proposal's passage (Relationship to proposal 0027) and is restated whole in terms that hold on proposal 0027's tree:
  - **Detection** gains `registry.unavailable` on `load_artifact` and on `artifacts:batchLoad` items, `materialize.fetch_failed` on a consumer's fetch of a manifest body or a large resource, and the object-storage dashboard, beside the existing `materialize.*` signal.
  - **Impact** is restated. A full `load_artifact` of a row holding a bundled-resource body in object storage is refused with `registry.unavailable` by the §13.4 admission read. A full load whose served manifest document goes through the manifest-body channel (a document above the 256 KiB inline cutoff, `pkg/objectstore/objectstore.go:41`) is refused with `registry.unavailable` when the registry's `Stat` or `Put` fails (`pkg/registry/server/server.go:1093-1097`, `:1204-1221`). A full load whose presigned manifest-body or large-resource fetch fails returns `materialize.fetch_failed` at the consumer (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:1567-1569`, `cmd/podium-mcp/large_resources.go:90-94`). Admission reads the object-held bodies of every row in the requested artifact's `extends:` chain, parents included, so a full load is refused with `registry.unavailable` when any row of that chain holds an object-held body, and only a load whose whole chain holds inline bundled resources, and whose manifest document is below the cutoff, loads in full. A HEAD revalidation and a matching conditional GET read no object storage and are answered from the stored content hash (§13.4), so a consumer in any cache mode keeps serving a cached record whose content hash is unchanged, and an `offline-first` or `offline-only` consumer serves a resolution hit without calling the registry (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:1330-1388`).
  - A sentence beside the Impact paragraph separates an outage from an object store opened at the wrong root or bucket. That store reports every body absent rather than failing its reads, so admission refuses every object-held row with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` rather than `registry.unavailable` (CODE-14's class table). The repair is to point the object store at the right root or bucket and restart, and the first start after an upgrade that met this condition runs the rewrite again, because the wrong-root guard held its marker back.
  - **Mitigation** keeps steps 1 and 3. Step 2's "Cached content remains usable." (`:62-64`) stays, and it holds in every cache mode for an artifact whose content hash is unchanged. The entry recommends no cache-mode change.
- `docs/consuming/handling-artifact-responses.md`: the host-side error table, whose `materialize.signature_invalid` row (`:253`) is the only signature row, gains a `materialize.signature_missing` row with the same handling, because CODE-3 moves every missing-signature refusal onto that code. It is a B4 hit.
- `deploy/runbook.md`: the "Signature verification failure storm" entry (`deploy/runbook.md:119-130`), the runbook §13.2 says ships with the chart (`spec/13-deployment.md:37`). Its detection line names `materialize.signature_invalid` alone and becomes `materialize.signature_invalid` and `materialize.signature_missing`, plus the `podium-mcp` startup refusal `config.signature_provider_unavailable`, because the storms the new defaults create surface on the second and third. Its mitigation replaces step 2, which names a `podium admin rotate-key` command that exists nowhere in the tree, and step 3, which states that affected ingests can be replayed once the keys are correct, with the repair SPEC-1 and SPEC-5 state: for a row whose stored hash is already the framed digest, which covers a key lost or rotated after the §13.4 rewrite and a migrated row the source had already framed, restore the key that signed the rows where it still exists and restart the registry, which loads its key only at start; for a row the rewrite left at the pre-framing hash as `signature_unverified`, restoring the key alone does not repair it, so restore the backup the upgrade order takes and start with the key that signed the rows (`CHANGELOG.md:87-91`), or, on a migration target, recreate the target store empty and re-run `podium admin migrate-to-standard` with the source's key in place (`docs/reference/cli.md:627-629`), either of which runs the rewrite again; and wherever neither route is available or the key no longer exists, ingest a new version of each affected artifact, because no other path re-signs a stored row. It is a B4 hit, assigned to S17 with the documentation share.
- `docs/reference/http-api.md:228`, a B7 hit: the sentence "A larger resource is returned in `large_resources`, a map of path to a presigned URL into object storage that the consumer fetches directly" gains the §4.1 exception: a resource the registry holds inline on the manifest record, which includes every resource of a row ingested while no object store was configured, is returned in `resources` at any size. The `load_artifacts` (bulk) section states that each resource reference carries `path` and `content_hash`, and either `presigned_url` or, for a resource the registry holds inline, `inline`, with `inline_base64: true` when the bytes are not valid UTF-8 (§7.6.2).
- `docs/consuming/handling-artifact-responses.md:49` and `:51`, B7 hits: the "Through the SDK" paragraph's "A resource above the inline cutoff arrives as a reference rather than bytes" and the "The inline cutoff" paragraph's "A larger resource travels as a presigned URL into content-addressed object storage" each gain the same exception, stated once in the `:51` paragraph and linked from `:49`.
- `docs/consuming/custom-via-sdk.md:96`, a B7 hit: "a resource above the 256 KB inline cutoff arrives as a presigned reference" gains the same exception.
- `docs/consuming/browsing-the-catalog.md:147`, a B7 hit: the `load_artifact` section's "The MCP server fetches each resource from the registry, inline at or below the 256 KB cutoff and through a presigned URL above it" gains the same exception, that a resource the registry holds inline, which includes every resource of a row ingested while no object store was configured, arrives inline at any size, and links to the `handling-artifact-responses.md:51` paragraph.
- `docs/authoring/bundled-resources.md:56`, a B7 hit: the Inline cutoff row's "Above it, the response carries a URL to fetch them from." gains "unless the registry holds the resource inline, as it does for every resource of an artifact ingested while no object store was configured".
- `docs/getting-started/how-it-works.md:300` and `docs/getting-started/concepts.md:267`, B7 hits: the sentence placing a resource above the cutoff in object storage gains the clause "on a registry with an object store", and a following sentence states that a registry without one keeps such a resource inline and returns it inline.
- `docs/authoring/frontmatter-reference.md:91`, a B7 hit: the `replaced_by` row's "Surfaced when `load_artifact` returns the deprecation warning." becomes "Returned by every `load_artifact` of the artifact, and alongside the deprecation warning when the artifact is deprecated (§4.7.4).".
- `CHANGELOG.md`: a `Removed` entry for `medium-and-above`, stating that a consumer configured with it, in the environment or in `sync.yaml`, refuses to start naming `never` and `always`, and that the operator picks `always`, or `never` against a registry running with `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, because on a signing registry the §13.4 stored-row admission refuses every row stored unsigned to every reader whatever the consumer's policy; a `Changed` entry naming the default flips, the registry-before-consumers roll order, and the machines that keep a stale `never`; a `Fixed` entry for the verification bypass and the reordering; a note that the first-start rewrite this release already performs for the content-hash change is also what attaches envelopes to stored rows, written as an extension of 0026's upgrade note; a statement that a registry enabling signing after that start signs no row it already stores, so an artifact gains an envelope only when a new version of it is ingested; and a replacement for 0026's class-to-code paragraph at `CHANGELOG.md:68-77`, B4 and B6 hits at `:68` and `:76`, which maps each class of row the first-start rewrite leaves untouched onto the code an upgraded consumer reports. From CODE-14 on, the registry's admission refuses each such row before any consumer check runs, so the replacement states the registry's codes from CODE-14's table: `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` for a row still at the previous release's digest, which covers an `unreproducible` row and a `signature_unverified` row at that digest, and for a `body_missing` row; `registry.unavailable` for a `body_unavailable` row while an object read fails or times out; `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` for a row the wrong-root guard reclassified as `body_unavailable`, and for every object-held row, while the object store reports their bodies absent, which is repaired by pointing the object store at the right root or bucket and restarting, because that class holds the rewrite's marker back and the start runs the rewrite again; and, on a signing registry, `materialize.signature_invalid` for a `signature_unverified` row at the framed digest and `materialize.signature_missing` for a row stored unsigned. Each refusal reaches every reader, the SDKs and `podium sync` included, and none depends on the consumer's policy. 0026's "Rolling the consumers" (`CHANGELOG.md:109-124`) and "Clear each consumer's cache" (`CHANGELOG.md:126-143`) paragraphs state a consumer's outcome during a mixed-binary roll and on a pre-upgrade cache bucket. Proposal 0027's delivery record decides those outcomes in the same release, so both paragraphs are 0027's passages and take no edit here (Relationship to proposal 0027). 0027's DOC-1 carries this proposal's consumer-side outcomes into them: the `config.signature_provider_unavailable` startup refusal, and `materialize.signature_missing` for a missing signature above `never`. The entry also states, beside the note's key-provisioning sentence, CODE-4's persistence refusal: a registry with signing on and no `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` refuses to start unless its store is the SQLite store beside the default key, so a Postgres deployment or a moved SQLite store sets the key path on the store's persistent storage or sets `PODIUM_SIGN=none` before its upgrade start. The entry restates 0026's refusal diagnosis in "Read the summary line the start logs" (`CHANGELOG.md:51-55`), which says that any refused start naming `PODIUM_SIGN` and `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` means the store holds signed rows and prescribes restarting with `PODIUM_SIGN=registry-key` and the key that signed them. The persistence refusal names the same variables and fires on the upgrade start of every Postgres or moved-SQLite deployment with no key path, including one whose store holds no signed row, so the restated paragraph distinguishes the refusals by their messages: the persistence refusal says the key would be generated under the process's home, outside the directory that holds the store, and is cleared by setting `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` on the store's persistent storage or setting `PODIUM_SIGN=none`; the signed-rows diagnosis applies only to the §13.4 refusals, the no-signer refusal, whose remedy becomes removing `PODIUM_SIGN=none` and pointing `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` at the key that signed the rows (CODE-4's edited wording), and the generated-key refusal, which says `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` names no file. The per-class codes in that replacement are the registry's, and proposal 0027 does not change them. The release note for CODE-14 states that a row altered in the store is refused to every reader on a signing registry, that turning signing on after the upgrade makes every row stored unsigned unloadable until a new version is ingested, and that during an object-storage outage a full load is refused with `registry.unavailable` when any row of the artifact's `extends:` chain, parents included, holds an object-held body, while a HEAD revalidation and a matching conditional GET still answer. The release note for CODE-14 also carries a `Changed` entry. On a deployment with an object store, an `artifacts:batchLoad` item carries each bundled resource at or below the 256 KB inline cutoff in the reference's `inline` field, base64-encoded with `inline_base64` when it is binary, where it carried a `presigned_url`. A client that reads only `presigned_url` must read `inline` as well. The Python and TypeScript SDKs already do. A second `Changed` entry states that, on a SQLite or Postgres store, `load_artifact` and each `artifacts:batchLoad` item return an artifact's declared `replaced_by` whether or not the artifact is deprecated, where they returned it only for a deprecated artifact, which matches the in-memory store (§4.7.4).

`OPERATIONS.md` names none of `PODIUM_SIGN`, `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES`, `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER`, or `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`; its only signing content is the Sigstore live-test section (`OPERATIONS.md:278-280`), which gates a manual smoke test on `PODIUM_SIGSTORE_*` and does not become wrong here. It carries no edit.

`grep -n -i sign tools/doccov/manifest.yaml` returns nothing, and every page DOC-1 adds a runnable command to is already in the manifest: `docs/deployment/clustered.md` maps to D-clustered and `docs/deployment/single-node.md` to D-single-node (`tools/doccov/manifest.yaml:47-50`), and `docs/reference/cli.md` to D-cli (`:29`). No manifest entry is added. The new runnable commands are the key-generation, verification-key extraction, and Secret-creation commands on `clustered.md` and the migration key-copy step on `single-node.md`, and TEST-12 stages the D-slug e2e case that executes each, per `.claude/rules/spec-driven-development.md`. The `kubectl create secret` and `helm install` lines stay covered by TEST-8's render cases and by S46, and TEST-12 records them in `test/e2e/standard_deployment_test.go`'s Known-gaps header as needing a cluster.

## Resolved in adversarial review

### Pass 1 (2026-09-19, automated)

- **CODE-4 claimed a `<path>.pub` file no code writes.** `readOrCreateEd25519` writes one file and no sibling. The `.pub` is dropped: CODE-6 points `signature_verify_key_path` at the keypair file, CODE-10 adds the one reader for its `public:` line, and CODE-4, CODE-8, SPEC-5's §13.10 row, and OQ-3 are restated to match.
- **The "last non-empty line is the base64 key" format was not what the writer produces.** The last line is `public: <base64>`, which `sign.PublicKeyFromBase64` rejects. CODE-5, the staged §7.5.2 paragraph, and CODE-10 now state one format, the `public:`-prefixed line, and one unit test pins the writer and the reader together.
- **SPEC-6 anchored on a `verify_signatures` key §7.5.2 does not document, while SPEC-5 deleted its only spec mention.** SPEC-6 is re-anchored on the `defaults:` schema block at `spec/07-external-integration.md:331-336` and stages both keys there, in the block and in the prose.
- **Every staged spec anchor outside spec/06 pointed at the wrong lines.** SPEC-1 (`spec/04-artifact-model.md:897-904`), the §13.10 Signing row (`:153`), the verification bullet (`:207`), and the §13.12 insertion point (`:495`) are re-anchored, each quoting the text it replaces so further drift is detectable at application time.
- **CODE-3 and TEST-2 claimed a move the code has already made.** The signature gate already precedes `decodeInlineResources` and `fetchLargeResources`. CODE-3 now states today's order and moves the gate above `auditLoadArtifact` alone, and the test is restated as a regression pin that passes today.
- **The signature-before-resource-fetch property had no spec basis.** §6.6 step 1 still owns the fetch, so the early-refusal claim is dropped from CODE-3 and the no-presigned-URL assertion from TEST-2, and SPEC-2 confines its edit to list item 2. No edit to `docs/getting-started/how-it-works.md` is needed.
- **CODE-2 broke two suites that assert a `Noop` signature verifies.** `test/conformance/reference_visibility_test.go` and `pkg/registry/core/extends_served_record_test.go` move onto a `RegistryManagedKey` fixture under TEST-1, and CODE-1's sentence no longer reads as a claim that the conformance test needs no change.
- **The audit before the consumer default flip selected the wrong set.** The refusal is a startup refusal, so the audit is over every process that starts `podium-mcp` with no verification material. The recipe names the disposition (`mcpServerEnv` sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never`) and the tests whose assertions encode the old defaults.
- **The shared-isolated-home premise was false.** The server runs under `t.TempDir()` and the bridge under `cmdharness.IsolatedHome(t)`, and `cmdharness.Run` suppresses the bootstrap. The watch-out states that, and TEST-6 states how the journey is wired.
- **CODE-6 wrote the key path unconditionally.** The bootstrapped body now branches on the resolved signing mode, derives the path through `registrySigningKeyPath`, and writes the registry pointer alone under `none`. The `PODIUM_SIGN=none` edge row is split into the standalone and the remote case, and TEST-6 gains the `none` arm.
- **`podium verify` could not verify under any configuration.** CODE-5 extends the key resolution to `cmd/podium/sign.go`, SPEC-1 states it, and the edge table and TEST-9 carry the working invocation.
- **The CLI sign and verify tests had no disposition.** TEST-9 lists each case in `cmd/podium/sign_test.go`, `cmd/podium/flags_after_positional_test.go`, `test/e2e/cli_reference_test.go`, and `test/e2e/governance_adoption_test.go` with its disposition and where its `spec: §4.7.9` citation lands, and adds the cases pinning the new refusals.
- **`config.invalid_sign_mode` was falsified and unregistered.** DOC-1 stages the `docs/reference/error-codes.md:84` row, the `config.signature_provider_unavailable` item is an amendment of the existing row, and CODE-7 adds the code to the §6.10 axis with its annotated test.
- **single-node.md's backup bullet conditioned the signing key on `--sign registry-key`.** DOC-1 stages `docs/deployment/single-node.md:185` with the new default and the rebuild repair.
- **S19 became unrunnable.** MAN-1 stages its step 5 under `PODIUM_SIGN=none` with its own key path, and its Expected block onto `materialize.signature_missing`.
- **The manual "Watch out for" paragraph on `--sign registry-key` became false.** MAN-1 stages `test/manual-validation.md:3155-3158`.
- **S68 could not distinguish the fixed build.** The scenario now invalidates the envelope alongside the `sensitivity` downgrade, and each step carries an **Expect** block.
- **SPEC-4 edited a §6.10 enumeration that does not exist.** The consumer-side codes land in the §6.9 rows, `ingest.sign_failed` gains a spec/07 ingest-case row and a mention in the ingest `Errors.` sentence, and `tools/matrix/matrices.go` is named as the axis's registration surface.
- **The §6.4 overlay path runs no signature check.** SPEC-1 and SPEC-2 scope both checks to registry-served artifacts and state the carve-out, with an edge row and a TEST-6 case.
- **CODE-3's reorder broke `test/integration/mcp_audit_trace_test.go`.** TEST-2 carries its disposition: the stub serves a matching `content_hash` and the stale comment is rewritten.
- **The §7.6.2 prefetch wrote signature-less buckets.** SPEC-9 and CODE-9 carry `sensitivity` and `signature` on the envelope, TEST-10 pins a warmed bucket, and the stale comment above `putExtras` is replaced.
- **The generated-key start refusal is keyed on the file's absence.** CODE-4, the edge table, and DOC-1 state that a wrong key file is not refused, that the pass leaves each row at its stored hash with one log line, and that the repair is the rebuild, and TEST-4 pins the wrong-key start.

- **CODE-4 demanded opposite behavior for an empty `PODIUM_SIGN`.** The validator accepts the empty string, `registry-key`, and `none`, and the empty string resolves to `registry-key`.
- **A multi-replica deployment generated a key per replica.** SPEC-1 and SPEC-5's §13.12 subsection state the shared-key requirement, with an edge row and a DOC-1 sentence in `docs/deployment/clustered.md`.
- **The shipped `docker-compose.yml` would mint a fresh key per recreate.** CODE-11 pins `PODIUM_SIGN: "none"` on the evaluation stack, with an edge row and a DOC-1 sentence.
- **§9.1's `SignatureProvider` built-in stayed "Sigstore-keyless".** SPEC-7 amends the row.
- **Every published MCP host-configuration recipe would stop starting.** SPEC-8 stages §6.11's recipes and DOC-1 stages `docs/consuming/configure-your-harness.md` and `docs/deployment/single-node.md`'s client-side section.
- **0026's CHANGELOG note on a `signature_unverified` row became false.** DOC-1 names `CHANGELOG.md:74-77` and stages its replacement, and CODE-4 attributes the change to CODE-1 rather than to the reorder.
- **DOC-1 named an `OPERATIONS.md` signing block that does not exist.** The entry is removed and the reason recorded.
- **The `PODIUM_SIGN=none` standalone arm claimed a consumer that starts.** CODE-5's startup predicate fires on exactly that configuration: the `always` default, the `registry-managed` default, and no key from either source. CODE-6, the edge row, and TEST-6's `none` case now state one outcome, a bridge refused with `config.signature_provider_unavailable` until the operator sets `PODIUM_VERIFY_SIGNATURES=never`, and DOC-1's `docs/reference/cli.md` entry carries the pairing.
- **TEST-9 paired two `cmd/podium/sign_test.go` ranges with the wrong names.** The names are `TestSignCmd_PositionalArtifactResolvesAndSigns` at `:35-49`, `TestVerifyCmd_PositionalArtifactVerifiesStoredSignature` at `:53-64`, and `TestSignVerifyCmd_ContentHashFormRoundTrips` at `:97-115`. Each bullet now leads with the name so a further drift in the numbers is harmless.
- **Two sign-then-verify round trips were dispositioned as `--provider noop`, which CODE-2 makes fail.** `TestSignVerifyCmd_ContentHashFormRoundTrips` and `test/e2e/cli_reference_test.go:1869-1875` `TestCLI_VerifyValid` both require exit 0 on the verify half, so both take the `TestGovernance_VerifyRoundTrip` disposition, and the e2e case is added to TEST-9's list.
- **SPEC-2 asserted that the resource fetch precedes the verification step.** The signature gate already runs above `decodeInlineResources` and `fetchLargeResources` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:1578` against `:1594` and `:1600`), which CODE-3 states. The clause now describes the edit, which is confined to list item 2, rather than the order.
- **S11 landed TEST-3's `config.signature_provider_unavailable` test before CODE-5 built the refusal.** The §6.10 cells are split across the steps that make each reachable: `ingest.sign_failed` in S11, `config.invalid_sign_mode` in S12 beside CODE-4, and `config.signature_provider_unavailable` in S14 beside CODE-5. CODE-7 and TEST-3 record the split so `matrix-audit` passes at every step boundary.
- **S16 named a `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` pin CODE-11 does not stage.** Under `PODIUM_SIGN=none` no key is loaded, so the step is restated to the one variable CODE-11 sets.

### Pass 2 (2026-09-19, automated)

- **spec/14 kept Sigstore-keyless as a selectable registry signing mode.** §14.9 step 3 and §14.12 step 4 offer a mode SPEC-1 and SPEC-7 abolish, and no edit list touched spec/14. SPEC-1 now carries both sentences, the S1 checklist entry names them, and the Summary's spec edit list names §14.
