The sentence is scoped to an artifact the registry reports at `low`, which is what every other statement of it in this document already says.
- **§14.7 enumerated an MCP consumer's variables without the verification key.** SPEC-8 now carries §14.7 step 1, which defers to the §6.11 recipe, and §14.5's operator setup, which gains the key-distribution step §14.6 through §14.8 inherit. The S8 checklist entry names both.
- **CODE-6 landed the key path on no machine whose `~/.podium/sync.yaml` `podium init` had written.** `writeFileIfAbsent` returns without writing when the file exists, and the documented standalone journeys run `podium init --global` first. CODE-6 gains `ensureSignatureVerifyKeyPath`, specified with the state it reads, its one caller, what happens when it does not fire, and its test; an edge row, a TEST-6 case, and the stale-file edge row follow.
- **Two CLI tamper negative controls went vacuous rather than red.** `TestVerifyCmd_PositionalArtifactRejectsTamperedSignature` and `TestCLI_VerifyTampered` lose their subject once the provider default flips, one because the keyless `registry-managed` provider refuses every input through the same exit and message a mismatch takes, and the other because the sign half stops emitting an envelope. TEST-9 carries both onto a `registry-managed` key fixture, asserting the exit code together with the message.
- **`pkg/sync.Defaults.VerifySignatures`'s doc comment stated the write CODE-6 deletes.** The comment also cited the §13.10 bullet SPEC-5 deletes. CODE-6 rewrites it in the same change, citing §7.5.2 and §6.2.
- **CODE-9 did not make every prefetched bucket verify.** The batch envelope carries no resource bytes and no `raw_frontmatter`, which the §6.6 step-2 content-hash check hashes, so a warmed bucket for a resource-bearing artifact or an `extends:` child is still refused. The Summary, the decision, the watch-out, SPEC-9's bullet, CODE-9, the edge table, and TEST-10 now scope the claim to the signature half and state the residue, and OQ-7 carries the question with the constraint any solution satisfies.
- **The e2e cases that enforce a policy with no key had no disposition.** `assertUnsignedHighRefused` and `TestArtifactResponse_SignatureInvalid` match the literal `signature_invalid` on an unsigned load, which CODE-3 falsifies, and every one of these cases builds its environment inline so CODE-5 refuses its bridge. The watch-out is corrected and TEST-2 enumerates each site with its asserted code and its verification material.
- **The new `config.invalid` emitter was not mirrored in the error-code reference.** DOC-1 gains the `docs/reference/error-codes.md:78` row, amended to name an unrecognized `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_PROVIDER` refused at `podium-mcp` startup, with the reason the existing code is reused rather than a dedicated one minted.

### Redesign 1 (2026-09-19, automated)

Two mechanisms were redesigned in one pass, because each had churned across successive review rounds without converging.

- **The standalone credential handover.** `defaults.signature_verify_key_path` was a mechanism this proposal invented, and five rounds each repaired one of its defects. It is replaced by one path resolver in `pkg/sign`, `KeyFilePath`, which `internal/serverboot`'s `registrySigningKeyPath` becomes a delegate of. The consumer falls back to the file the registry writes, so the boundary is that file's `0600` mode under one user account and the resolution order is stated once, in §4.7.9.
- **The hand-written edit-site inventories.** Every round found a file asserting a flipped default that no edit list named, and repaired that one file. AUDIT-1 replaces the inventories in CODE-1, CODE-5, CODE-6, DOC-1, and TEST-9 with a derivation rule: a stage-1 sweep produces the audit set, S9.5 assigns each file to the step that falsifies its statement, and S17 closes on a re-run of the sweep.

The redesign deleted the `sync.yaml` key in every one of its statements, the `pkg/sync.Defaults.SignatureVerifyKeyPath` field, the §7.5.2 schema line and prose for the key, `ensureSignatureVerifyKeyPath` with its indentation detection, re-parse validation, replay pin, already-set guard, and non-firing branches, the conditional key-path write in the bootstrapped body, the workspace-then-home scope resolution for key material in both the bridge and the CLI, the restatement of the resolution order across §4.7.9, §6.2, and §7.5.2, the eight unit cases that existed to pin the deleted helper, `TestStandaloneBootstrap_AddsTheKeyPathToAnInitWrittenFile`, and the duplicate statements of the keypair round-trip test in CODE-10 and TEST-6, which TEST-4 now owns.

What the redesign added: §6.2 gains a `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` row, because a consumer process now reads the variable; §13.12's description of that variable states the registry and the consumer readings separately; the edge table gains the stale-local-key row and the second `PODIUM_SIGN=none` row; TEST-5 gains `TestSignedArtifact_StaleLocalKeyRefusesTheLoad`; and the checklist gains S9.5, which S10, S12, S14, and S17 depend on.

Open decisions the redesign recorded:

- **D1. Whether to delete `medium-and-above` rather than keep it as a rollout posture.** The default is to keep it, and the question is staged as OQ-8.
- **D2. Whether the consumer falls back to the registry's key file at all.** The default is the shared resolver. Its cost is that a machine which once ran a standalone registry, later pointed at a remote one, starts on the stale key and refuses each load rather than refusing at startup. The edge table records that outcome and TEST-5 pins it.
- **D3. Whether AUDIT-1 is a deliverable with its own step or an exit condition on S17 alone.** The default is the deliverable with S9.5, because S10, S12, and S14 apply the assignment it produces.

### Redesign 2 (2026-09-20, automated)

Two mechanisms were redesigned in one pass, because each had churned across successive review rounds without converging.

**CODE-5's verification-capability check.** The startup predicate existed only because the provider was built twice: once by a capability check at startup and once per load by `buildSignatureProvider` (`cmd/podium-mcp/main.go:2008`). The redesign resolves the verifier once, in `loadConfig`, through one resolver, `resolveVerifier(policy, name)`, into a `config.verifier` field, and states the outcome for every combination of policy, provider, and material in one table. `resolveVerifier` carries the policy axis the existing `buildSignatureProvider(name)` cannot see, and that function becomes its provider-construction half, which is what lets the `noop`-under-an-enforcing-policy refusal come out of the same resolution as the rest. The failed resolution is the startup refusal, so there is no predicate to keep in step with the resolver, no per-load file read, and no discarded trust-root read error. Under `never` no material is resolved at all, which collapses the whole `never` column to one cell.

**AUDIT-1's derivation rule.** Redesign 1 replaced the hand-written edit-site inventories with a token sweep. Two rounds since then each found a file asserting a falsified claim that the token sweep cannot return, and each repaired it with a new exception: an S17 confirmation step for pages publishing an MCP `env` block, and a hand-staged `docs/reference/cli.md:689` bullet with its own justification. Both are the earlier repair copied to a newly discovered site. The rule is restated on a claim inventory with a finder per claim. Axis A is the token sweep, unchanged. Axis B finds a file that asserts a claim in prose naming no identifier, or by omitting a variable the claim requires, with one command per claim.

The redesign deleted the startup capability predicate and its fixed decision, CODE-5's implementor's choice, the per-load provider construction, the two-arm re-derivations in four edge-case rows, stage 2 of the sweep, the S17 `env`-block carve-out, DOC-1's "plus the one page the sweep cannot return" preamble, and DOC-1's `cli.md` justification sentence. MAN-1's S46 prose predicate is kept, re-pointed at the paragraph B3 returns a line inside.

On its first run axis B returned `pkg/sign/registry_managed.go:14-19`, which states a per-org key path, an Ed25519 keypair per tenant managed via a secret backend, and rotation handled by that backend. SPEC-1 falsifies all three, the file carries no axis-A token, and no edit list in this document named it. Its falsifying deliverable is spec-only, so the assignment rule's spec-only arm routes the file to S10, the first code step landing a `pkg/sign` deliverable, rather than to a spec step that has already run.

Open decisions the redesign recorded:

- **D4. Whether axis B's `[Ss]igstore-keyless` token belongs in B1.** The default is to keep it, because `spec/09-extensibility.md:26` and `spec/14-common-scenarios.md:180` are reachable no other way, at the cost of four files that match on the provider's own identity and dismiss on the first read.
- **D5. Whether a key file replaced under a running bridge should take effect without a restart.** The default is the restart, because `config.verifier` is resolved once like every other `config` field, and a per-load re-read is the I/O the redesign removed.
- **D6. Whether the in-process `&config{}` constructions belong in AUDIT-1's inventory as a ninth claim.** The default is no. They are Go constructions rather than prose claims, axis B's finders return prose, and CODE-5's in-process-construction bullet states the rule that selects the constructions needing `verifier` and the finder the S14 applier re-derives the set with over the tree at S14, which includes the constructions S11 and S13 add.
- **D7. Whether S16 keeps an AUDIT-1 share.** The default is to keep the assignment rule and record the empty share, because axis A returns no `docker-compose.yml` and B3 does not reach it on this tree, while the rule costs one clause and covers a later compose-side claim.

### Pass 14 (2026-09-19, automated)

- **The fallback for a set but malformed `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY` was stated two ways.** SPEC-3's §6.2 rows consulted the key file only when the variable was unset, while SPEC-4's §6.9 row and CODE-5's table refused only when neither source resolved, which let a mistyped remote key start the bridge on a stale local key. ### Scope cut by decision (2026-09-22)

- Proposal 0027 owns served-record verification; this proposal shrinks to the policy, the defaults, and the keys, and lands after 0027. The Relationship to proposal 0027 section lists what moved and names the 0027 surfaces this proposal depends on.
- SPEC-1's policy paragraph carries the digest-agnostic constraint: the governed signature is verified before any gate reads manifest content, and every returned, cached, or written body is covered by the verified digest.
- CODE-3 keeps the read-event move and the missing-signature code, restated against 0027's reordered `deliverLoadArtifact`; CODE-12 keeps the mirror's verification without the body binding.
- OQ-7 is marked superseded, and OQ-10 records an assessment of which stored-signature passages 0027 makes obsolete, for the owner to decide.

### Owner decisions (2026-09-23)

- This proposal lands after 0026 and before 0027, and all three ship in one release. Every dependency on a 0027 surface is removed: CODE-3 reorders today's `deliverLoadArtifact`, SPEC-1 names the served `signature` as the governed signature, and the Relationship section names what 0027 retargets.
- Stored-row admission moves here from 0027 in option (b)'s form, as SPEC-10, CODE-14, and TEST-14, with a Decision, a Fixed decision, edge rows, a manual scenario, and doc bullets.
- OQ-10 is resolved: stored signatures are required again, because admission verifies them.
- OQ-7 is resolved as moved: 0027 removes the prefetch.
- The manual scenarios are renumbered to S65 through S69 for the landing order, and S69 is added.

### Pass 19 (2026-09-23, automated)

- **Admission left the uncovered `ExtendsPin` column in charge of which rows form the `extends:` chain.** `assembleResult` serves a row unmerged when its pin is empty and `resolveExtendsChain` walks whatever parent the pin names (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1672`, `:1708-1717`), and neither the hash nor the signature covers the pin. SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph and CODE-14's check now compare each row's pin with the `extends:` reference its admitted bytes declare (empty exactly when none is declared, the declared parent ID, a version `version.Resolve` matches, and for a content-hash reference the pinned parent's admitted hash), refusing a mismatch with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`. - **Registries built without `WithAdmission` but with an object store refused every object-held body, and no disposition covered the ingest-built tests.** `server.NewFromFilesystem` now calls `WithAdmission(nil, probe.objectStore, objectstore.DefaultReadTimeout, 0)`, `pkg/objectstore` gains `DefaultReadTimeout` as the one statement of the 30-second default, TEST-14 gains `TestNewFromFilesystem_AdmitsObjectHeldBody` and an object-store disposition with its own wiring finder and the files it returns, and the Watch-out and S13 name both.
- **`storetest.Seal` could not compute the digest of a record whose bundled body is held in object storage.** Seal now takes `t testing.TB` and the object store and assembles bodies as admission does, failing the test when an object-held body cannot be read, and TEST-14 states that such a fixture is served by a registry holding the same object store.

- **The Relationship to 0026 misstated the generated-key refusal.** The clause now reads that the pass refuses the start when signing is on, the key file is absent, the rewrite has not completed, and a stored row carries a signature, matching the Fixed decision and `refuseGeneratedSigningKey` (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:506-545`).
- **SPEC-5 and DOC-1 attributed untouched-row and lost-key refusals to the consumer.** SPEC-5's §13.3 bullet and its lead-in, the §13.12 lost-key and untouched-row sentences, and DOC-1's operator-guide and `single-node.md` key-loss bullets now attribute each refusal to the registry's §13.4 stored-row admission, for every reader under any consumer policy, and defer to §13.4 for the codes. The §13.12 sentence on replicas signing under different keys states that each replica's admission refuses the others' rows.
- **The class table said restoring the object repairs a `body_missing` row.** The row now states that such a row is refused with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` whether or not the object is restored, because the pass does not hold its marker back and does not run again (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:351-354`, `:407-411`), names the repairs, and routes a row the wrong-root guard reclassified to the `body_unavailable` row.

- **SPEC-10 mapped every failed object read to `registry.unavailable`.** The §13.4 paragraph now refuses a body object storage reports as absent with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` and every other failed or timed-out read with `registry.unavailable`, matching CODE-14 and TEST-14, and CODE-14 states that the hash comparison runs before the pin and signature checks.
- **A restored signing key was said to admit a row on its next load.** CODE-14's cache bullet, the key-loss edge row, the Fixed decision on repairs, SPEC-5's §13.12 text, and DOC-1's operator-guide, `single-node.md`, hardening-paragraph, and runbook bullets now state that the registry loads its signer once at boot (`internal/serverboot/signing.go:46-72`, `internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:903`), so the repair is to stop the registry, restore the key file, and start it again.
- **DOC-1's `custom-via-sdk.md` sentence said SDK consumers load an artifact whatever its signing state.** The sentence now states that the SDKs run no client-side check and read no policy, and that the registry's admission still refuses a row with a missing or invalid stored signature on a signing registry and a row whose bytes do not reproduce its hash on any registry, returning the code to the SDK caller.
- **The runbook's object-storage outage entry kept "Cached content remains usable".** DOC-1 now restates the entry's Impact paragraph and mitigation step 2: an object-held row's load is refused in full unless the replica remembers a verdict, an `always-revalidate` consumer does not fall back to its cache for that refusal, `offline-first` and `offline-only` consumers keep serving cached content, and all-inline rows are unaffected.
- **The staged progressive-adoption and CHANGELOG advice to run `never` during partial signing coverage no longer worked.** The Decision on the policy split, the `medium-and-above` edge row, DOC-1's `progressive-adoption.md` bullet (including the Day 0 option and the alternate-ordering bullet at `:120`), and the CHANGELOG `Removed` entry now limit `never` to a registry running with `PODIUM_SIGN=none`, state that turning signing on over unsigned rows makes each unloadable until a new version is ingested, and state that a deployment that means to sign keeps signing on from Day 0.
- **CODE-14's pin check refused every row whose frontmatter is empty or does not parse.** `manifest.ParseArtifact` returns `ErrNoFrontmatter` for empty input (`pkg/manifest/parse.go:41-45`), so the lifecycle legacy row and the direct-store fixtures TEST-14 repairs with `storetest.Seal` alone were refused. CODE-14 now treats such a row as declaring no `extends:`, admits it when its pin is empty, and serves its stored type, body, and sensitivity through `parsedArtifact`'s existing fallback (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1822-1833`); SPEC-10, the column-edit edge row, TEST-14's unit arms, its fixture disposition, and its lifecycle sub-bullet state the same rule.
- **The problem analysis still had an upgraded consumer refusing the rows the first-start pass leaves untouched.** The paragraph on enabling signing later now attributes the refusal to the registry's §13.4 stored-row admission for every reader, with the codes in CODE-14's class table, matching SPEC-5's §13.12 and §13.3 text.

### Redesign 1 (2026-09-23, automated)

The redesign respecified stored-row admission once, derived its documentation claims through a new finder, and assigned one owner to each passage this proposal and proposal 0027 both edit.

- **Areas redesigned.** SPEC-10 and CODE-14, with every site that restated them: the Summary, the Fixed decisions, the Watch-out bullets, S5a, S9.5, S13, S17, the ordering constraints, the Current state, the admission Decision, SPEC-1's consumer-key sentence, SPEC-5's §13.3 and §13.12 text, the edge rows, TEST-14, MAN-1's S69, DOC-1's `progressive-adoption.md`, `cli.md`, `error-codes.md`, operator-guide, runbook, and `CHANGELOG.md` bullets, OQ-10, the Non-goals, and the Relationship to proposal 0027. AUDIT-1 gains four claim-inventory rows and the B6 finder, and SPEC-10 and CODE-14 join its list of falsifying deliverables. The Fixed decision on AUDIT-1 states that a `CHANGELOG.md` release-note entry for a behavior this release adds is written from its deliverable and is not an edit site.
- **Reason.** The verdict cache existed to make revalidation cheap, and it added a tuning variable, a staleness rule, a concurrency surface, and a divergence between replicas. A HEAD and a matching conditional GET return no content, and the consumer then serves only a bucket it holds under that hash for that artifact ID, fetched through an earlier admitted load, so admitting them protected nothing. CODE-14 now gives `LoadArtifactOptions` a `Revalidate` predicate that `handleLoadArtifact` sets, so those two requests are answered from the resolved row before `assembleResult` runs, and the predicate computes the validator through the expression the ETag branch uses. Admission's codes, order, and repairs were restated in more than ten places, and each now points at SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph, CODE-14's class table, or SPEC-5's §13.12 repair. Admission names no identifier the tree carries, so no finder returned the pages it falsified, and AUDIT-1 dismissed `docs/reference/error-codes.md:121` although DOC-1 edits it. Both proposals staged conflicting text for the runbook outage entry, the operator-guide tamper and escalation passages, `error-codes.md`, and four `CHANGELOG.md` paragraphs.
- **Deleted.** The verdict cache with its key, LRU, mutex, and replica-divergence caveat; `PODIUM_ADMISSION_CACHE_ENTRIES`, its §13.12 row, `cfg.admissionCacheEntries`, and the `envInt` read with its `0` rule; `WithAdmission`'s fourth parameter; `TestAdmit_VerdictCache`, `TestAdmit_VerdictCacheConcurrentLoads`, `TestLoadConfig_AdmissionCacheEntries`, and `TestLoadArtifact_ObjectStoreOutageRefusesUnrememberedRows`, which `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidationSkipsAdmission` replaces; the integration assertion that a HEAD for a refused row answers the refusal; five of the six store-edit edge rows, merged into one; the operator-guide paragraph beside the Backup and restore list, whose content DOC-1's Backup and restore bullet already carries; the `docs/consuming/custom-via-sdk.md` bullet, which no finder returns; and this proposal's restatement of `CHANGELOG.md`'s "Rolling the consumers" and "Clear each consumer's cache" paragraphs, which the ownership table gives 0027.
- **Open decisions recorded.** OD-1: revalidation is answered without admission and no verdict is kept, so a consumer that holds a copy of a row fetched while the row was admissible keeps serving it after the row becomes inadmissible, for as long as the stored hash is unchanged, and MAN-1's S69 and DOC-1's tamper check observe a refusal through a full load. The alternative admits revalidation behind a bounded verdict cache. OD-2: `CHANGELOG.md`'s "Rolling the consumers" and "Clear each consumer's cache" are 0027's passages, which depends on 0027's DOC-1 carrying this proposal's consumer-side outcomes into them. The alternative has this proposal restate both paragraphs and 0027 edit them as this proposal leaves them. Each default is the smaller mechanism, and the owner confirms each.
- **Follow-up in proposal 0027.** The Relationship to proposal 0027 lists the edits 0027 needs: its CODE-3 and CODE-5 keep the `Revalidate` validator equal to the full-GET validator on 0027's tree, its TEST-4 gains the HEAD ETag assertion, and its runbook, operator-guide, `error-codes.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, "Mint at serve time", and outage edge-row text drop the passages this proposal owns and the remembered verdict. This redesign does not edit 0027.

- **No test pinned that the `Revalidate` bypass keeps the §6.3.1 load-scope check.** The scope gate and the admission call share the `assemble` closure (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1518-1530`), and every bypass test used an identity with no active scopes. TEST-14 gains `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidationKeepsLoadScope`, a unit test asserting `ErrNotFound`, a `visibility.denied` event, and no `artifact.loaded` event for an out-of-scope identity and a version-pin mismatch, and `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidationOutsideScopeIsDenied`, an integration test asserting that a HEAD and a matching conditional GET from such a token answer as the full GET does, never 304, with no content-hash, version, or ETag header. Both cite §13.4 and §6.3.1, and the "When it does not fire" bullet names them.
- **Correction: the `revalidationRedactKeys` bullet claimed a bypassed read returns a chain error as a full load does.** After this pass moved the full load onto `admitChain`, the bypass still walks through `resolveExtendsChain` unadmitted (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1701-1720`), so a row whose ancestor carries an undeclared pin toward a missing row or back at the child answers `registry.not_found` or `registry.invalid_argument` on a bypassed HEAD or 304 (`pkg/registry/server/server.go:1400`, `:1404`) where the full load answers `materialize.content_hash_mismatch`, and today's message names the parent pin (`core.go:1714`). CODE-14's second IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE now keeps the walk's sentinel, rebuilds the error from the sentinel and the requested ID as `admitChain` does, and states that the bypass sentinel can differ from the full load's, consistent with SPEC-10 and OQ-11. TEST-14's third `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidatedReadEventRedactsKeys` arm now asserts a message naming only the requested ID and no longer implies sentinel parity beyond the agreeing-pin case.

### Pass 24 (2026-09-23, automated)

- **`docs/consuming/browsing-the-catalog.md:147` stated the size-only delivery rule and no finder returned it.** The `load_artifact` section's "inline at or below the 256 KB cutoff and through a presigned URL above it" carries no axis-A token and matched no B7 arm, so S17's re-run could not return it, while SPEC-10's §4.1 and §7.2 edits falsify it for a resource the registry holds inline. B7's pattern gains the arm `presigned URL above it`, B7's hit list names the line among the pages DOC-1 stages, and DOC-1 gains a bullet giving it the exception `custom-via-sdk.md:96` gets, linked to the `handling-artifact-responses.md:51` paragraph.
- **The scope-bypass tests asserted no `artifact.loaded` event for a denied load.** `LoadArtifact` emits its `artifact.loaded` event in a deferred function on every call, a not-found or denied call included, at `ResultSize` 0 (`pkg/registry/core/core.go:1463-1480`), so a correct implementation failed both tests and the clause did not separate the two orders. `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidationKeepsLoadScope` and `TestLoadArtifact_RevalidationOutsideScopeIsDenied` now assert that the denied call's `artifact.loaded` event carries `ResultSize` 0 and no `content_hash` key in its `Context`, which `assemble` sets only after `assembleResult` succeeds (`:1530-1541`), beside the existing `ErrNotFound`, status, header, and `visibility.denied` assertions. The Pass 23 entry above records the earlier wording.

DOC-1 now stages a new `### Signing failures` subsection appended to `## Common operational pitfalls` (`:179-189`), states that every signing failure bullet it names lands there, and replaces the nonexistent bullet with a bullet for the `config.signature_provider_unavailable` startup refusal and its missing-key cause. The shared-passages row in the Relationship to proposal 0027 table names the same subsection.

DOC-1's migration bullet now states that the target refuses every such row with `materialize.signature_invalid` to every reader and under any consumer policy while the other key is in place, and that placing the source's key at the target's `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` and restarting the target, which loads its key only at start (`internal/serverboot/serverboot.go:903`), repairs those rows with no new version.

## Open questions

**OQ-1. Does the consumer-facing default flip ship in the same release as the producer default?** The checklist orders S12 before S14 and this document assumes one release, because splitting them across releases leaves an interval in which the store is signed and no consumer checks, which is the status quo, and because a default flipped later signs no stored row while 0026's rewrite completion record is present (SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph). A reviewer who wants the consumer flip held to the following MINOR gets a safer rollout and a second coordinated upgrade for operators. S14 is severable without touching S10 through S12.

**OQ-2. Is the rotation limitation acceptable as documented behavior?** §4.7.9 currently claims quarterly rotation and SPEC-1 replaces that claim with a statement of the behavior: a rotation invalidates every prior envelope and an artifact verifies again only once a new version of it is ingested, because 0026's first-start rewrite leaves untouched a row whose envelope does not verify under the configured key, on its first run and on any rerun SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph describes. The alternatives are a verification key set, which is a provider redesign, and a re-triggerable signing pass that trusts a retired key, which is a key set by another name. Neither is staged here.

**OQ-3. Should the standalone bootstrap hand the consumer a pointer to key material?** It is resolved, and the answer is no. The consumer resolves the registry's key file by the same `sign.KeyFilePath` the registry resolves, so the handover is the file's `0600` mode under one user account rather than a configuration value. Nothing in the tree writes a `.pub`, and no `sync.yaml` key is added, so there is no second place for the path to be stated, no insertion into an operator's file, and no scope-resolved key path a checked-in `.podium/sync.yaml` could redirect. A deployment where the registry is not the same user on the same filesystem supplies `PODIUM_SIGNATURE_VERIFY_KEY`, which every deployment other than standalone already does.

**OQ-4. Which release carries the follow-up Sigstore proposal?** It is latent today, and it stops being latent the moment a registry signing mode mints a keyless envelope. The two should be scheduled together.

**OQ-5. Should a later proposal add a re-triggerable signing pass?** 0026's `RehashManifest` can write a fresh envelope onto a stored row, and only 0026's marker-gated first-start rewrite drives it. A registry that enables signing after that start has no in-place repair: each artifact gains an envelope only when a new version of it is ingested, and every earlier version stays unsigned. A pass an operator can trigger again would remove that, at the cost of a trigger surface and of the marker semantics 0026 declined.

**OQ-6. How does the Helm chart hold the signing key under the new default?** It is resolved: the chart mounts an operator-supplied Secret at `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH` and refuses to render without one unless `signing.mode` is `none` (CODE-8). A chart default of `none` was rejected because it would leave clustered deployments, which most need verification, unsigned by default.

**OQ-7. Should a prefetched bucket be made to pass the §6.6 step-2 content-hash check, or be left unwarmed?** It is resolved and moved. Proposal 0027 removes the §7.6.2 prefetch warm-up and replaces §7.6.2's warm-up sentence with a rule that the MCP server does not prefetch (0027's OQ-8, decided 2026-09-23). Between this proposal and 0027 the prefetch behaves as today, which the edge-case table records.

**OQ-8. Should `medium-and-above` be deleted rather than kept as a rollout posture?** It is resolved, and the value is deleted. The policy takes `never` or `always`, and a consumer configured with the old value refuses to start (CODE-13).

**OQ-9. Should the registry refuse to generate a signing key where its home may not persist?** It is resolved, and the answer is yes. A start with signing on, no `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`, and a store other than the SQLite store in the default key's directory or the memory store is refused before any key is generated (CODE-4).

**OQ-10. Does 0027's serve-time attestation change what 0028 requires of stored signatures?** It is resolved by the owner's decisions of 2026-09-23 (Resolved in adversarial review): the registry's stored-row admission verifies stored signatures (SPEC-10), so each passage the 2026-09-22 assessment questioned stands.

**OQ-11. Should revalidation stay outside admission?** Resolved by the owner on 2026-09-23: yes, as OD-1 of the Redesign 1 dated 2026-09-23 recorded. A HEAD request or a matching conditional GET is answered without admission, and no verdict is kept. A consumer that fetched a row while it was admissible therefore keeps serving its copy after the row becomes inadmissible, for as long as the stored hash is unchanged. The alternative admits revalidation behind a bounded verdict cache, which adds a tuning variable, a staleness rule, and a divergence between replicas. The owner confirms the default.

**OQ-12. Does proposal 0027 own `CHANGELOG.md`'s "Rolling the consumers" and "Clear each consumer's cache" paragraphs?** Resolved by the owner on 2026-09-23: yes, as OD-2 of the Redesign 1 dated 2026-09-23 recorded, which depends on 0027's DOC-1 carrying this proposal's consumer-side outcomes into them. The alternative has this proposal restate both paragraphs and 0027 edit them as this proposal leaves them. The owner confirms the default.

**OQ-13. Is the selection limit acceptable?** Resolved by the owner on 2026-09-23: yes, for this release; signing the row's identity and layer is a follow-up. The stored signature attests the content hash alone, so a party that can write the metadata store without the signing key can withhold, relabel, re-layer, or roll back an admissible row, and admission does not detect it (SPEC-10; CODE-14's column table). A consumer under `always` accepts the same rows today. Comparing the stored version with the version the admitted manifest declares would refuse a relabel within one artifact, at the cost of one comparison, and would leave a transplant across artifacts and a rollback by tombstone open, because the manifest carries no artifact ID. Closing the limit requires an envelope over the row's identity and layer, which changes the §4.7.9 signed value and is not staged here. The owner confirms the default.

**OQ-14. Should the first-start rewrite's completion record be protected from a party that can write the metadata store?** Resolved by the owner on 2026-09-23: no, for this release. SPEC-10 states the limit, the operator guide documents it (DOC-1), and evidence of completion held outside the metadata store, such as an operator-set authorization to sign existing rows, is a follow-up. The record lives in the `data_migrations` table of the same store the rewrite signs (`pkg/store/sqlite.go:161`, `pkg/store/postgres.go:185`), and the rewrite runs whenever it is absent (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:127`), so such a party that deletes it and waits for a restart has the registry sign unsigned rows it wrote, repeatably. A store-resident record cannot close this, because a signed record detects a forged one and not a deleted one, and a heuristic over the store, such as refusing to mint when any row already carries a framed envelope that verifies, is defeated by clearing every envelope first. Any closing design must satisfy these constraints: the evidence that the rewrite completed lives outside the metadata store or is unforgeable and undeletable by a party without the signing key; the first start of this release still signs every unsigned row of a store that was never rewritten, which is how an upgraded store gains envelopes; and `podium admin migrate-to-standard`'s documented re-run on an empty target still makes the target's next start rewrite and re-sign the copied rows. The owner confirms the default or chooses a design.

## Relationship to proposal 0026

0026 length-frames the §4.7.6 content hash, which moves every stored digest and therefore invalidates every stored envelope, since each envelope is over the digest string. Its migration is a rewrite the registry performs on the first start of the new version, before it ingests or serves. The pass recomputes each stored row from the bytes the registry holds and, where a signer is configured, re-signs every row it rewrites and mints a first envelope for a stored row already at the framed digest that carries none. It leaves untouched a row whose stored envelope does not verify under the configured key, a row whose stored bytes reproduce neither digest, and a row whose bundled-resource bytes it could not read, and an untouched row keeps whatever envelope it had. It refuses the start, writing nothing, when a row it would rewrite is signed and no signer is configured, or when signing is on, the key file is absent, the rewrite has not completed, and a stored row carries a signature (`internal/serverboot/rehash.go:506-545`). SPEC-10's §13.4 paragraph and OQ-14 state when the pass runs again, and a rerun classifies a row signed under a retired key `signature_unverified` and does not re-sign it. This proposal ships in the same release because that start is the one event that signs stored rows: with the producer default flipped beside it every row the pass writes leaves the upgrade signed, CODE-14's class table states what admission answers for a row the pass leaves untouched, and a default flipped later signs no stored row while the completion record is present. 0026's mandated consumer cache clearing is also what removes the stale envelopes a warmed cache would otherwise replay through the stricter gate. 0026's OQ-1 is resolved in favour of the first-start rewrite.

0026 is implemented. CODE-14's admission takes the same signer, object store, and read deadline the pass takes, and reuses the pass's deadline-bounded object read. The pass uses the boot path's signer, and the signing-mode test is the single function `registrySigningEnabled`, so CODE-4's change reaches the loader, the pass, and both start refusals with no second edit; the no-signer refusal is keyed on a nil provider, which `none` still produces. CODE-4 and TEST-4 own what remains: the fixtures in 0026's tests that read an unset `PODIUM_SIGN` as no signer, the no-signer refusal's error wording, and the test for the generated-key refusal under the new default. `podium admin migrate-to-standard` copies stored signatures unchanged and clears the target's rewrite record, so under this proposal's default the target registry's first start after the migration needs the source's signing key at the target's `PODIUM_SIGN_KEY_PATH`. A target with no key file there is refused, and a target holding any other key file, including one an earlier start generated or a chart-mounted Secret created from a different key, starts and classifies every copied signed row `signature_unverified` without touching it. A row the source had already framed keeps its framed digest and the source's envelope and is repaired by placing the source's key at the target, and only a row a never-upgraded source still held at the pre-framing hash waits for a new version; the edge-case table and DOC-1 state these outcomes. CODE-4 also records that 0026's accepted-failure text for a `signature_unverified` row (`CHANGELOG.md:76-77`) is falsified by CODE-1 and CODE-14 rather than by the reorder: on a signing registry, admission refuses such a row under every consumer policy, with the code CODE-14's class table gives. DOC-1 stages the replacement of the class-to-code paragraph that sentence closes (`CHANGELOG.md:68-77`), because admission also decides the code every other untouched row that fails a §13.4 check reports, whatever the consumer's policy (CODE-14's class table). A registry that turns signing on after its marker is set is this proposal's case; SPEC-5's §13.12 text states that such a registry signs no stored row and that an artifact gains an envelope only at a new version, and OQ-5 carries a re-triggerable signing pass.

`test/e2e/signed_artifact_helpers_test.go` already carries 0026's `version.CanonicalContentHash` call (`test/e2e/signed_artifact_helpers_test.go:126`), so no 0026 edit to it remains. TEST-7 adds `StripSignature()` beside the existing tamper helpers, which 0027 re-expresses over its delivery record, and adds no sensitivity-tampering helper, because after CODE-13 no check reads `sensitivity`.

## Relationship to proposal 0027

This proposal lands before proposal 0027, by the owner's decision of 2026-09-23, and all three proposals, 0026 included, ship in one release. It is written against the tree 0026 left and depends on no 0027 surface. Proposal 0027 owns served-record verification: it adds the §4.7.10 delivery attestation, drops `signature`, `raw_frontmatter`, and `manifest_merged` from the `load_artifact` response, replaces the consumer's §4.7.6 recomputation with a check of the delivery record, and removes the §7.6.2 prefetch. 0027 countersigns only rows this proposal's admission admitted, on every path it countersigns.

0027 retargets these surfaces of this proposal from the served `signature` to its `delivery_signature`, in the commit that removes the served field:

- `verifyServedArtifact` (CODE-12): `verifyContentHash` inside it becomes 0027's delivery-record check, and `enforceSignaturePolicy` is called with `(delivery_hash, delivery_signature)`. The order CODE-3 sets, verification ahead of the §8.2 read event and the §4.4.1 gates, is kept.
- SPEC-1's §4.7.9 text: the paragraph naming the served `signature` as the governed signature, and the policy paragraph's exception for bodies the registry serves from admitted rows, which 0027's delivery record covers.
- TEST-2's verification-order and mirror cases and `mcp_audit_trace_test.go`'s stub, which serve a record that passes the content-hash recomputation, and TEST-7's tamper helpers and `StripSignature`, which act on the served `signature`.

**Moved to 0027.** The 2026-09-22 scope cut deleted these from this proposal, each because 0027 replaces the surface it was written against, and they stay in 0027:

- SPEC-2, the replacement of §6.6 step-2 item 2. 0027's SPEC-4 replaces the same item. The constraint SPEC-2 carried that 0027's text does not state is in SPEC-1's policy paragraph.
- SPEC-9 and CODE-9: `signature` on the §7.6.2 batch envelope, and the prefetch's recomputation and skip. 0027 removes the prefetch.

The mirror's bundled-resource case stays here as TEST-2's `TestResources_ReadVerifiesOverBundledResources`, because on this proposal's tree the mirror's recomputation hashes the resources it fetches.

**Moved from 0027.** Stored-row admission (0027's former SPEC-10, CODE-17, TEST-22, its Decision "Admit the requested row at the registry", and its OQ-6 and OQ-7) is this proposal's SPEC-10, CODE-14, and TEST-14, in the form the owner chose on 2026-09-23: object-held bodies are read, the stored signature is verified where a signer is configured, the fields CODE-14's column table marks derived come from the admitted bytes, and every row of an `extends:` chain is admitted.

**Shared passages.** Each passage both proposals would otherwise edit has one owner, the proposal whose change decides its shipped text. The owner writes the passage whole. The other proposal stages no edit to it, except the clause the table names, which it applies to the owner's landed text without restating the passage. This proposal writes every passage it owns in terms that hold on 0027's tree: it names the signature and content hash a consumer verifies and does not name the served `signature` field.

**Revalidation on 0027's tree.** CODE-14's `Revalidate` bypass returns the unadmitted result without calling `assembleResult`, which is where 0027's CODE-5 sets `Extends`, so 0027 carries three obligations that keep the bypass's validator equal to the caller's full-GET validator. 0027's CODE-3 computes the `Revalidate` predicate and the ETag branch from `loadArtifactETag(contentHash, res.Extends)` over the unadmitted result. 0027's CODE-5 sets `Extends` on that unadmitted result, from `rec.ExtendsPin` and the same visible set, before `assemble` calls `Revalidate`, and on the result the bypass returns. 0027's TEST-4 reverse-order arm gains the assertion that a HEAD's ETag for an identity equals that identity's full-GET ETag.

**Revalidation ordering in 0027's text.** A HEAD and a matching conditional GET are answered from the resolved row without this proposal's admission and read no object storage, and this proposal keeps no verdict. 0027's "Mint at serve time" Decision and its object-store outage edge row state that ordering and name no remembered verdict.

**The chain walk in 0027's text.** 0027's CODE-6 names `assembleResult` as a caller of `resolveExtendsChain` and threads the requested ID through that walk so no chain error names an ancestor. From CODE-14 on, `assembleResult` walks the chain through `admitChain`, whose every error is built from its sentinel and the requested ID alone (CODE-14's callers bullet), so 0027's CODE-6 drops its `assembleResult` call-site sentence and applies to `resolveExtendsChain`'s remaining callers, `revalidationRedactKeys` and 0027's CODE-8 search-descriptor strip. 0027's TEST-6 load cases keep their assertions, and its fixtures are sealed with `storetest.Seal` and declare the `extends:` references their pins name, because admission otherwise refuses a row whose pin its bytes do not declare with `materialize.content_hash_mismatch` before the walk reaches the missing ancestor or the cycle.

**Batch resource bytes in 0027's text.** 0027's Decision "Remove the §7.6.2 prefetch warm-up rather than make it verify" states that the batch envelope carries no resource bytes (`pkg/registry/server/batch_load.go:30-55`). That is already false in the standalone-without-storage mode, and from CODE-14 on a batch item carries every resource the row holds inline in the reference's `inline` field. 0027 therefore restates that clause as "no `sensitivity` and no bytes for a resource held in object storage". The Decision's conclusion does not change, because a large resource still travels by URL and the envelope still carries no `sensitivity`. 0027's SPEC-5 edits the §7.6.2 wire example and the prefetch sentence, and SPEC-10 edits the Bandwidth bullet between them.

S17 records the disposition for a finder hit inside a passage the table gives 0027. 0027's staged text that restates an admission outcome in a passage this proposal owns is removed from 0027, including its runbook sub-bullet naming a row "whose verdict the serving process remembers", because this proposal keeps no verdict. 0027's S13 closes by confirming that its docs commit leaves `deploy/runbook.md` unchanged and changes `error-codes.md` and `operator-guide.md` only in the clauses its rows name.

## Non-goals

- **Generating the signing key inside the cluster.** A pre-install hook Job that mints the key and writes the Secret would remove one operator step, and it would need cluster RBAC to create Secrets and would mint a fresh key on a reinstall over a store whose rows an earlier key signed. The operator supplies the Secret.
- **Sigstore verification correctness.** The certificate-identity policy, verification at the transparency log's attested time rather than at wall-clock time, entry binding, inclusion-proof and signed-entry-timestamp verification, refusing an envelope that declares `log_index: -1`, refusing a digest algorithm other than `sha256`, and a versioned self-describing envelope are a separate proposal. No registry signing mode mints a Sigstore envelope into the store, so every one of those defects is latent for served artifacts, although `podium sign --provider sigstore-keyless` can already produce such an envelope outside the ingest flow, and the fix carries a dependency decision about adopting an upstream Sigstore library that must be taken on its own terms. What lands here is the §4.7.9 text that stops describing the model as working.
- **A registry key-publication route with a first-use pin.** It would make `always` the default with no operator action on a non-standalone deployment. It is also a trust-on-first-use mechanism with its own security semantics, its own `sync.yaml` state, and its own re-pinning flow, and adding it here would land it unreviewed inside a rollout proposal.
- **A verification key set and non-destructive rotation.** See OQ-2.
- **A re-triggerable pass that signs already-stored rows.** 0026's `RehashManifest` writes a fresh envelope onto a stored row, and only 0026's marker-gated first-start rewrite drives it. This proposal adds no second driver, no second store method, and no trigger. The same-release constraint makes one unnecessary for the upgrade, and OQ-5 carries the question for a registry that enables signing later.
- **Signature verification, or a fetched-body hash check, in `podium sync` or the SDKs.** `EnforceVerification` has one production caller, and that bound is unchanged. Neither consumer compares a body fetched from object storage with its hash, and no item here adds that comparison; CODE-14's bullet on the paths that serve no admitted content states the gap. DOC-1's operator-guide tamper check states that the registry's §13.4 admission applies to `podium sync` and the SDKs, and the sensitivity-enforcement row states that neither runs a client-side signature check.
- **A grace policy value, a legacy provider alias, or an exemption for artifacts ingested before the upgrade.** Podium is pre-1.0 and each is a second behavior retained beside the first, which `.claude/rules/code-best-practices.md` forbids.
- **Rescoping `bootstrapStandaloneFiles` away from the explicit-server-config branch.** Deleting the relaxation line removes the leak this proposal cares about, and the bootstrap's other writes are benign.

## S9.5 assignment record

This section is the working record AUDIT-1 requires. S10 through S17 apply the share assigned to them here. The finders ran on 2026-09-23 against commit `add7e2d`, so every line number below is that tree's and shifts as the earlier steps land; each applier re-reads the named passage rather than trusting the number. A step whose share is listed as empty records that outcome. A file under `spec/` is a confirmation of the applied spec steps, and no spec residue was found, so no spec commit precedes S10.

### Finder results

### Bridge audit

The finders: `grep -ln 'mcpExec(' test/e2e/*_test.go` (34 files), `grep -l 'buildMCP(' test/integration/*.go` (14 files, dispositioned under S14), the in-process `loadConfig()` callers (`cmd/podium-mcp/config_env_test.go`, 15 calls, and `cmd/podium-mcp/main_helpers_test.go`, 4 calls, both S14 under TEST-5), and `grep -n '&config{' cmd/podium-mcp/*_test.go`. On the last, the constructions that set a policy above `never` and deliver a signature are `cache_mode_test.go:213` alone, and the S14 applier re-derives the set after S11 adds TEST-2's constructions.

An e2e spawn whose environment comes from `mcpServerEnv` or `chMCPEnv` takes the blanket S12 edit and is not listed. The remaining spawns, by TEST-7's predicates:
